# Render system

`src/render/`: from `doc.html` (`report.html` in an older document) to the page a reader opens. The shared kit (`kit/`, the CSS and scripts every document page loads, served as `/kit/*`), the starter a new document is made from (`starter/`), and the Node side: the shared assets, listed once (`assets.mjs`: their tags, which a document uses, how each kit file is made), loading and checking documents (`reports.mjs`, `typecheck.mjs`, `src/render/chart-declarations.mjs`), what each `/kit/` file holds, made from the kit's sources (`kit-build.mjs`: a source as it is, or an esbuild bundle), and building previews and offline exports (`pipeline.mjs`). The overview of all systems is [internals.md](internals.md).

## The kit

What a document loads from `/kit/` (its tags the render puts in, in their order, "Kit tags": a document writes none) is made from the sources in `src/render/kit/` by `kit-build.mjs`, as the assets' list says (`assets.mjs`): a file as it is, an esbuild bundle of a folder (one self-contained IIFE, "use strict"), or the stylesheets an index lists, joined (the nine colors' class lists asked for as `@text-colors` and `@highlight-colors`, and the three dots of a folded code line as `@fold-dots`, which `src/shared/code-folds.mjs` writes once for the kit's `code.css` and the editor's open code alike, the TeX logo and √x of a formula not read as `@tex-mark` and `@formula-mark` (`src/shared/math-marks.mjs`, the editor's bars and chips too); the kit's words a stylesheet shows as `@kit-words;`: "The kit's words"). Writing `public/kit`, inlining an export and the tests all take a kit file from `kit-build.mjs`; the live preview rebuilds them when any source under `kit/` changes.

| `/kit/` file | Made from | Parts |
|---|---|---|
| `report.css` | `styles/`, joined in the order of `styles/index.css` | `tokens` (values), `base` (body, first paint, links, inline code, text colors and highlights: nine of each, light and dark), `page` (reading column, header, the page's icon: 56px above the title), `controls`, `sheets`, `state`, `math`, `mention` (a link shown with its icon), `code`, `highlight`, `flow` (body text in every flow, small text), `todo`, `flowchart` (流程图: a list's steps as cards with arrows between them), `section`, `sizes` (sizes a block may carry), `columns` (rows of columns), `figure`, `media` (what the four media blocks share), `image`, `file`, `bookmark`, `embed`, `chart`, `dialog`, `zoom` (a picture opened large: "Opening a picture large"), `scrollbar` (every scrollbar the kit draws: "Scrollbars the kit draws"), `table`, `callout`, `details` |
| `tables.js` | `tables/` (bundled) | `index.js` (DocTables, watching the page), `cells.js` (rows, values, numeric columns; what a number or a missing value is, is `src/shared/number-reading.mjs`'s, which the page editor's 一键优化 reads cells by too), `layout.js` with `auto-column-widths.ts` (column widths), `scrollbars.js` (a frame's two rails, each the kit's one scrollbar, `scrollbar.js`, bundled into the database's drawing too, as its thumbs' giving way past an end, `scroll-squeeze.js`, is; what a frame hides is `src/shared/table-hides.mjs`'s, which `gallery qa` measures with too), `sticky.js` (which cell is the column that stays: `fixedFirstCell`), `sort.js` |
| `charts.js` | `charts/` (bundled) | `index.js` (DocCharts: `define`, `get`; the chart component), `declared.js` (charts declared in their cards, of the types the document's script defines: a drawing or a part), `from-table.js` (a chart drawn from a table of the page: "Charts from a table"), `core.js` (what every host is handled with: the target a script names, emptying it before a drawing, its layout notes) |
| `runtime.js` | `runtime/` (bundled with `src/shared/components/`) | `kernel.js` (DocKit: instances, signals, the frame, failures, an export's cleanup; see "Runtime"), `bindings.js` (state bindings: "State bindings"), `reveal.js` (going to a block: "Links to blocks"), `embeds.js` (a PDF's frame shown through a blob: address), `flowchart.js` (a flowchart's rows, widths and arrows, as its directions say and it measures, in a stylesheet the document adopts), `spacing.js` (a space between inline code or a formula and a word right beside it, where the text has none), `zoom.js` (a picture clicked opens large: "Opening a picture large"; which pictures is `src/shared/zoom.mjs`'s), `choice.js` (every `<select>` opens the kit's menu card, never the browser's popup, and `DocKit.choose`: "A choice is the kit's menu"), `tip.js` (the kit's tooltip for `data-doc-tip`: "A tooltip is the kit's"), `index.js` |
| `code.js` | `code.js` (bundled with Shiki, minified), after Shiki's grammars, a line each (`kit-build.mjs` `grammarLines`: those `src/shared/code-grammars.mjs` lists, each checked to embed what the list says) | the grammars (`globalThis.DocCodeGrammars`), then the highlighter that reads them: an export keeps the lines its code uses ("Export") |
| `ui.js` | `ui.js` (bundled with `src/shared/export-wrap.mjs` and `src/shared/page-address.mjs`) | the display settings (in an offline file first its words copied, 复制为完整文本 and 复制为 Markdown, of what the file carries: `script[data-doc-copy]`) and the export of the current view; the policy and the UMD isolation of that copy are the CLI export's (`EXPORT_CSP`, `isolateUmd`), written once; on a custom page the theme alone ("A custom page") |
| `board.css`, `board.js` | `board/` (`index.css` joined, `index.js` bundled) | a custom page's own: its margins, the boards' grid of square cells and the cards' frame (a bare card's none), and the script that places each card at its cells (one of fit content as high as its content, those under it drawn pushed down), says each board's columns and cell and draws a drag's draft ("A custom page"); no document loads them; the cards' editing is the editor's (`src/editor/page/board/`) |
| `database.css`, `database.js` | `database/` (`styles/index.css` joined, `index.js` bundled, minified) | the 多维表格's drawing ("Databases" below), the kit's own modules with no framework: `index.js` (the component), `store.js` (the database, the reader's state, the writes), `app.js` (its head, filter bar, body and layer), `views.js` (the layouts, "Databases: views"), `table.js`, `peek.js` (a record's details), `filters.js`, `settings.js`, `props.js` (a column's menus and editors), `editors.js` (a value edited where it stands), `data/` (reading the file, values, filters and sorts, the formula language), `ui/` (elements, cards, icons, colors, reordering, scrolling at the edges of a drag, values), `words.js` and `words-en.js`; loaded by a page that holds one |
| `math.js` | `math-bundle.js` (bundled: `math.js` with KaTeX's mhchem, whose `katex` is the page's, `page-katex.js`) | A formula KaTeX could not read (its error, `.katex-error`; a formula drawn with a command it does not know in red that a strict reading refuses too, the editor's test; a formula of an element of its own, `.doc-math-inline` or `.doc-math-block`, that KaTeX left as written, finding no end to it: `\(\frac{\)`) is marked `.doc-math-invalid` (KaTeX's error span, its TeX kept in it unseen as `.doc-math-tex`, KaTeX's reason its title, which `gallery qa`'s formula-error quotes) and shown as the page editor shows it, in the editor's values (`styles/math.css`): in the text a gray chip, √x and 无效的公式; a formula block a gray bar as wide as the page, the TeX logo and 无效的公式, the gap of a media block around it; the marks are `src/shared/math-marks.mjs`'s (`@tex-mark`, `@formula-mark`), the words the kit's (`math-invalid`). |
| `dialogs.js`, `sheets.js`, `state.js`, `tokens.js` | the file of that name | |

A component's look is its style file, named as its folder in `src/editor/components/` (how the preview editor treats it) where there is one: `table.css`, `code.css`, `callout.css`, `highlight.css`, `details.css`, `dialog.css`, `sheets.css`, `math.css`, `columns.css` and `figure.css` (with `chart.css`); paragraphs, headings, lists, quotes and dividers are `flow.css`. In `styles/`, the order matters only where rules of the same weight reach the same element; `index.css` lists those places, and the rules for narrow widths and print stand at the end of each file. A new stylesheet must be listed there (the build refuses one that is not).

### Names

The format's names are one family: classes, keyframes, events and meta names `doc-*`, attributes `data-doc-*` (and their `dataset.doc*`), custom properties `--doc-*`, the runtime's globals and the SDK's types `Doc*` (`DocKit`, `DocCharts` …, `skills/gallery/sdk/doc-*.d.ts`), the font alias `Doc Segoe`. Until 2026-10-26 they were `report-*`, `data-report-*`, `--report-*` and `Report*`; what the product is named after (Gallery, before 2026-10-26 HTML Report Studio: the `gallery:*` storage keys and `x-gallery-*` clipboard types, with the old `html-report-studio:*`, `report-studio:*` and `x-report-studio-*` still read) and `/__gallery/edit/`, the file layout (`report.html`, `reports/`, `gallery new`), the editor's `ge-*` and the workbench's own classes (`report-frame`, `report-view`, `report-state`) kept theirs. The old names still work: `src/shared/old-names.mjs` holds them (a closed set, the kit's of that day, `OLD_NAMES` and `OLD_GLOBALS`) and the rules that make them new (a class list's, a stylesheet's, a script's, and a DOM's for a paste: `newDomNames`), and `src/shared/new-names.mjs` `newNames()` makes a file's text new where they stand: attribute names, classes, a meta's name, custom properties in any attribute, on* code and `javascript:` addresses, and the text of the document's own `<style>` and `<script>` (a whole old name wherever it stands there, their comments too, but never a path or a file's name: `assets/report-chart.png` stays; a JSON script, a comment of the page, a code sample and every text a reader reads stay as written; the kit's tags and an export's inline copies of them keep their text), lines kept, a second pass changing nothing. An id the document spelled as an old name (`id="report-summary"`) is renamed with all that names it, so they still meet: its selectors and script strings, `for`, `headers`, `aria-labelledby` and the other attributes of ids, and a `#…` an attribute points to it by (`href="#report-summary"`, `data-doc-dialog-open`, `url(#…)`; `newAttributeValue`); a fragment of another file stays. Every reader of a document's text past the places that take it in reads the new names only: `naturalize()` runs it first (so the preview before showing a file and at its first edit, `gallery check`, `build`, `export` and `ids`, and `gallery import` see the new names, silently: no change line says it, and a file takes them the next time a tool writes it), as do `loadReports()`'s `written`, the import of an export (`import.mjs`), a document copied or moved to another slug (`workbench/service.mjs` reslug) or renamed from the workbench (the edit service's `renameReport`), `gallery qa`'s reading of `report.html` and the paste of another program's HTML (`parseClipboardHTML`). An old `report-*` class of none of the old set (a typo) is left as written, and the checks take it in the kit's namespace (`kitNamespace`: "no class of the kit's (doc-…?)"), never as a class of the document's own. The kit answers to the old globals as well (`window.ReportCharts` is `DocCharts` …) and tells `report-theme-change` beside `doc-theme-change`, for a script of the document's folder no tool rewrites; an export made before keeps its own kit, and `gallery qa` asks for a new export of one (its `report-export-source` is no `doc-export-source`).

To see what a change of the kit does to the pages, `node tests/browser/kit-diff.mjs report.css=<earlier file> …` compares every element's attributes and computed style with an earlier version of kit files (see [testing.md](testing.md)); reorganizing the kit must leave it unchanged.

### The kit's words

What the kit's own chrome says to a reader (a button's or a control's label and tooltip, a sortable table's header and status line, the zoom viewer's, the display settings, an empty code block's title 代码块, an excerpt's 第 120–139 行, a chart's line of why it drew nothing) is the kit's table `src/render/kit/words.js`, in Chinese and English, never `t()` (the kit runs in exported files, where Gallery's UI is not): one part for each bundle that speaks (runtime, code, ui, tables, charts; the lists' and the math's parts are the stylesheet's alone), so a bundle carries its own words alone; a script says one with its part's `say` (`sayCode("copy-code")`). A document's own words are never translated. Which language a page reads them in (`kitLanguage`): shown by Gallery (the page edited, previewed, presented in 演示视图, the 写作偏好 dialog's), the UI's, which the page's first script puts on its root as `data-ge-language` before the kit draws anything (`languageScript` of `src/shared/i18n/`, written in the head by the preview server, as the UI's modules read it: the stored choice, else the browser's language); in a file of its own, the page's `lang` (English for `en` or `en-…`, Chinese otherwise). An export of the view takes every `data-ge-*` attribute off (`ui.js`), so the UI's never travels; a document the workbench makes while its UI is English is `lang="en"` (the starter's `zh-CN` otherwise), so its export reads English. A stylesheet shows a word through a property of the root, each named in `STYLE_WORDS` (an empty code block's title reads `var(--doc-word-code-title)`; a to-do item's box says 未完成 or 已完成 to a screen reader as its alt text, `content: "" / var(--doc-word-todo-open)`; a formula KaTeX could not read says 无效的公式, `var(--doc-word-math-invalid)`): `kit-build.mjs` writes `@kit-words;` (in `tokens.css`) as the root's properties from the table, Chinese, and English under the same rule (`:root[data-ge-language="en"]`, or no `data-ge-language` and `lang|="en"`). The reasons a chart drawn from a table draws nothing (its table gone, merged cells) are keys of the charts' part (`message: [key, ...values]`, `src/shared/table-chart.mjs` and `charts/from-table.js`): the charts' part holds those alone, as a chart's own words are the document's. `tests/unit/kit-words.mjs` holds every word to both languages and the same `{0}`, `{1}`…, every word said to its bundle's part, none unsaid, and the language each kind of page reads; scenario `kitWords` the pages themselves.

## Runtime

`/kit/runtime.js` (`kit/runtime/`, an esbuild bundle carrying what the kernel reads of the components' descriptions in `src/shared/components/`, their `match` and `runtime` (`runtimeDescriptions()`, put in by the build as `__COMPONENT_RUNTIME__`, read from the description files as they are when the build runs (`component-runtime.mjs`), so a rebuild under the live preview carries an edited description; `tests/unit/runtime.mjs` checks the list against the kernel), not the whole description) defines `DocKit`, the kit's runtime, and loads first in every document; every other kit script registers its component with it. A component says what it does, `DocKit.component({ id, mount(host, context), on, when, added, owns, dehydrate })`; where its instances are (`match`) and what it leaves on the page (`runtime`) are its description's (docs/internals.md, "Components"). `mount` returns the instance's handle (`update(reason, detail)`, `destroy()`, and the `api` a document's script sees), or null for a host it leaves alone or starts once without keeping anything (a code block, the formulas). The kernel (`runtime/kernel.js`):

- **Finds instances** when the component registers (`when: "now"`: charts) or once the page has loaded (the default, after the document's own script: Sheet areas, detail cards, tables, code blocks, formulas), and those added later (an undo puts a card back), except for components that start only with the page (`added: false`: code blocks and formulas, which the editor renders itself later).
- **Stops at the own region of an instance of a custom block** (`data-doc-component` on a `div` or a `section`, `src/shared/components/installed.mjs`): no other component starts on one or in its own region, whatever its selector finds there, and neither the formulas (`math.js`), the spacing (`runtime/spacing.js`) nor a picture's viewer (`zoomable`) go in. Its slots (`data-doc-slot`, a part its manifest edits as `flow`) hold the document's blocks, where all of that goes on as anywhere on the page, another instance among them: whose an element is goes by one rule (`heldBy`, `componentAt`: the regions folded from the outermost in, an instance's own slot opening the document's again), which the kernel's `inOther`, the fallback's inert and the formulas' targets (a slot the outermost of its layer) read; a change in a slot is no change of what its component owns (`ownedHere`), so what is added there starts, and goes to the component as `source`; its `runtime` marks and generated nodes are of its own region alone (`takeRuntime`, `dehydrate`), and the fallback's look stops at a slot (`@scope ([data-doc-fallback]) to ([data-doc-slot])`, `styles/installed.css`), a slot's blocks flush with its edges. A page's references are no such step and go in as anywhere (`renderReportHtml`: a picture named in one has to reach an export, and so does what `embedIcon` draws for a `.doc-icon`). What the kit knows of a custom block it is told: `DocKit.describe({ id, match, parts, runtime })`, a line the render writes for each custom block a page has instances of and a preview for the catalog's others ("A custom block on a page"), the same description the scan and the checks read (`readCatalog()`, `src/shared/component-catalog.mjs`). A component then registers by its id, `DocKit.component({ id: "local/board", on: ["source"], mount })`, and starts on its instances, not needing a `selector`.
- **Shows an instance of no custom block as it stands.** Once the page has loaded, and as one is added, an instance whose custom block the kit was not told of, or was told does not load, and that no component registered for gets `data-doc-fallback`, for the look of `styles/installed.css`, the kit's alone, as a disabled or missing custom block loads no CSS: what reads by itself (a title, paragraphs, lists, a table) reads, and what is wider than the page scrolls in it. What a script of data in it holds (`isDataScript`, none in an instance it holds), which no reader would see, the kit draws right after the script (`runtime/shown-data.js`, `.doc-component-data`), the same for any custom block and styled by the kit alone: an array of objects as a table, its keys the columns; an object as its keys beside their values; any other array as a list, below its top a line of its values where it holds values alone (`.doc-component-line`); a value as itself, true and false as words, null as a dash, and a value alone, or a script of nothing, which is empty, a list of one with no bullet (`.doc-component-one`). A cell of no more than twenty characters keeps them on one line (`.doc-component-short`), the instance scrolling where that is wider than the page. Three levels down a value is said by how many it holds, and past twenty entries, eight columns, two hundred characters or the four hundred values one drawing holds, whatever each level holds, what is left out is said (`.doc-component-more`). What is no JSON is shown as it is written, and said to be none only where the note is, a preview that is no presentation. Every word of it is text, markup in a key or a value too, and no part's: its root is a table, a list of terms or a list, none of which a text part can be (`NO_TEXT`), and the page finds no part in it, whenever it marks an instance's text parts (installed/page.mjs `elementsOf`). It is drawn again only for a script that says something else or a node put back, and goes with the kit's other marks. Its fields and buttons are made `inert`, what it holds being read-only, each marked `data-doc-fallback-control` but those inert already. In a preview a note stands first in it, `.doc-component-note`, marked `data-doc-dev`, in the UI's language (`kit/words.js`), a word to who writes the page, saying why when the custom block is there and does not load: none in 演示视图 (`?present`), and none on paper. `DocKit.dehydrate` takes all of that off a copy of the view (the description's `runtime`), and the copy's kit draws it again. An export shows the same look with no note; an instance of a custom block is one too until an export carries its custom block.
- **Keeps one instance per host.** Every instance starts from its markup: no document script makes one (a script asks one for its state: `DocCharts.get`, `DocSheets.get`, `DocTables.getState`).
- **Tells an instance what it asked to hear** (`on`): `resize` (its width; one ResizeObserver for all hosts), `theme` (`doc-theme-change`, which ui.js sends when the theme shown changes: the reader's choice, a workbench's, or the system's while the reader has chosen none), `layout` (`doc-layout-change`, from the editor), `viewport`, `fonts` (once the page's fonts are in, with no detail; then, for each later load, the faces it brought in as the detail, `FontFace` each, so that what measured its text in other fonts need not measure it again: a table lays itself out again only for a family it measured its words in, or for KaTeX's when it holds a formula), `source` (a change inside a part its description edits as text or data, or in a slot, the document's blocks, with the records, whoever made it and whether the component owns what stands around it; an attribute's change among them where it says something of the file, `SOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` of `src/shared/component-source.mjs`, a to-do item's `data-checked`, a cell's `rowspan` and `colspan`, each `data-sort-*` of the table description, and a `class` only where its look classes change, `LOOK_CLASSES`, a color, a cell's alignment or bold, so that what the kit and the editor toggle as they lay a page out, a table's `number` and `doc-table--auto`, a code block's folds, is none and no component draws again for ever; nothing an instance among a slot's blocks draws, which it makes and makes again, a clock, but what its own parts say (`ofTheFile`: the change's region, `heldWithin` of `installed.mjs` for what an element holds, `heldBy` for an attribute); none for what `DocKit.quietly` changes, which is no word of the file; told in the kit's next frame, first, all the changes since the last frame in one notice, so that what the component asks to draw as it hears them draws in that frame), `content` (changes inside it, with their records; and `{ quiet: true }` as a third argument for the changes made through `DocKit.quietly(change)`, below) `reveal` (an element around it shown or hidden: `hidden` and `open`, which are no `source`), `editable` (a custom block's component: its context can write from now on, "A custom block on a page"), `clock` (each whole minute of the reader's clock, its detail `"minute"`, or `"day"` for the first minute of another day, midnight or the first a page that slept wakes to: one timer for the page, armed while an instance listens, so that no block keeps a timer of its own for a clock or a date) and `data` (`doc-workspace-change` on the window, its detail the kind of the workspace's data that changed, `todos`, `recent`, `stats` or `activity`, which the page's live script sends as the server says `data <kind>` ([workbench.md](workbench.md) "The workspace's facts"), told to an instance only for a kind it read with `context.workspace.query`: its `kinds`; and, its detail `"table"`, to a view of a table, an instance of a custom block whose needs hold `tables`, once a batch of the page's changes leaves the rows of the table it reads other than they were, "A custom block on a page").
- **Hears a change that leaves the page looking as it was** (`DocKit.quietly(change)`: the page editor rebuilding its blocks from the file as it starts, editor.md "Browser side"): what `change` did goes to the instances at once, its content changes marked quiet, and so does what they change as they hear it (a table's sort controls put back); hosts it adds start and hosts it took off go as with any change. A table hearing a quiet change lays itself out again once the page is idle (a second at most), not in the next frame: the frame editing starts in, which the outline's first drawing and a workbench's ready wait for, measured every table of a long document again (a long document's 365 cells: about 0.1 s).
- **Says when the page is drawn**: `html[data-doc-drawn]`, set once the page has loaded, the fonts are in and two frames in a row found nothing queued for drawing (the resize observer answers a frame's drawing in that frame, so one look is not enough); an export of the current view does not keep it. The page editor waits for it to put the reader back where they were (`restoreView`) instead of a timer.
- **Draws in one frame** what instances ask to draw (`context.frame(run)`), each in its own try: a failure is told on the console and, in the preview, next to its instance (`.doc-kit-error`, marked `data-doc-dev`, so neither the editor nor an export takes it), and the others draw on; a custom block's component goes further ("A custom block on a page"). A failure to start is told the same way, or thrown to the script that asked (`adopt`).
- **Destroys an instance once its host has left the page**, checked after each batch of changes, so a node moved within the page in one go is never destroyed; `context.signal` aborts with it.
- **Takes what instances left off an export of the current view** (`DocKit.dehydrate`, called by ui.js): each component's `dehydrate` (a table's sort controls taken out and its widths off, a chart's drawing off but its data), one that throws being told and costing no copy, then what the descriptions list under `runtime` (generated nodes, marks set on authors' elements and on `<html>`); `runtime.state` (a Sheet chosen, the theme) stays. The copy's kit makes the rest again, so an export of an export is the same.

One MutationObserver (children, text, `hidden` and `open`; once an instance hears `source`, the attributes that say something of the file and `class` too, with what each was, `attributeOldValue`, the page with no custom block paying nothing for them) watches the page for all components; what a component draws into a host it owns (`owns`: a chart's SVG) is not watched. An added node is looked in for hosts only when it holds one of a component that starts later (one selector for them all), and each change goes to the hosts it is in, found from where it happened up, not by asking each host whether it holds it: the page editor's rebuild makes thousands of changes at once. `DocKit.stats()` counts the instances (live by component, mounted, destroyed, failed) for tests and the console. The editor needs nothing of its own for this: its moves keep the instances, its deletes destroy them, its undo starts them again.

Code blocks (`code.js`) are drawn as the page renders with their lines in plain text: their rows, line numbers, fold arrows and range of lines, everything that lays them out (colors change no width: one monospace font). Their colors (Shiki's tokens, from its JavaScript regex engine) come after the frame the page shows in, while the page is idle, the blocks near the window first (an IntersectionObserver, 400px past it), each line taking the spans a block drawn at once gets (`DocCode.draw`, which the page editor calls when a block's language changes, colors at once): tokenizing every block before the first paint held the page hidden (a page of fifteen code blocks: about 0.4 s). A block drawn again meanwhile, or opened for editing, is left to what drew it; one taken off the page with its block (a cut, which an undo puts back) is colored once it is back in sight. `DocCode.colorAll()` colors every block still waiting at once: before an export of the view (ui.js), before printing (`beforeprint`) and before a picture or PDF of the page is taken (`settle`, `src/transfer/browser.mjs`; kit-diff.mjs too). Code in a language the kit does not color is drawn at once, as are diffs. The grammars are lines of `code.js` before its bundle (`globalThis.DocCodeGrammars`, one a grammar: `src/shared/code-grammars.mjs`), loaded as a language is first colored, with those it embeds (HTML: JavaScript and CSS); a language whose grammar, or one it embeds, the file does not carry is drawn plain, as one the kit has none for. The preview loads them all (1.14 MB, of which the highlighter 0.19 MB): its editor picks any language for a block and colors it at once, a block a live pull or a paste brings is colored as it comes, and the file is one the browser keeps. An export carries those its code uses ("Export"), which `DocCode.grammarsUsed(root)` reads off a page as the source's reading does (`pageGrammars()`, the export's), the same rules (`autoLanguage` and `optionsLanguage`, the language a block or a diff is colored in, are the shared module's): each code block's language (`data-language`, else its code's `language-<id>` class, else its title's extension or what its code looks like) and each diff's (its options' `language`, else its `path`'s extension), a template's too; all of them where any script but the kit's own (not one of JSON), an event handler or a `javascript:` address names `DocCode` (`ReportCode` too) or a code block's or a diff's class (`doc-code`, `doc-diff`, their `report-` names; `NAMES_CODE`): such a script may draw code of any language, or put a block on the page as it runs (a document's script, a custom block's), which the kit draws though no source holds it. A reader cannot change a block's language in an exported file (the language menu is the page editor's).

## Build pipeline

- **Per-report isolation.** `loadReports()` loads each document on its own; a malformed directory, invalid `report.json`, or failing check is collected in a `slug → messages` map instead of throwing. `buildReports()` writes previews only for passing documents and removes the stale preview of a failing one. `exportReports()` exports passing documents and leaves an earlier export of a failing document untouched (the CLI says it was not updated). Each command processes the documents it names (`gallery export <slug>…`), or every document of the workspace with `--all` (`gallery check` with none named checks every one; the others refuse none, never every document). The gallery command (`src/cli/bin.mjs`, the package's `<package>/bin/gallery.mjs`, which checks the Node version first, then `src/cli/gallery.mjs`) reads the command line before anything is loaded: `--version` prints the version first, alone on its line, then what is newer than it (`src/cli/updates.mjs`, the one module it loads: a Gallery skill older than the gallery, a newer Gallery on npm from a cache it never waits for; docs/internals.md, "Packaging"), and `help` the commands (`OVERVIEW` in `src/cli/usage.mjs`), importing nothing else; `--workspace <dir>`, anywhere before `--`, and `init`'s own `<dir>` name the workspace, which it finds (`findWorkspace()` in `src/shared/workspace.mjs`) and sets as `GALLERY_WORKSPACE` before it loads the command's module (`src/cli/<command>.mjs`, its `run(argv, context)`), so `src/shared/paths.mjs` computes the workspace's paths from it and a server the command starts inherits it; a command that reads or writes documents makes the workspace when it is not there (`ensureWorkspace()`; `stop` never does), and one that cannot be written is said with what to do (`Cannot write the workspace <abs> (EACCES): allow Gallery to write there, or pass --workspace <a folder you may write>.`), as is another program's `gallery.json`. `--help` or `-h` prints the command's usage (`USAGE` in `src/cli/usage.mjs`: comments do not survive the release's bundle) and exits 0; an option it does not take, a wrong number of arguments, no document named, documents named with `--all`, or a name that is not a slug (`slugPattern`, lowercase kebab-case: `Natural` would reach `natural/` where case is ignored, `../x` a file outside the workspace) or one the workspace keeps (`exports`, the names Windows keeps for devices) print why first, then one line, the usage's first with where the rest is (`Usage: gallery qa <slug>... [--tiles] [--blocks <id,…>] [--no-export]   (gallery qa --help says more)`; `usageFailureText()` and `UsageError` in `src/cli/arguments.mjs`: the whole usage first buried the reason), and exit 2, so a mistyped command reads and writes nothing. After `--` every argument is a name (a title `"-Q3 回顾"`: `gallery new q3 -- "-Q3 回顾"`). The commands say a failing document as `FAIL <slug>: N problems`, each on a line of its own, and exit 1 when any selected document fails, except with `--allow-failures`. Export says which shared tags an older source writes that the document does not use (`shared scripts and styles left out (unused): …`, `pruneUnusedKitTags()`); its own `assets/` are not touched.
- **One reader of the HTML.** The checks read a document as one parse5 tree (`parseReport()` in `src/render/report-tree.mjs`: every element with its place in the source, scripts on, so a `<noscript>` holds text), through the helpers every system shares (`src/shared/html-tree.mjs`: `attr`, `hasClass`, `walk`; the components' selectors `matchesNode`, `partOf`, `instancesOf`): the component, chart and layout checks, the shared tags' check, the TypeScript program's scripts and their lines (`typecheck.mjs`; no JSDOM in the render), and the page's icons (`iconsOf()` in `emoji.mjs`: `icon` and `page.header`'s selectors, an icon is any element the description matches). A file that starts with a byte order mark is read as a browser reads it (`parseReport()` puts a space in its place, which keeps every offset and line). What stays text is what rewrites the file's own text and must leave every other byte of it: the embeds of the icon, pictures, files and frames, and of every other reference to the document's folder (`emoji.mjs`, `images.mjs`, `files.mjs`, `embeds.mjs`, `asset-references.mjs`; the pictures, files, frames and references are the tree's elements, rewritten at the place the tree gives their attribute, `showReferences()` in `media-ref.mjs` and `embedReferences()`, or in a `<style>` at the place of the address in its text, so a tag reads as a browser reads it, any case, double, single or no quotes, spaces around `=`, a `>` in a quoted value, character references, a class as its tokens, and their checks and their embeds read the same attribute, while markup a comment, a `<textarea>` or a script's string spells is none of them), the shared tags' pruning and putting in (`assets.mjs`: put in at places the tree gives, the tags a source writes found on it), and `structureFailures()`'s look at the text for what no element holds (a starter placeholder). Every check that names a place in the file says its line (`line 37: …`).
- **One TypeScript program.** `diagnoseReports()` compiles every document's inline scripts in one program for speed. Each diagnostic carries its owning `report.html`; diagnostics outside all documents (SDK declarations) are attached to every document. Its semantic diagnostics are asked of the documents' files and the SDK's declarations alone (`diagnosticsOf`): TypeScript's own lib files and the type packages (d3's) are still read and their syntax checked, but not type-checked again on every run (checking `lib.dom.d.ts` took about 600 ms of each `gallery check`), and `diagnoseScript()` (a block's `kit.js`) asks the same way. When no document holds a script or a declared diff, no program is made at all (a check of prose took a second or more for it). TypeScript returns forward-slash paths, so owner lookups go through `path.resolve()`, and printed paths use `/` on every platform. TypeScript's word on a type alone in a document's own script (`typeOnly`: a semantic diagnostic in an inline script of its own, not in a declared diff's options, and not what a browser refuses or throws on though TypeScript's checker, not its parser, says it: `TS2304`/`TS2552`, a name nothing defines; `TS2300`/`TS2451`, a name declared twice, two classic `<script>`s' `const data` among them, as they share one global scope; `TS2448` to `TS2450`, a name used before its declaration; and a grammar error, `TS1000` to `TS1999`, such as an `await` in a function that is not async) is no failure: strict TypeScript says `'box' is possibly 'null'` or that a number is no string of a plain browser script (`setAttribute("width", 300)`, a `.find()` or `querySelector()` result used straight), which a browser runs as written, and an Agent's drawing of its own (`DocCharts.define`, its own SVG) should not have to be typed to pass. `checkReports()` keeps each as the document's `scriptNotes` and `reportNotes()` prints it among its other notes (`notesByKind()`'s `notes`, after those to act on), `line 7: TS18048 in the document's script (column 26): 'first' is possibly 'undefined'. (strict TypeScript's word on a type: a note, not a failure; …)`; one on a line taking `d3.min`, `d3.max` or `d3.extent`, or naming d3's `NumberValue`, adds that d3 reads values of no type as strings and the fix is the data's shape in JSDoc (`untypedNumbers`: a drawing's `data` is `any`, and an Agent changed correct code three times to quiet such notes, in an Agent simulation of 2026-10-26). A syntax error (the program's syntactic diagnostics, TypeScript's own syntax in a JS file among them: `TS8010`, and the grammar errors the checker says), a name nothing defines, declared twice or used before its declaration, what a declared diff's options say, and every diagnostic of the shared declarations still fail; `typecheckReports()` still gives every diagnostic (the SDK's contract, `tests/unit/sdk.mjs`).
- **Declared charts.** `chartDeclarationFailures()` (`src/render/chart-declarations.mjs`) checks every `data-doc-chart` host: its type must be one the document's script defines (`DocCharts.define("type"`), or the `kit.js` of a custom block the page holds an instance of, which runs on that page (`definedTypes`: `packagesOf`, so that a block can bring a chart type of its own for the chart tables an Agent writes in its slots; one defined with a name the kit refuses, not lowercase letters, digits and hyphens with a letter first, as `ciPlot`, is said by that rule, not as a type the script does not define; a type no define names fails with how to define it, whatever its name, or, when the script defines one a letter or two apart or holding it (`nearestType`: `region-bar` for `region-bars`), with that name, a slip said as one); a host drawn from a table (`data-doc-chart-data`) holds no JSON, and its key names one table's frame, whose table has no merged cells; any other host needs its data in a JSON script, which must parse. A view of a table (`data-doc-from` on an instance of a custom block: "Charts from a table") is held to its table the same way (`tableViews`: on an instance alone, a key, one frame carrying it, holding a table), its merged cells, its table's rows past `VIEW_ROWS` and a block whose needs hold no `tables` noted by `chartDeclarationNotes()`. A table left written with `data-doc-chart` where no card stands (in a `<figure>`, a cell or a whole block) fails with how to write it. Every declared diff (`div.doc-diff`) is checked too, by the check the code kit draws it with (`validateDiff()` in `kit/diff-options.js`, a plain module Node imports directly: `/kit/code.js` carries the highlighter): its lines need a type, single-line content and a positive line number. The TypeScript program checks a declared diff's options against the SDK too: each becomes the argument of `__declaredDiff(…)` (a call the SDK declares for the checks alone), written over the blanked start tag or at the end of the nearest line above with room, so a wrong option is reported at its HTML line and column. `chartDeclarationNotes()` notes, never failures, a card drawn from a table with no title and a header that names no column or one twice; `tableChartReadings()` says, a line each, what each chart drawn from a table reads of it (its rows, each column's values as numbers, words or null, its `<tfoot>` rows and the cells past the header left out).
- **Components.** `componentFailures()` (`src/render/component-checks.mjs`) says first each attribute whose value the file opens with a curly quote, `class=”doc-sheet-tab”` or `data-sheet-tab=“base”` (`curlyQuoted()` in `src/shared/html-tree.mjs`, read from the attribute's place in the file, never from its value, so `title="“引语”"` is no failure; an editor that keeps a file's “…” style writes them, as Claude Code's Edit does when its old text matched only with its quotes made straight): HTML takes no curly quote for a quote, so the value is read unquoted with the quotes in it, and the failures that follow from it (a Sheet area with no tabs, a tab with no panel) would not say why: `line 119: attribute class=”doc-sheet-tab” uses curly quotes (U+201C/U+201D), which HTML reads as part of the value: write straight quotes, class="doc-sheet-tab"; an editor keeping a file's “…” style may change them`, the next ones `line 120: attribute data-sheet-tab=“base” uses curly quotes too: write data-sheet-tab="base"`; the whole-block note (`writingNotes`, below) is not given to an element whose class is so written. Then it holds a document's markup to the components' descriptions (`src/shared/components/`): each instance has the parts it needs and no more than it may (a detail card's content, a Sheet area's tabs and panels; a `details.doc-details` without one of its parts, a plain disclosure given the kit's class, is one line, `details (line 12) carries the kit's class without the kit's parts: write a plain <details> without the class (its <summary>, then what it holds): the export makes it the kit's`, never each part it lacks, which led an Agent to write the kit's markup by hand: `PLAIN`; a Sheet area with neither a row of tabs nor a panel is one line too, why it is no short form, `shortSheets` in `src/shared/components/sheets.mjs`, and what to write, `sheets (line 30) has no tabs and is no short form: its <section> (line 31) has no heading (<h2>–<h4>); write a <section> straight in it for each tab…`; an `a.doc-bookmark` the export did not make a card, a bookmark's short form that is none, says what one is), parts that go together pair (each tab its panel), every `data-doc-*` attribute and `doc-*` class is one an author writes (not a mark the kit sets, not a misspelling, which it names the nearest name for, nor a document's own class named like the kit's), `data-doc-bind` is on an element that can hold a key, every key `data-doc-show` reads is bound by something, and no formula is written between single dollars: `$…$` with `\` or `^` inside, or a subscript as TeX writes one (`_` after a letter of its own, a bracket or a brace: `x_1`, `a_{ij}`, `(x+y)_2`; a name's underscore, `$JAVA_HOME`, `$_GET`, `${user_name}`, `$my_var$`, is no formula), the dollars where pandoc takes them for a formula's (a non-blank right after the first and right before the last, no digit after it), in text the page shows fails at its line, `line 12: $x^2$ is not drawn as a formula (single dollars are off: they are money): write \(x^2\)` (the kit draws none, so the reader would see its TeX; money such as `$5 and $6`, `$$…$$`, an escaped dollar, code, a script, a formula of the kit's and an element with `data-doc-math-dollar` never fail).
- **Instances of custom blocks.** `componentFailures()` says of each element with `data-doc-component` what is wrong with it as an instance: on anything but a block-level `div` or `section`, or naming no `<scope>/<name>`. One of a custom block the workspace holds (`currentCatalog()`, `src/shared/component-catalog.mjs`: `<home>/components/*/*/component.json`) is checked as a kit component is, by the parts its manifest says it has, and a custom block a page uses has a script a browser can read: `kit.js` is compiled here, with `vm.Script`, and never run, once for each stamp (`local/x: its kit.js does not run: Unexpected token (line 2)`). One of no custom block is no failure, there being nothing to check it by: `componentNotes()` prints a note under `gallery check` and `gallery export`, and says why when the custom block is there and does not load ("The catalog of custom blocks" below). What stands in an instance's own region is checked by nothing but the kit's own names, closed there too (`doc-*`, `data-doc-*`; `report-*`, which `newNames` passes by there, is a name of its own): the walks stop at one and go on in its slots (`BODY` of `src/shared/components/index.mjs`: `walk` with `stop` and `through`; `instancesOf`, the custom blocks' files a page loads, the declared charts, the links' notes), where what stands is checked as the document's, an instance there by its parts and its custom block loaded, so a class of the kit's in it asks for no parts, a `<pre>` standing straight in one or a `$` in its text are the component's, a key it binds binds nothing outside it, a chart's mark declares nothing and a link in it gets no note. That goes for the element itself, whatever class it carries. What reads the file for what it holds goes on reading it: an id there is still taken. A slot is checked as its manifest says (`slotFailures`, and the parts' own check): `data-doc-slot` on anything but a block-level `div` or `section`, on the instance itself, outside any instance's own markup (in the document, in a slot, in a part edited as text or data), carrying a `data-block-id`, of a name that is no part edited as `flow`, twice, or missing where the manifest does not say `optional` (`has no body (data-doc-slot="body")`).
- **Removed calls.** A document script that still makes what markup says now (`DocSheets.mount`, `DocCode.View`/`DiffView`/`DiffStats`/`enhance`, `DocTables.enhance`/`enhanceAll`/`makeSortable`, `DocDialogs.enhance`/`enhanceAll`) fails with what to write instead (`REMOVED` in `pipeline.mjs`); the SDK no longer declares them and the kit no longer exports them (`DocTables` and `DocDialogs` hold only what a script may still ask: state, layout, open and close; `DocCode` is the page editor's alone: `drawLines`, `decorate`, `draw`, `fold` and what its language menu lists).
- **Code.** What a document's code may not do is looked for in its code alone (`codeUses()` in `typecheck.mjs`: its inline scripts, the handlers of its `on*` attributes and its `javascript:` addresses, each parsed by TypeScript, so a code sample, prose, inline `<code>`, a comment, a string, a chart's or a diff's JSON, or another object's `data.fetch` is none; a script of any JavaScript type runs, and an address is read as a browser's URL parser reads it, tabs and line breaks gone; `window.`, `self.` and `globalThis.`, and their elements by a literal, `window["fetch"]`, name the global): any use of `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket` or `EventSource`, `navigator.sendBeacon` or `import()` (`NETWORK`) fails, a call, a `new` or any other (`fetch.call(…)`, `const load = fetch`; an export's policy blocks the network: `line 37: the document's script calls fetch(…): embed its data (inline JSON, DocState); …`), and so does any use of `localStorage` or `sessionStorage` (`DocState` instead), each with its line and what holds it; `typeof fetch`, which asks whether there is one, is no use, and a name the code binds itself (`function load(fetch)`) is its own. `quickFailures()` reads the code once for both.
- **Block ids, the document's id, the size.** A repeated `data-block-id` is counted on the elements of the tree (an id a code sample spells is none; one written without quotes or with character references is one) and named with the lines of the elements carrying it (and their columns when two share a line, `10:4 and 10:30`), the copy to take it off being the later one unless the copy went before its original. Every document has `main[data-doc-id]` (the page's first `<main>`, none a `<template>` holds) equal to its slug (`DocState` and the shared UI keep its reading state under it; the workspace changes it with the slug): missing and different are said apart. The editable source is 1,000,000 bytes at most, and the check says how big it is and what to do (aggregate or sample the embedded data, or split the document).
- **Images.** An image block's picture (`figure.doc-image > img`), and a bookmark's icon and cover (`figure.doc-bookmark img`), is a file of the document's folder, `assets/<file>` of an image type, that is there (a picture with no `src` fails too), and a bookmark's link a web address (`imageFailures` in `render/images.mjs`; a fast check). One the preview serves no file by says why and what to write (`assetProblem()`, below), never only the markup: `line 12: an image's picture (assets/B263_古文孝経.png): its name holds 古, 文, 孝, 経, which the preview serves no file by (a name is 1 to 81 characters, …): copy the file in under such a name, as assets/b263.png` (the `<img>` had its `alt`: no `alt="…"` template, which sent an Agent adding alts to 43 pictures). The preview serves it from the folder (`/__gallery/edit/asset/<slug>/assets/<file>`, with `data-ge-src`); a build and an export embed it as a data: URL (`embedImages`).
- **Files.** A file block's file (`figure.doc-file > a.doc-file-link`) is a file of the document's folder, `assets/<file>`, that is there, with a `download` name (`fileFailures` in `render/files.mjs`; a fast check). The preview hands it out from the folder for saving (`/__gallery/edit/file/<slug>/assets/<file>`, with `data-ge-href`); a build and an export embed it as a data: URL (`embedFiles`).
- **Embeds.** An embed block's frame (`figure.doc-embed > iframe.doc-embed-frame`) shows an https page, sandboxed, or an HTML (sandboxed without `allow-same-origin`) or PDF file (with no sandbox: a sandboxed frame shows no PDF) of the document's folder that is there (`embedFailures` in `render/embeds.mjs`; a fast check, each failure with its line). The preview shows a file from the folder (`/__gallery/edit/embed/<slug>/assets/<file>`, an HTML file under a CSP sandbox, with `data-ge-src`); a build and an export embed it as a data: URL (`embedFrames`), the runtime showing a PDF's through a blob: address (`runtime/embeds.js`). The export's policy allows `frame-src https: data: blob:`. For a platform that strips frames (语雀 strips every `<iframe>`), `gallery export --embeds-as-links` writes each embed block as a link (`embedsAsLinks`, run on the source before it is rendered, so no file is embedded for a frame that is gone): the bookmark's markup (`figure.doc-bookmark > a.doc-bookmark-card`, the look a document has for a link to a page, which `report.css` holds on every page), the figure keeping its other classes, its id and its caption; a web page's card says its frame's `title` (else its host) and its address and opens it in a new tab; a file of the folder, which no reader elsewhere can open and the export then carries not, is a card of its name and `嵌入的文件，没有随导出带出` (English on an English page), with no `href`, which `bookmark.css` makes none to click; an empty block stays as it is. Imported again, such an export's embeds are bookmarks: it is made for a platform, not to come back.
- **The files of the document's folder** are named by one rule and one table, `shared/media.mjs`: `assets/<file>` (a name of letters, digits, dots, dashes and underscores; capital letters in an extension count; flat and ASCII on purpose, the rule kept: what breaks it is said by `assetProblem(value, { picture })`, one reason each, for the images', files', embeds' and every other reference's check alike: not in `assets/` (a web address, another folder, `./assets/`, `ASSETS/`, `..`), in a folder of it (`move the file into assets/, as assets/scan.png`), a name of characters it does not take, each named (a space as "a space"), too long or starting with neither a letter nor a digit, with a name it takes (`assetName()`: the name's runs of ASCII letters and digits in lowercase, dashes between, its extension kept: `B263_古文孝経__NDL1288405.png` → `assets/b263-ndl1288405.png`, `picture.png` when none is left), and, for a picture, a file of no picture format (`.tiff is no picture format a page shows (png, jpg, gif, webp, avif, svg or ico): convert it to one`)), its media type by its extension, the three routes the preview serves them at (`asset`, `file`, `embed`: what each takes), the upload limits (which blocks are media blocks is their description's `media`). `render/media-ref.mjs` shows the references to such files for the images, files and embeds, the elements each finds on the tree, in one pass over the text (`showReferences()`: the preview's route with the file said in `data-ge-<attribute>`, an export's data: URL; a reference that is not a file there is, or not what the route takes, stays as written; a page's icon builds its own `<img>` at the same route address, `servedUrl`); `server/media-route.mjs` serves them, each route's headers in one table (a picture as its type, an SVG under a CSP `sandbox` of no permission, which an `<img>` never reads, so that one opened on its own, in a frame or by a link, runs nothing in the preview's origin; a file as an `attachment`, an embed's HTML under a CSP sandbox). The preview names each such file by what it is now: its route's address with `?v=<stamp>` (`servedUrl`, every reference of the page alike, `asset-references.mjs`'s and the page icon's too; none for a file that is not there), the stamp 12 hex digits of the file's size and its times of writing and of change (`fileStamp`, `stampOfStat`: any write is a new stamp, a copy keeping the time of the file it copies too; the bytes are never read for it), so that a file written again under its name is a new address: Chrome never fetches an address a page has used again while that page stays open, whatever the answer's `no-cache` says, and so a picture an Agent copied over `assets/shot.png` went on showing the old one (2026-11-17; [editor.md](editor.md) "A file of the folder written again"). The routes take no part of the query and send the file as it is now, `no-cache`, its stamp its `ETag` (looked at before and after it is read, and given only when it did not change meanwhile), and answer a request holding that tag (`If-None-Match`) 304, from the file's metadata, its bytes not read: a page loaded anew (a reload, the workbench's page swapped in) asks again for each picture and is sent only the ones that changed. A custom page's `assets/` (`servePageAsset`) are tagged so too. The workbench's image of a page icon (`/__gallery/edit/icon`) is kept for good for an emoji, a glyph and an icon, never for a picture of the folder, which is asked for again each time (`no-cache`), tagged by its stamp and answered 304 while it did not change, as the media routes' (`serveStamped`). The block's own checks (`imageFailures`, `fileFailures`, `embedFailures`) stay in their modules. A page's icon takes the pictures of the folder but no `.ico`. What file a reference anywhere names is `referencedFile()`'s to read (`./assets/…` too, in any case, its query and fragment left out, its %-escapes read; none leaving the folder by `..`), an export's data: URL of it `dataUrlOf()`'s (`media-ref.mjs`); the table has the types of fonts and captions too (`woff`, `woff2`, `ttf`, `otf`, `vtt`), which such references load.
- **References to the document's folder.** `render/asset-references.mjs` reads every reference the markup makes to a file of the folder (`assets/…` or `./assets/…`: an element's `src`, `href` and `poster`, an `<object>`'s `data`, the `background` of a `<body>`, a `<table>` or its parts (which browsers still draw), each candidate of a `srcset`, and in a `<style>` or a `style` attribute each `url(…)`, `@import` and string of `image-set(…)`; text that spells one is none, nor is CSS in a `/* */` comment or a CSS string, `content: "url(…)"`, and a CSS escape is read as CSS reads it, `url(assets\/a.png)`). Every one outside the media blocks and the page's icon (whose modules show theirs, as `pictureTags`, `fileBlocks`, `frameStarts` and `iconImageStarts` find them) is shown by `embedReferences()`, the last step of `renderReportHtml()`, at its place in the file's text, by what the page does with it. A picture (an `<img>`'s or an `<input>`'s `src`, a `srcset`, a `<video>`'s `poster`, a `background`, a `<link rel="icon">`, an SVG's `<image>`, CSS `url()` and `image-set()`), a font, a video or audio and its captions: an export holds it as a data: URL in its place, a `srcset` only its largest candidate (by width, else density) with its descriptor, so that the picture keeps the size the page lays it out at and is as sharp as the densest screen takes it. The `<img>`'s `src` stays beside it, embedded too: HTML wants one, a screen of density 1 takes it where the `srcset` gives densities, and an import of the export takes the picture from it (`img[src]`); where the `srcset` gives widths no browser reads it, so its file's bytes are an export's cost for nothing (a `srcset` is rare in a document, and an Agent who writes one with widths can make `src` its smallest candidate). The preview serves a picture from `/__gallery/edit/asset/…` and any other such file from `/__gallery/edit/file/…` (its bytes, never shown as a page in the editor's origin). A link (`<a>`, `<area>`): a data: URL with a `download` name, the file's where the link gives none or an empty one (a browser opens no page from a data: address, Chrome refusing top-level navigations to one, so the reader saves the file); the preview's file route, which hands the file out. An iframe's page (an HTML file): a data: URL; the preview's `/__gallery/edit/embed/…`, which sandboxes it. The preview says the attribute's value as the file has it (`data-ge-<attribute>`, in place of one the file writes itself; not for a style), and the page editor, which rebuilds editable text from the source (a link in a paragraph has its `assets/…` again there), opens such a link at the file route on a click (`followLink`). `assetReferenceFailures()` fails, each with its line: a file that is not there (or a reference leaving the folder); what an export cannot hold (an `<object>` or `<embed>`, which the export's `object-src 'none'` refuses; a script's `src`; a stylesheet linked or `@import`ed, whose own `url()`s would be read from its place: its rules go in the page's `<style>`; a PDF in an iframe of its own, which an embed block shows (the kit's runtime hands its data: URL to the browser's PDF viewer as a blob: one, `runtime/embeds.js`, for an embed block's frame alone); an iframe of any other file but a page, which the preview's file route would hand out for saving on every load: a picture is an `<img>`, a file to save a link; anything else that loads a file); a file the preview cannot serve (a name outside `assetFile`'s rule, each case said by `assetProblem()` as the media blocks' are: `ASSETS/` is written in lowercase, a file in a folder of `assets/` moved into it, a name with a space, a character outside a-z, A-Z, 0-9, `.`, `-` and `_`, a first character that is none of a letter or a digit, or more than 81 characters copied in under a name it takes, one offered); a link that would open an HTML file (an export cannot open a page from a data: URL: an embed block shows one, or `download="<name>"` hands it out as a file); and a picture in an icon written as an icon is not read (single quotes, another class: `<div class="doc-icon"><img src="assets/<file>" alt=""></div>`). What fails stays as written in the preview and an export. A media block's file that is not there is its own check's to say. The same file referenced again is embedded again (an attribute names no copy held elsewhere in the file; CSS can, by a custom property the author writes once, `--picture: url(assets/<file>)` and `var(--picture)` where it shows, which serves a CSS background alone), so the copies the references outside the media blocks and the page's icon add, as data: URLs (each beyond the first, or each where a media block or the icon holds the file too), may make an export 1,000,000 bytes bigger at most (`REPEAT_BUDGET`): over it, one failure names each file repeated, how often and on which lines (a `srcset`'s smaller candidates, which an export leaves out, count for nothing). The media blocks' own repeats are theirs and count for nothing: four image blocks of one picture (a block copied in the editor keeps its `src`) pass, as they did before any other reference was embedded. `files` false (the workbench's 导出 without its files) leaves every reference as written.
- **What every export needs.** `exportFailures()` (`pipeline.mjs`) is what any export of a document runs, whichever way it is made, what an offline file adds and no more, a component's validity being `componentFailures()`, which both of the server's exports run too ("A custom block on a page"): a `<head>` start tag for the export's policy, no external resources or unsupported script and stylesheet tags (the page's `script`, `link` and `img` elements; one a comment, a code sample or a script's string spells is none), no network in its code, and its pictures, files, frames and folder references as above. They are part of `quickFailures()` (so `gallery check`, `gallery export` and the preview note them), and `offlineReports()`, the workbench's 导出, runs them with `componentFailures()` and no other (not TypeScript, nor the other authoring checks: placeholders, labels, block ids) before it makes a file, and its `check(slug)` runs them before a Markdown export, which is made of the source, what they say of a custom block left out: a document that fails makes no file, but an error with `reason: "check"`, its `slug` and its `failures`, which the transfer service answers with 422 (docs/workbench.md, "Import and export"). The transfer service makes each page's natural HTML the kit's in its file first, as the preview does before it shows one (`edits.naturalize`), so a page nobody has opened is checked as the kit's, as `gallery export` checks it.
- **The page's icon.** One `.doc-icon` at most, holding an icon (an emoji, `data-icon="/icons/<name>_<color>.svg"` (MingCute's) or `"/icons/tabler/<name>_<color>.svg"` (Tabler's), a name its set has, or `<img src="assets/<file>" alt="">`), its image in the document's folder (`iconFailures` in `pipeline.mjs`, the image's file `assetReferenceFailures`; fast checks). Each failure names its line (two page icons, the second's). One whose value is an icon written in other quotes than double ones (`data-icon='…'`, `<img src='…'>`: a page reads none) says what it holds as written and the icon as the file writes it. One that holds no icon says what it holds (`data-icon="rocket"`, `"AI"`, an `<svg>`) and the three kinds, an example of each as the file writes it, the ten colors among them; a name its set has not says the same name in the other set when that has it (`"airplane" is MingCute's: /icons/airplane_blue.svg`), else up to six names of its set sharing a word with it (`glyphNames()` in `emoji.mjs`).
- **Natural HTML and editing notes.** `gallery check` and `gallery export` (`src/cli/natural.mjs`) read the selected documents as the preview shows them: their natural HTML made the kit's in memory (`loadReports({ prepare })`, `shownSource`, docs/editor.md "Natural HTML"; with the kit tags its blocks then need), so what an Agent writes as any page would (a bare formula, `<details>`, `<pre>`, a bare table or picture, `<mark>`…) is checked, built and exported as the kit's. `gallery check` and `gallery export` first say, once, what the preview wrote of a document's natural HTML as the kit's markup when the reader edited it (or exported it from the workbench) since a command last said it (the edit service's record, docs/editor.md "Natural HTML": `preview <workspace>/<slug>/doc.html: at 14:32, when the reader edited the page, the preview wrote your natural HTML as the kit's markup: line 120 details, line 140 chart t1, line 140 table`, a line for each note, then `  those lines are the file's as you had left it`; `previewRewrites` in `src/cli/natural.mjs`), and right after it the 批注 Gallery deleted since because the words they were about are gone (the edit service's record, docs/editor.md "Annotations": `annotations <workspace>/<slug>/annotations.json: at 14:32, Gallery deleted 2 批注 (annotations) whose words are gone from the document (rewritten or deleted):`, each `  lob25hue "<its text>" on "<the words it quoted>"`, then `  they were the reader's: say in your hand-off which your edit carried out and which you left, and never write them back`; one back in `annotations.json` since, an undo having brought its words back, left out; `previewDeletions` in `src/cli/natural.mjs`). `gallery qa` says both too, before it exports or looks. They include the Agent's last write: the server checks a document's 批注 some 1.9 s after a write (the file quiet for 1.5 s, then 300ms), so `gallery export` reads them after its hand-in, which the server answers only once the 批注 are checked against what it wrote and what that deleted is on disk (a hand-in without a server checks them itself), and `gallery check` and `gallery qa` first ask the server to check them now (`POST /__gallery/edit/settle`, `previewSettled()` in `src/cli/hand-in.mjs`; no server, or an older one: nothing to wait for). Read before, an export run in the same command as the write said nothing and the next command did (the skill test of 2026-10-29). Check never writes a document: `gallery check` says what the export will write (`natural <workspace>/<slug>/doc.html: mark (line 12), details (line 30) not rewritten yet: the export rewrites them in this file as the kit's markup (nothing to fix)`, a line for each thing a rule drops, writes as the nearest kit color or keeps on a code block's pre or code), and the blocks it will give an id (`pendingBlockIds()` in `source/natural.mjs`: those of the text as pages see it without one, each at the line its start tag opens on, the file's lines, which naturalize keeps; none for a document whose ids repeat): `ids <workspace>/<slug>/doc.html: 2 blocks without an id yet: the export gives each one`, then each on a line of its own, `  line 40 <p>`, when they are ten at most (more, a first draft's every block, are counted alone; `idLines()` in `src/cli/hand-in.mjs`). The failures' lines are those of that text, the file's own but for the kit tags it adds in the head (a line each). **The export writes** (the hand-in, `src/cli/hand-in.mjs`): before it checks a document, `gallery export` writes its file as the preview shows it with an id for every block that has none, unless the quick checks and the charts' own validation of that text fail it (`handInBlockers()` in `pipeline.mjs`: its export would fail, and a file that is not exported takes no id, no natural HTML rewritten; TypeScript, slow, runs once, after the hand-in) (`handedInSource`, docs/editor.md "Block ids"), through the preview server's queue while one of this Gallery runs (`POST /__gallery/edit/hand-in`, found by `src/shared/preview-server.mjs`: a file in the edit state directory, one for each directory of documents, with its port, process and token; a server that is gone, or serves another directory, leaves the command to write the file itself, as the server does, the file read again right before it is replaced), and says what it wrote (`natural …: … rewritten in this file as the kit's markup (nothing to fix)`; `ids …: 12 blocks given an id`, then each block given one on a line of its own, the line its start tag opens on in the file written, its start tag as written but its id and style, three attributes at most, each value's line breaks and tabs one space, and its id, `  line 516 <button class=doc-sheet-tab type=button data-sheet-tab=opus> → wm63stkv`, when they are ten at most (a revision's few; more, a first export's every block, are counted alone: thirty such lines read as noise before the export's result): `handedInSource()`'s `given`, `startTagLabel()` in `source/ids.mjs`; a preview server of an older version that says the count alone, the count). No line asks the Agent to read again the files the tools wrote: a `重读：` line did until 2026-11-13 (the hand-in's, `add-block-ids.mjs`'s, `gallery set`'s, `history --restore`'s, `revert`'s and the page commands'), and the user took it out, an Agent of today judging for itself what to read again from what the lines say was written. What it prints comes in this order (`src/cli/export.mjs`): what the preview wrote and deleted since, the failures (`FAIL <slug>: N problems` and each on a line, on stderr: they came last, and were read past), the exports made (`Exported <workspace>/exports/<slug>.html (… bytes, sha256 …)`, its absolute path), the notes to act on first (`fix <slug>: …`: a path of this machine, a heavy picture), the other notes (`note <slug>: …`), what the hand-in wrote, last, for each document exported, its deliverable on a line of its own, `离线文件（用户要文件时再给）：<the absolute path of exports/<slug>.html>` (`deliverableLine()` in `src/cli/deliverable.mjs`, which `gallery qa`'s verdict repeats: the deliverable said as `exports/<slug>.html` alone was the one path an Agent made whole itself, wrongly once after its context was compacted, and the workspace, with its `exports/`, may be anywhere), and on stderr the count of documents that failed (`1 document failed: <slug> (the failures are listed first, above)`). A document whose ids repeat, a file that keeps changing while it is written, or a server that does not answer in 30s fails that document, nothing written or exported (repeated ids are named as the check names them, `data-block-id repeats: …`, at the lines of the text the check reads, found before the hand-in, or by the hand-in itself at the file's; none is renamed: which block is the copy is the author's to say; the workbench's 导出 and the workspace's export fail such a page the same way, and a page an Agent has half written too, docs/editor.md "Writing"). A second export writes nothing. The hand-in also keeps the document's name and icon in step with its page (`inStep()` in `source/natural.mjs`, as the editor does when the reader renames the document or changes its icon): `<title>` takes the h1's words when it does not hold them (`report.json`'s title replaced where it holds it once, else the whole of it; words of the old title that hold the new one's do not count: a `<title>` "销售报告（草稿）" becomes "销售报告"), `report.json`'s title becomes the h1's and its icon the header's own `.doc-icon` (one of no valid value is left to the check; a header with no icon leaves `report.json`'s as it is, an icon left out not being one taken away, and says so: `icon reports/<slug>: report.json has the icon "🎯" and the page's header none: write it there (<div class="doc-icon">…</div> before the h1) or take "icon" out of report.json (the export leaves report.json's as it is)`), and the export says so (`title reports/<slug>: <title> and report.json's title written as its h1 says it: "…"`, `icon reports/<slug>: report.json's icon written as its header has it: "🎯"`; `gallery check` says what it will write, `title reports/<slug>: <title> not as its h1 says it yet ("…"): the export writes it`, `inStepLines` in `src/cli/hand-in.mjs`); a second export writes nothing. `gallery check` and `gallery export` then print a note, never a failure, for each text the preview will still leave read-only (`readOnlyTexts`: a picture, a comment or a control amid the words, a display formula amid the words (one alone in its paragraph but for a bare `<` or `>` in its TeX, which keeps it from being a formula, said apart: `line 12: <p>: the formula holds a bare < or >: write \lt / \gt (or &lt; / &gt;)`), an unknown tag, a table's caption the intake did not take, a list of checkboxes that is no task list, once for the list, with what to write instead, `ul.doc-todo › li, li[data-checked]`, and words straight in a container that holds blocks too, which the intake could not make paragraphs; not what is inside a whole block of the document's own), with its line and why, and for what the preview takes otherwise than as blocks (`writingNotes`): each whole block of the document's own once, its outermost (`line 40: <div class="intro"> is a whole block (整体块): the reader moves it whole and edits its words in place; right for a layout of yours that is no figure (a figure, a diagram or a timeline, goes in <figure class="doc-figure">); drop its class only if its contents were meant as ordinary blocks`, a fact and not a fix: a whole block is how the Agent draws what the kit has no block for; not a text block with a class of its own, nor one of the kit's elements with a class of the document's own beside the kit's, nor one with no words and no picture, a host the document's own script draws in or a drawing, nor one holding an `<svg>` or a `<canvas>`, nor one of a class the document's own `<style>` names in a selector, a layout of its own: the note is for words wrapped in a class that were meant as ordinary blocks), and a table's column widths (`<colgroup> widths: the kit sizes columns itself; a merged table uses yours`), and a line or paragraph separator (U+2028, U+2029) in the page's text anywhere but its scripts and styles, code and the kit's blocks too, at its line (`line 88: a line separator (U+2028) in the text: the page shows it as a break or an empty line, not as a character (a tool that decodes \u2028 or \u2029 escapes writes one); write the break the text means (<br>, a new paragraph, a new line in code) or take it out`: an Agent's Write tool turned an escape it wrote into one, and a code block in a table showed empty, in an Agent simulation of 2026-10-26). Last, a note for each block the document's own script keeps (`scriptKept`, the preview's own reading of the units, docs/editor.md "In-place text editing", bound blocks), with the names that keep it, so that the Agent renames one the script does not select by: `line 40: <figure> in 方法 kept by the script: .lo (the reader cannot delete or convert it; rename any of these the script does not select it by)`; a block kept only for one it holds is left to that one's note (a d3 drawing's `row.lo` once kept a figure of class `lo` and its section, and nothing said so, in an Agent simulation of 2026-10-28).
- **Layout notes.** `layoutNotes()` (`src/render/layout-notes.mjs`) prints a note, never a failure, under `gallery check` and `gallery export`: for body text between two sections that does not start with a heading of its own, which reads like text the section above was meant to hold, said by the section's title, the first block's line and its tag and first words (the skill test of 2026-11-06 found a note naming the section alone, with no line to find the blocks by). Body text straight in the page is legal (a flat document, a preface, an epilogue, a run with its own heading), so nothing asks for sections to be added. And for body text given a width of its own (a `max-width` or `width` in a `style` attribute of a paragraph, list item, heading, quote or kit text group, or in a rule of the document's `<style>` aimed at body text as such; not a widget's own text, a selector with a class of the document's own, and not `none`, `auto`, `100%`): its lines break before the reading column's edge and leave an extra right margin ([visual-design.md](visual-design.md#composition)). And what is worth a look in a chart drawn from a table (`chartDeclarationNotes()` in `chart-declarations.mjs`): a card with no title, a header naming no column or one twice. `notesOf()` in `pipeline.mjs` gathers every note `reportNotes()` prints: these, what in a table chart's table reads oddly, and the two below, in two kinds (`notesByKind()`): those an Agent acts on first, each saying what to do (the two below, a path of this machine and a heavy picture), printed first, of every document, as `fix <slug>: …`, then the rest as `note <slug>: …` (an export's local path once came last among chart readings under the same `note`, and was left in); after them, information with nothing to do, how each table chart reads its table, as `chart <slug>: …` (its `readings`).
- **Local paths.** `localPathNotes()` (`src/render/local-paths.mjs`) notes, at its line, each path of the author's machine in the text a reader reads: a drive's (`C:\Users\…`, `D:/data/…`, after a Chinese character too, not after a Latin letter or a digit, and two characters of a name after it at least, so `http:` and a choice `A:/B` are none), a Unix home or system folder's (`/Users/…`, `/home/…`, `/tmp/…`, `/opt/…`; not inside a web address), a home's as a shell writes it (`~/…`, `$HOME/…`), a share's (`\\server\share\…`), an environment variable of one (`%TEMP%`), and a path through `AppData` or a `scratchpad`, or through a `Temp`/`tmp` folder in a path from a drive, the root or such a variable (a project's relative `build/tmp/` is none); a web address's folders are its site's, whatever their names (a word holding `://`, but a `file:` address). The text is the page's, its `<title>` among it: not its attributes, comments, scripts or styles, not a `<template>`'s or a `[hidden]` element's, not a code sample (`pre`, `code`, `kbd`, `samp`, `var`), and not a formula's TeX (between `\(` `\)`, `\[` `\]` or `$$`, between single dollars under `data-doc-math-dollar`, and all a `.doc-math-inline` or `.doc-math-block` holds). A path ends at a blank, a quote, a bracket, a `;` or a Chinese sentence's punctuation, or at a Chinese character once a file's extension ended (`C:\work\data.csv里`). Twelve are said one by one, the rest in one note with their lines. A note, never a failure, among those to act on first (`fix <slug>: line 12: "C:\Users\…" is a path of this machine in the text a reader reads: a reader on another machine cannot open it. Write instead where it came from, in words, or a path relative to materials/ (materials/data.csv)`, `INSTEAD`; the note of the rest ends so too): whether the document goes to such a reader, and whether the path stays, is the author's to know.
