- **Heavy pictures.** `largePictureNotes()` (`src/render/picture-sizes.mjs`) notes each picture of the document's folder an export embeds as it is (`referencedFiles()`' `embedded` of `asset-references.mjs`, as `embeddedFiles()` gives them: a media block's, the icon's and every other reference an export holds, each time it holds it) that is over 1 MB or longer than 2400px on its long side; not the picture of an `<img>` the export makes a copy of ("The pictures an export embeds": `pictureTask` at the ceiling the document's look gives, `lookCeiling()` in `pipeline.mjs`), which weighs what the page needs whatever its file does, so an Agent copies a picture in as it is (a picture that is so heavy is said only where it is shown more than once, then with no weight, or where a CSS background names it), but where the export makes no copy (`copies`, which the command gives: no Chrome or Edge on the machine, `--original-images`), where it is weighed as it is and its note says why ("The pictures an export embeds"): its lines, size, pixels (`pictureSize()`, read from its first bytes: PNG, GIF, JPEG, WebP, AVIF; none for an SVG), what the export holds of it as data: URLs (a third bigger, each time) and about what a copy would weigh: 2000px on its long side, a JPEG, WebP or AVIF one written as a JPEG of quality about 82 (a quarter of a byte a pixel), a PNG or GIF one as it is (with a JPEG's weight beside a PNG's when that is less than half, for a photograph); one that is only heavy, as a JPEG of quality about 82; a lossless WebP (VP8L) is weighed as a PNG is, and no copy is said that is not a quarter lighter at least (`assets/photo.jpg (line 40): 3.4 MB, 7458×4972px; the export embeds it as it is, 4.5 MB as a data: URL — a 2000px copy would be about 0.6 MB`). A picture the document names by a name the preview serves none by (`referencedFiles()`' `unserved`: in a folder of `assets/`, a name of characters it does not take), which fails, is weighed all the same, as the export will embed it once it is copied in under a name it takes, so that the copy is made the size the page needs at once (`…; its name keeps it out of the preview and the export (the failure at its line says why); copied into assets/ under a name they take, the export embeds it as it is, 2.0 MB as a data: URL — a 2000px copy would be about 0.1 MB: copy it in at the size the page needs`). And a picture the page shows more than once, whatever its size, is noted (each place holds a data: URL of its own: `assets/map.png (lines 12 and 80): shown twice: the export embeds it twice; show it once and refer back to it in words (0.4 MB as data: URLs)`, the same words after a heavy picture's note); an icon's picture (the page's, a bookmark's icon or cover, a link's icon beside each link to its site) counts for none. A note, never a failure, and nothing is embedded once for all its places (no runtime dedupe).
- **Assets.** `ASSETS` (`assets.mjs`) lists every shared asset once, in load order: its id (its tag's `data-doc-kit` or `data-doc-vendor`), a kit file and how `kit-build.mjs` makes it or a vendor file and where it comes from, the global it defines, whether every document loads it (`always`: `styles`, `runtime`, `state`, `tokens`; `added`: `ui`, for every document whose main has a `data-doc-id`, and every custom page), whether every custom page loads it and no document (`page`: `board-styles`, `board`), the assets it loads after, and whether it is a UMD bundle. The list is checked as it loads (an id twice, an unknown or misordered `after`, a kit file not saying how it is made, an asset no document would keep, a component needing an asset that is not listed), and everything else follows from it (`tests/unit/assets.mjs`): the tags (`kitTags`; [report-contract.md](report-contract.md)'s, where it lists them, the same, in order), the preview's `public/kit` and `public/vendor`, an export's inline copies, and the tags a page gets.
- **Kit tags.** A source writes none (the starter and the Component Catalog write none): the render puts in every tag a document needs and does not write (`withKitTags()`, in `renderReportHtml()`, so the preview, a build, `gallery export` and the workbench's 导出 alike), those every document loads, those it uses (`usedAssets()`, below: a component in its markup, found by its `uses` or its `match`, src/shared/components/; a global the code its scripts run names; a file its custom blocks' manifests name in `assets`) and `ui.js`, in the list's order. Its stylesheets go in its head, before its own first stylesheet (a `<style>` or a linked one: the document's own rules win over the kit's of the same weight), else at the head's end; its scripts in its body (ui.js puts its button there as it loads), before its first script of JavaScript after its `<main>` (where a document's own script goes: one that calls `DocCharts.define` runs once `charts.js` has), or before the outermost `<svg>` or `<math>` that holds that script (an SVG's `<script>` loads no `src`), else at the body's end. Never in the main or before it: `charts.js` starts each card as the parser reaches it (`when: "now"`), so a card below the kit's scripts could start before its data were read whole; a script in the head, before the main or in it that names a global of the kit is the checks' to move (below). A page that writes no `<head>` or no `</body>` gets them where a browser still reads them as its head's or its body's; each tag stands on a line of its own at the indent of what it stands before, with the file's line ends. An older source that writes the tags keeps them (the render prunes those it does not use, below), and what it lacks goes among those of its kind (stylesheets, scripts): right after the last the list puts before it, else right before the first, so its page is as it was. What the checks ask of the tags a source writes (`tagFailures()`, each with its line; a tag a source does not write is never a failure): each is the kit's own tag as it is (`tagOf()`, the text the build prunes and an export inlines: one written otherwise would stay linked, which the export refuses), written once, and they load in the order their `after` lists ask; an export's inline copy of the kit (`data-doc-kit="runtime-inline"`: an exported file copied in as a source) fails, as the kit would load twice, and so does anything marked `data-doc-package`. A classic script of JavaScript (a module runs once the page is read) that names a global of the kit before the kit's script defining it loads fails too (`earlyScriptFailures()`, with its line; broad on purpose, a name in a function counting too, comments not): one in the head, before the main or in it is told to move after `</main>`, one before a tag an older source writes to move after that tag. The TypeScript program declares the kit's globals (`skills/gallery/sdk/index.d.ts`) whatever tags a source writes. A block the page editor makes that needs an asset (a Sheet area: `sheets.js`) has it on the page at once (`ensureSheetsKit`); its commit gives a file that writes the kit's scripts (an older one) the tag among them (the edit service's `finish`, `withNeededKitTags()`), and nothing to one that writes none, whose page gets it from the render. The browser scenario `kitTags` serves and exports a document made from the starter.
- **Asset pruning.** `usedAssets()` scans the document with the shared tags removed; detection is deliberately broad because a false positive only keeps an asset, while a false negative breaks a page. A component's assets, a custom block among the components for a page that has instances of it (its manifest's `assets` of the kit), are kept when one of its `uses` patterns matches (src/shared/components/: `doc-code` or `doc-diff`; `\(`, `\[`, `$$` or `data-doc-math`; `data-doc-sheets`; `doc-dialog`; `data-doc-chart=`; `<table` or `doc-table`) outside comments and the text of code samples (what `pre`, `code`, `textarea`, `noscript`, `option` and `style` hold at any depth, the elements KaTeX leaves alone, `MATH_IGNORED_TAGS` in `components/math.mjs`, shared with `kit/math.js`, and a script of JSON, `application/json` or `application/<x>+json`; the tags of the elements in a sample still count, as a browser shows a block the parser puts there, and a script of JavaScript counts, as it may write blocks and formulas into the page), or when the page has one (its `match`, as the checks find it), an asset when its script names its global (`DocCode`, `DocMath`, `DocSheets`, `DocDialogs`, `DocCharts`, `DocTables`, any `d3` word) outside comments: in the code of its inline scripts of JavaScript (templates' too), its `on…` attributes and `javascript:` addresses (read as a browser reads one: its tabs and line breaks out), its comments left out by TypeScript's parser (only a script naming a global is parsed), so a `//` in a string (a URL) or a regular expression stays code, and a name in prose, a code sample or a comment (a script's comment naming `DocCharts`) keeps nothing. An asset kept keeps those it loads after (math keeps KaTeX; D3 is kept by a script that names `d3`, the charts asking for none). Pruning runs in `renderReportHtml()` (`render/report-html.mjs`), the one chain a build and the preview server both show a document through (prune what an older source writes and does not use, put in every tag it needs and does not write, "Kit tags", then the icon, pictures, files and frames, then every other reference to the document's folder, `mode` "preview" or "export"), so previews and exports match and a new media block is added to it once.
- **Export.** Each remaining tag is replaced by `<script|style data-doc-kit|vendor="<id>-inline">`, the first of each asset written as the kit writes it, found on the page's parse tree as the checks and the render find it (`withKitCopies()`: a tag a comment spells stays as it is, and the element the render put in after it is inlined); a custom block's tags go the same way ("A custom block on a page"). Of `code.js` it carries the grammars the page's code uses, as it carries D3 or KaTeX only for a page that uses them (`inlineReport()`, for the asset whose `build` says `grammars`: `pageGrammars()` of `src/shared/code-grammars.mjs` read off the page's tree with its packages' scripts in, `keptGrammars()` keeping their lines; "The kit's words" says which): a page whose only code block is JSON carries 0.19 MB of it, not 1.21 MB (2026-11-17: its file 0.61 MB, not 1.62 MB; five languages 0.91 MB). The reader's 导出当前视图副本 keeps the same lines (`ui.js`, asking `DocCode.grammarsUsed` of the copy once every script is in), and a copy of an exported file keeps what it carries. D3 and KaTeX run inside a wrapper that shadows `exports`, `module`, and `define`, because some desktop HTML viewers expose CommonJS globals and D3's UMD loader would otherwise leave `globalThis.d3` undefined. KaTeX fonts are inlined as data URLs. The page's icon (`.doc-icon`) is a picture in the file already, and in a build's preview too (`embedIcon` in `emoji.mjs`). An emoji becomes the Twemoji image the preview shows, as a data: URL (from the pack in `src/shared/emoji/data/`; one the pack lacks stays the emoji). The pack holds no flag (Gallery ships none since 2026-11): a flag a document holds shows, like an emoji newer than the pack, in the system's emoji font. An icon of the picker's 图标 tab becomes its SVG inline (`currentColor`: the kit colors it by the color in its `data-icon`, light and dark, `styles/page.css`). An image of the document's folder (`assets/<file>`) becomes a data: URL, and so does every other reference to a file of the folder, at its place (a `srcset` its largest candidate, a link with a `download` name: "References to the document's folder"); the picture of an `<img>`, a copy as light as the page needs ("The pictures an export embeds"). A restrictive CSP (`connect-src 'none'`, no remote sources) is added right after the page's own `<head …>` start tag, as the parse tree finds it (any case, with attributes; not one in a comment or a script's text: `withExportPolicy()`), before the shared scripts go in; a page that writes none fails (`exportFailures`, and the export itself throws), never an export without its policy. The policy is `EXPORT_CSP` (`src/shared/export-wrap.mjs`), the one the reader's 导出当前视图副本 writes too; only `gallery export --allow-images <sources>` widens it, for a program that puts the file's pictures on another platform and links them there (`exportPolicy({ images })`: its `img-src` is `data: blob:` and those sources, every other directive the same). `imageSources()` reads them strictly, the policy's own call as well as the command's, so that nothing given there adds a directive or another kind of source: `https:`, or an https origin (`https://host`, a port allowed, `*.` as its host's first label at most), letters made lowercase and a trailing `/` dropped, 20 at most; anything else is refused with why (a space or a line break, `;`, a quote, so `'unsafe-inline'` and `'self'`, a letter outside ASCII, with the host's `xn--` form to write instead, `data:` and `blob:`, which it shows already, another scheme, `*`, a path, a user, a port out of range). Video, audio and fonts are not widened: nobody publishes those elsewhere, and they stay inside the file. The export fails if any linked script or stylesheet remains (an element of the file, as the parse tree finds it). The file carries two things besides the page, each a `<script>` of data on a line of its own before `</body>`, written last, after its pictures are made lighter and the links between an export's pages are written (`src/transfer/carry.mjs` `carriedExport`, given to `offlineReports` and `exportReports` as `carry` by the command, the workbench's 导出 and 导出文档; a render's `carry: false` for a PDF and a chart's picture, and its `rewrite` for the links, so that what it says is the file as written; src/shared/export-wrap.mjs `COPY_DATA`, `CARRIED`): the document's words as `gallery read --format text` and `--format markdown` write them (`script[data-doc-copy]`, JSON, `<` escaped: `read-view.mjs` `wholeReadings`, which `gallery read` uses for a whole document too), what the display menu's 复制为完整文本 and 复制为 Markdown put on the clipboard (`ui.js`, "Display settings" below), worked out where the file is made because the page holds neither the editor's reading of each kind of block nor jsdom, and a Sheet's other tabs, a closed disclosure and a custom block's words are read from the file there (the Component Catalog's 45 KB of 1.97 MB); and the document as its author wrote it (`script[data-doc-source]` of type `application/x-gallery-source`: JSON deflated raw, in base64), for an import of the file to give that document back rather than one worked back from the page: `{ v: 1, file, source, doc, files }`, `source` the doc.html's text as the export read it, `doc` its doc.json's `title`, `icon`, `description` and `type` and the look it shows (`documentLook`), `files` each file of its `assets/` the page names, by its name and its bytes' sha256 (the bytes themselves are the page's data: URLs, never twice; `copy` the sha256 of the lighter copy the export holds of a picture; `data` the bytes of an embed's file where `--embeds-as-links` left it out), and `file` the sha256 of the export's text without that line, so that a file changed after it was made (by hand, or by a program that put its pictures on a platform) is imported as the page it now is; and, as the value of its `data-doc-source` attribute, the HMAC-SHA256 of that base64 text by this computer's export key (`exportKey`: 32 random bytes in `export-key` of the user's data folder, `userDirectory("data")`, `GALLERY_DATA_DIR` first, made the first time it is asked for, never sent; none where that folder cannot be written, the export then signed by none). A sha256 the file says of itself proves nothing of who wrote it; the signature tells an import on the same computer that the file is one of its own exports, the one file whose scripts an import keeps (docs/workbench.md "Import and export"). It carries no custom block's code: an import never puts a block into a workspace (a block the workspace lacks shows what it holds, read-only), and what is not needed is not carried. What it carries is read at most twenty times the file and 64 MB once inflated (`carriedCeiling`: the inflating stopped there, at once), and its source at most 16 MB (`CARRIED_SOURCE_MOST`; the pictures are the page's own data: URLs, never in it), past which it is none and the file is read as its page. It carries nothing no export holds (a cropped picture's original, a `srcset`'s smaller candidates), nothing an export was asked to leave out (文件和图片除外, `--no-files`: the names alone), nor the 批注 (annotations.json, never in an export) and doc.json's `source` and `created` (where the document's material came from, when it was made). An export without these lines (one made before Gallery 0.3.1, or changed since) comes back by undoing each change the export made at its place (`src/transfer/own-report.mjs`, docs/workbench.md "Import and export"), an exported file coming back as the document its author wrote (a picture the export made lighter comes back as that copy, a WebP), the whole page as written: its inline copies, its policy, the two lines it carries and the browser tab's icon go (each with the line break the export put with it), an icon's picture is its emoji again (a glyph's SVG goes), a Sheet tab's emoji picture goes, and each data: URL at a place an export holds a file (`referencesWhere()` in `asset-references.mjs`, the places this export embeds at, the media blocks' and the icons' among them) is a file of the new document's folder again, its address written there (`addressEdits()`: an attribute, a `srcset` candidate, a `url()` in a style). A change to what an export does to the markup is one to undo there too (`tests/unit/roundtrip.mjs` exports a document of every such thing and imports it three ways: as exported, changed after, and with the two lines gone). What that way cannot know stays as it falls: a file's own name (an import names each as an upload is, unless the export carried it), a `srcset`'s smaller candidates, a cropped picture's original (`data-original`: the import leaves it out, `data-crop` with it).
- **Preview index.** The built previews are the workspace's own (`.gallery/built/<slug>.html`, nothing serves them: the export builds through them; only they are pruned of documents that are gone, never `exports/`, whose files are the user's), and `.gallery/built/reports.json` merges the previous manifest with the documents just built and keeps entries whose preview file exists.
- **The pictures an export embeds.** The copy in the file, never the workspace's own (`src/shared/export-pictures.mjs`; feedback of 2026-11-17): the picture of each `<img>` of the page (none a template holds; a CSS background, a `srcset` and a script's data keep their pixels: a background shows at its own size, and a copy would change it) that is a PNG, a WebP or a JPEG over 16 KB and no animation (an animated PNG or WebP; a GIF, an SVG, an AVIF and an icon file as they are) is drawn on a canvas at most twice the reading column wide (`pictureCeiling`: 1424px, 1296 at 偏窄, 1552 at 偏宽, from the page's `data-doc-measure`; at 全宽 by the document's look, `data-doc-width="full"`, no column, and its width kept) and written as a WebP of quality 0.9, its transparency kept; a lossy one (a JPEG, a lossy WebP) only when it is wider than that (written again at its own width it would lose more for little: so a copy is never copied again); never enlarged; the copy kept only when it is a tenth lighter at least (`lighter`). A WebP holds 16383 pixels a side at most, and Chrome's encoder, given a larger canvas, crops it and says nothing (a 1000×20000 full-page screenshot lost its last 3617 rows): a picture longer than that is drawn smaller still, both sides within it and its shape kept (that screenshot 819×16383; at 全宽 a strip 20000px wide 16383px), and a copy whose size, decoded, is not the size drawn is none. The page shows no picture wider than its column, and twice that is what a screen of two pixels to a point shows sharp; a reader who turns 全宽 on in the file, or opens a picture large (放大, never past its own size), sees it at most 1424px wide, which a wider original would have filled. The pixels are a browser's (`encodePicture`, self-contained): the reader's own for 导出当前视图副本 (`ui.js`, as it embeds each picture), and for the command's and the workbench's exports the user's Chrome or Edge (`src/transfer/browser.mjs` `encodePictures`, a blank page of the shared browser, three at once), which `offlineReports({ pictures })` and `exportReports(…, { pictures })` are given by what exports (`src/cli/export.mjs`, `src/server/export.mjs`, the transfer route): the render starts no browser, and `smallerPictures()` (`src/render/export-pictures.mjs`) finds the `<img>`s' data: URLs in the file it made, asks each picture once and writes each lighter copy in its `src`. A picture the browser cannot read (a PNG whose data is broken, one too large for its memory) is tried on its own: it stays as it is, the others' copies go in, and the command says so on the same line (`; 1 embedded as it is: the browser could not read it (InvalidStateError: …)`), as the reader's 导出当前视图副本 tries each on its own. Without a browser the file keeps its pictures as they are, and `gallery export` says so under its `Exported` line (`  pictures: 8 embedded as they are (1.8 MB): no Chrome or Edge on this machine to make them lighter`), as it says what it made (`  pictures: 8 of 8 made lighter for the page (at most 1424px wide, as WebP), 1.8 MB → 0.9 MB; --original-images keeps them as they are`); `--original-images` embeds every picture as it is. `gallery check`'s and `gallery export`'s note on a heavy picture (`largePictureNotes`, "Heavy pictures") weighs it as this export embeds it: a picture of an `<img>` the export makes a copy of gets none, but only where it makes one: without Chrome or Edge on the machine (`pictureCopies`, `src/transfer/browser.mjs`, which the command gives `reportNotes(…, { copies })`), or with `--original-images`, it is weighed as it is, its note saying why (`; no Chrome or Edge on this machine makes the export's copy lighter: copy it in at the size the page needs`). A PDF takes its pictures as they are (`{ pictures: false }`: the PDF writer encodes each again, a JPEG as it is and any other losslessly, and a WebP copy made a PDF of eight screenshots 4.35 MB where their PNGs made 1.25 MB), and so do a Markdown export (the files themselves) and the pictures of its charts. Chosen by looking (2026-11-17: eight 2000px screenshots of the product, light and dark, and photographs, side by side at the page's size and at their own, magnified): WebP 0.9 at 1424px kept a screenshot's text as sharp as its PNG at the page's size, at 37–49% of its bytes (eight screenshots 1.8 MB → 0.9 MB, their document's file 2.91 MB → 1.68 MB, its export 2.4 s → 5.0 s with the browser's start); 0.8 blurred colored code at its own size a little, and a lossy WebP's colors are a quarter as fine as its light (4:2:0), which only a magnified look shows; the browser's lossless WebP and PNG were heavier than the PNGs they came from (a screenshot drawn smaller gains in-between pixels).
- **Live preview.** Editor modules and their bundled emoji data keep one origin-root URL (`/__gallery/edit/v/<stamp>/src/…`) across all document storage directories; only document requests carry the owner prefix, so changing projects reuses the browser’s module cache. The global custom-block files likewise keep their canonical `/__gallery/components/…` URLs in tags and generated metadata, avoiding a project-prefixed request and redirect before the cached asset. Project routing reads the registration map once per request (or one batch of runtime subscriptions), with no registry cache between requests: removing a registration takes effect on the next request even if its runtime is already open. Each storage runtime caches the final owner-qualified preview HTML, including the address rewrite, under the existing source/stamp key. The preview server's routes are one module, `src/server/routes.mjs`, which two hosts serve: under `npm run dev:local` (and a gallery command's server in a source checkout) the `reportPreview()` plugin in `src/vite.config.ts` (`src/server/index.mjs`) puts them ahead of Vite's own handling (Vite for the workbench's modules, their hot reload, and `public/`), its watcher (chokidar) telling them of changes; in the release, the package's own server (`src/server/production.mjs`: node:http on 127.0.0.1, no Vite) puts them ahead of its static files (`static.mjs`: the built workbench, `/` never kept and its `assets/` kept for good; the kit's and vendors' files by their path, `no-cache`; a path never out of its folder) and watches the workspace with `fs.watch` (`watch.mjs`: one recursive watch on Windows and macOS, one a folder on Linux, the events made chokidar's). Either refuses a request naming another host than `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` (403), first. The routes answer `/generated/<slug>.html` themselves: they read the document's `doc.html` (`<workspace>/<slug>/`, or `report.html` in one made before Gallery 0.1; the 写作偏好 page's in `.gallery/pages/<slug>/`) on every request, shows it through the build's own chain (`renderReportHtml()`: the same kit tags pruned and put in, `ui.js` among them, the page's icon, pictures, files and frames, and every other reference to the document's folder, each from the server's routes), hides the display settings' button from the first paint (a style in the head, `data-doc-dev`: the workbench has its own theme and export, which still go through `ui.js`), serves the page's icon as a picture from the start (`embedIcon`; an image from `/__gallery/edit/asset/<slug>/assets/<file>?v=<stamp>`, the value in `data-ge-icon` for the editor), and injects a small inline script that opens a server-sent-events connection to `/__gallery/events?slug=<slug>` and then removes its own element, so the browser "导出当前视图副本" control (view copy) never copies it. Besides its document's writes (`report <version>`) the stream says `notes <version>` (its 批注 changed), `changes <stamp>` (the review of the Agent's changes to it started, grew, ended, was kept or reverted: the editor reads it again, [editor.md](editor.md) "Agent's changes"), `look <word>` (its `doc.json` or the workspace's `gallery.json` was written: the look the document shows now, `font=serif&size=large` or nothing, which the page puts on at once, "Design defaults", a document's look) and `asset <new|now> <file>=<stamp>,…` (a file of its folder's `assets/` as it is now: `new` for one written, told by `src/server/asset-news.mjs` once it is whole and quiet, 250ms with no change and, a PNG, a JPEG, a GIF, a WebP or an SVG, its own format saying it ends there, one its format check does not see whole told as it is once quiet for 10s (a phone's motion photo, a JPEG with a video after its end of image, shows as a reload shows it; one really cut short is no picture the page swaps in, as it loads and decodes each first), the stamp told last not told again, a file taken away not told and forgotten; `now` as the page connects, for every file of its folder told so while this server ran (what was told is kept per document, no file looked at), which catches up a page served from what was kept a moment before a write, or hidden while it came; a file written while no server ran shows new once the page is loaded anew; the live script is served with the stamps the page names its files by, read off the page as rendered (`render/media-ref.mjs` `servedStamps`), keeps them in `window.__galleryAssets` and tells the page of the files whose stamp moved, which swaps the addresses that name them: [editor.md](editor.md) "A file of the folder written again"), for none of which a page is shown again; and `data <kind>` (what the workspace holds changed, a kind of what a block reads of it: [workbench.md](workbench.md) "The workspace's facts"), which the page hands to the kit (`doc-workspace-change`) and which every page, a custom page's too, hears. A message the script does not know changes nothing (until 2026-11-08 a document's page took any such message for a change of its own and reloaded); one its stream says after it was lost and came back, each kind of the workspace's facts is told as changed. The workbench listens on the same stream with `?workspace` and hears `workspace <stamp>` alone, which no page hears: the outline written by the server, a document's folder or source file made or taken away, a `doc.json` changed, told once for a burst of them (workbench.md "One source"); registry changes and writes invalidate the shared process’s cached project snapshots directly. It hears `updated <key>` there too, which no page hears either: a page someone else wrote (`src/server/updates.mjs`), `<key>` as the sidebar names it (a document's slug; `~ws-<id>/<slug>` for a document of another registered workspace, whose runtime hands it to the home's stream, `onUpdated`, `src/server/projects.mjs`; `@<slug>` for a custom page, the default workspace's), told once a write as the preview takes it (the announcement's `tell` below: never half written, after its quiet moment; a custom page's after the 120ms of its burst). A write is someone else's when the file's text changed since the server last heard of it (`createUpdates`: a hash of each document's `doc.html` and each custom page's `page.html`, seeded as the server starts with what `edits.takeAll` took of each, its `taken`, and with each page's file) and the reader did not write it: the edit service's own last write of that text says whose it was (`writerOf`: `reader` for a commit of any page, a rename, an icon, a hand-in the reader asked for; a text it did not write, or wrote for the Agent, `gallery history`'s restore, `gallery revert`, an Agent's hand-in, is the Agent's), and a custom page's the page service's (`pageWriterOf`, `src/editor/page-service.mjs`: a gesture's `page` and Gallery's own `gallery` the reader's side; `gallery page add`'s `agent` and a write that is not the service's the Agent's). `doc.json` or `page.json` written alone, the same text told again, and a page made since the start (its first write: `gallery new`, 新建, an import, a copy, one back from the trash) are none, the next write of it by someone else is one; one gone is forgotten. The workbench marks the page ([workbench.md](workbench.md) "Updated elsewhere"); one said while its stream was down is not said again. A page made is kept (`src/server/page-cache.mjs`, the last 20) and served again for the same text (the workbench mounting a document's frame twice, a document opened again, a page reloading: a few milliseconds instead of 20 to 80), as it is made of nothing but that text and its key: read or presented (`?present`), the stamps of the editor's modules and of each kit file it loads, its folder's (the times of `<workspace>/<slug>/` and its `assets/`: a file put in or taken out shows at once; one written again in place once the watcher tells of it, which drops the document's pages), the stamp of the catalog of custom blocks as the server holds it ("The catalog of custom blocks" below), and the look the document shows (its `doc.json`'s and `gallery.json`'s, read as the page is asked for). A stamp (`src/server/stamps.mjs`) is read from the files themselves as the page is served (each one's modification time and size, never what the watcher said, which comes a moment late) and names what the page loads: the editor under `/__gallery/edit/v/<stamp>/src/editor/page/editor.mjs` (every module it imports under the same stamp) and the kit's tags as `/kit/<file>?v=<stamp>` (`renderReportHtml`'s `kit`, the tags it puts in and those a source writes as the kit writes them), all answered `Cache-Control: max-age=31536000, immutable`: a browser fetches and compiles the 231 modules and the kit once, and a page opened later asks for none of them; a file saved makes a new stamp, so the next page asks for it again. By their paths alone the modules are `no-store` and the kit is the host's (Vite's `public/`, the release's `kit/` and `vendor/`; revalidated). In the release the stamps of the modules and the kit's files are their contents' hashes, which the release build writes into `release.json`, not their times (npm gives every file of a package one time: after an upgrade a module of the same size would keep its stamp, and a browser its old copy); its files never change while it runs, and the server stops when its package is replaced ([internals.md](internals.md), "The npm package"). Vite's HMR client is deliberately not used: it reloads the page whenever the dev server comes back after a disconnect, while `EventSource` reconnects quietly and the page reloads only on an explicit message. It runs the fast checks only (`quickFailures()`: structure, the components' markup, the shared tags, removed calls, what an export needs; not TypeScript or the charts' own validation) and, when one fails, puts a note at the bottom left of the page listing them (`preview-checks.mjs`, `data-doc-dev`: no export or edit takes it); a document that fails them still previews. Showing a document never writes it: the page is served its natural HTML made the kit's in memory (`edits.read()`: the version as pages see it, of that text), and the page opens on that version. A save to `report.html` or `report.json` notifies only the pages showing that document, with that version (an Agent's natural file is no one else's write to a page that shows it, so the page does not reload again and again), once the preview takes the file: never while an Agent has it half written between two edits of one change (a disclosure opened and not closed yet, a Sheet area's tabs before their panels), and once it has not changed for 1.5s, at once for the edit service's own writes (`src/server/announce.mjs`, which looks at a held file again every second at least); a page's load waits for a half-written file too (10s at most) and is then served what the preview last took of it, and no commit is applied to one (docs/editor.md, "Writing"). A page already showing that version (the one that just saved and reloaded) ignores it, so a save never reloads its page twice; a change under `src/render/kit/` or `src/shared/components/` rewrites `public/kit` with fresh esbuild bundles and notifies every page (in a source checkout: the release's kit is built once), and a change of the catalog notifies every page with its stamp, which each also hears as it connects. `/generated/reports.json` is also answered live, so new documents appear on the index after a refresh. With `?present&version=<id>&key=<key>` (the workbench's 历史版本, [workbench.md](workbench.md) "History") the document route serves a recent version of the document instead of its file (`versionPage`): the key the edit service's list of versions gave opens it (`versionShown`, [editor.md](editor.md) "Recent versions"; another key, or a name that is no version's: 404), a request another site started is refused (`Sec-Fetch-Site` cross-site or same-site: 403; one with none, `same-origin` or `none` is answered), and the page is the presented one (no editor, check notes or display button, framed by Gallery's own pages alone), made each time and kept by no cache (a version's page would push a document's out of it), frozen: no live script (it hears nothing; a block's `context.fetch` is answered as on any page this server serves), and a script first in its head, before the kit's tags (`versionScript`), reads the browser's storage as it is but keeps what the page writes there in the page's memory (the kit's `state.js`: a tab chosen or a section folded on an old version is no reading state of the document's), takes a block's `workspace.open` (nothing done) and lets a link go nowhere but a web page of another site, which opens in a tab of its own (a document's link would bring its page, the editor on it, into the dialog); an anchor and a download stay the kit's and the browser's. `context.edit`, a table view's changes and `workspace.setTodo` write nothing there: no editor says the page takes edits or answers them. A version that changed something of the one kept before it is rendered from its text with that marked in it (`versionShown`'s `marked`), and its head loads one module of the editor's under the editor's stamp, `editor/page/changes/version.mjs`, which draws the marks once the kit says the page is drawn (`data-doc-ready`), unless the address says `marks=off` (workbench.md "History"); it is none of the editor and writes nothing either. `predev:local` (`src/cli/build-kit.mjs`) writes `public/kit` and `public/vendor`, and `writeSharedAssets()` overwrites those files in place so a CLI build during a live session never leaves the server without them.
- **A document on a command line** (`documentNamed()` in `src/cli/arguments.mjs`, for `gallery check`, `export`, `qa`, `open`, `move`, `set`, `list --tree` and the documents `gallery new --under` and `--after` name) is named by its slug, by its link (`周报-k3xv9q2a`, what stands after the `#` of an address, or its page id alone) or by a whole address, which is what a person hands an Agent: any form `pageAddress` reads, on any port of this computer, with a block or not, read by the list of folders alone, for the words in a link are a label (`namedPlace` in `src/shared/page-address.mjs`; workbench.md "A document's link", which says what that means for the same folder name in another workspace). `/#<link>` reads as well when Git Bash has written a path of its own before it (`C:/Program Files/Git/#<link>`), as it does with any word that starts with `/`. `gallery open` opens at the block an address names; the others take the document. A slug no document holds goes on to the command, which says so; a document in the trash is said with its title, and what names no document is a mistake (exit 2), a folder of a name no slug can be staying no command's.
- **`gallery list [<words>...]`** (`src/cli/list.mjs`) lists the documents for an Agent to find the one the user means by its title, its topic or an address: first the workspace and the writing preferences (`startLines()`, as `gallery new`), then each document (a folder named by a slug with its `doc.html`, or `report.html` in an older one; the writing preferences' page, in `.gallery/pages/`, left out) as its slug and title, its description, where it is in the sidebar's tree when it is under another (`位置：project (项目) › notes (排序笔记)`, the documents above it from the top: `placeLine` in `src/cli/tree.mjs`), its address (`地址：http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#<link>` while the server answers as its announcement says, as `page list` gives it, else the path `/#<link>`, none needing to run; a workspace other than home with `~<its id>/` before the link, as `gallery open` gives it, so that the address opens that workspace's document, the JSON's `address` too), its last edit (its file's time, `上次编辑：2026-10-23 14:05`) and its file's absolute path, the one edited last first. One argument that is a document's link or whole address lists that document, for an Agent handed an address by the user; a folder name stays a word like any. When words find exactly one document (and no web link), the last line is the address to give the user (`handOverLine()`: `交给用户（交稿时给；用 Chrome 或 Edge 打开）：` and the workbench's whole address as `gallery open` gives it while a server runs, else a `提示：` that none runs and that `gallery open <slug> --no-browser`, run at the handoff, prints the `交给用户` line): a revision gives the address in its handoff (SKILL.md's Revising a document and Handoff), with no `gallery open` first and no address sent before the change; until 2026-11-13 the label said to give it now and the hint to start the server now, against the skill. One argument that finds nothing and names a document of the trash, by any of these, says so with its title, as the other commands do. With words (split at spaces), only those whose title, description or slug holds at least one of them, upper and lower case alike and anywhere in them (Chinese is written without spaces), the best first: more of the words, then more of them in the title, then in the slug, then the last edited (`rankReports()` in `src/shared/search.mjs`); after them the documents whose text holds any of them (their `doc.html` read as the workbench's 搜索文档 reads a document, its blocks' lines: `reportLines` and `findIn`), more of the words first, then more places, each with its first place (`正文：` and a snippet around the words); none found is said, with how many there are. `gallery list --tree` prints the documents as the sidebar shows them instead (`readTree()` in `src/cli/tree.mjs`: the outline as the workbench reads it), in its order, two spaces a level, a line each: its slug, its emoji when its icon is one, its title, and `（说明：…；上次编辑：…）`; `gallery list --tree <document>` that document and those under it, after its `位置：` line (a document not in the tree exit 1, one of the trash exit 2, more than one named exit 2). `gallery list [<words>...] --all` searches every workspace of the machine's registry instead (`listAll`; the current one first and always, registered or not), by each document's title, description and slug alone (`rankReports`, not its text; no words: every document, the one edited last first), each result after the line `工作区：<path>` of its workspace, which is what `--workspace` takes to work on it there, then as a plain list's (slug and title, `说明：`, `位置：` from that workspace's own tree, `地址：`, `上次编辑：`, its file), and a last line on passing that workspace as `--workspace`; a registered workspace no longer there is named on a line of its own and not searched. It only reads: no `startLines()` (no 写作偏好 made in the current workspace), and each workspace is read by the functions that take its root (`documentsOf(root)`, `documentLinks(root)`, `readTree(root)`), so nothing is written in any of them (`tests/unit/workspace-registry.mjs` compares another workspace's files before and after); `--all` with `--tree` exit 2.
- **`gallery workspaces`** (`src/cli/workspaces.mjs`) lists the machine's registry of workspaces (`src/shared/workspace-registry.mjs`: every workspace a command other than `stop` and `skill` found, `gallery init` made or a server started in, but one in the system's temporary folder, in `workspaces.json` of the user's data folder, `GALLERY_DATA_DIR` first; docs/internals.md), the current one first (`*`, registered as it is listed), then by the last edit of their documents, those no longer there (`不在了`: the folder or its `gallery.json` gone; kept, for a drive comes back) last: each by its name (`src/shared/workspace-name.mjs`: `gallery.json`'s `name`, else its folder's, climbing past the generic names, `没有起名` said; the folder above in `（上一级：…）` when two are called alike, and the workspace's own path in `（…）` where that is alike too, `workspaceLabels`), its `ws-` id, `路径：`, `文档：` (how many, and the last edit) and `服务：` while a server runs (its announcement, `readAnnouncement`). `--scan [<folder>]` registers what `scanForWorkspaces` finds in a folder (the Documents folder by default, 6 levels down, no `node_modules`, `.git`, dot folder, recycle bin (`$RECYCLE.BIN`, `SCAN_SKIPS`) or folder in a workspace), each said `新登记` or `已登记`; `--name "<name>"` writes the current workspace's name into its `gallery.json` (`writeWorkspaceName`: in place of the name it had, else right after the mark, all else and the file's own layout kept; the workspace made first when it is not there; a name empty, over 80 characters or of more than one line exit 2); `--forget <path or ws- id>` takes entries off the registry, nothing on disk changed (none named exit 1). Two of these at once exit 2. `gallery init <dir> --name "<name>"` names a workspace as it makes it (`名字：<name>`).
- **`gallery page new|list|open|add`** (`src/cli/page.mjs`): the custom pages ("A custom page"; `add` under **`gallery page add`** there). `new` refuses the home page while it is not made yet, as it does once it is there (`homeNotMade`; not where the user took it away, or `GALLERY_NO_DEFAULT_HOME` is set, which `new` makes when asked), and ends, with a server running, with the 交给用户 line `gallery open` has (the page's whole address, after a `下一步：` line that says to send it at once and, at the end, where the page is in the sidebar); without one, its `下一步：` line starts with running `gallery page open <slug> --no-browser` and sending the address that prints, before any card, so that the address comes first either way (SKILL.md's custom pages, step 2). `list` gives each page's whole address then (the path after the port without a server). Both take a server for running only when it answers on its port as the one its announcement names (`runningPort`: `findPreviewServer`'s `answering`, as `gallery open` asks; a server stopped without closing leaves an announcement whose process's number may be another program's by now, whose address would be a dead one); and in a workspace with no page and no home yet says only that the home page is not made, in place of "No custom pages yet"; `open`, `add`, `try` and `check` of `home` say the same alone (`missingPageSaid`) in place of "No custom page home".
- **`gallery page list --json`, `set`, `move`, `card` and `reset-cards`** (`src/cli/page.mjs`, `src/cli/page-manage.mjs`; since 2026-11-11): what the sidebar's menus and a page's own gestures do, for an Agent the user asks, each calling the function the interface calls. `list` leaves out a page in the trash (the workbench service's `trash` moves its folder into `.gallery/trash/<id>/page`, so `listPages` no longer finds it) and with `--json` prints one object, `{ workspace, server: { port } | null, pages: [{ slug, title, icon, defaultCards, address, file }], homeNotMade }`, its fields only ever added (the usage says them). `set` writes `page.json`'s title and icon with `setPageMeta` (`src/shared/pages.mjs`; `renamePage`, which the workbench's rename calls, is its title alone): an emoji or an icon of the 图标 tab, not a picture, which the sidebar would look for in a document's folder; `page.html`, whose `<title>` is the page's own, is left alone and the command says so, and the server's watcher of `page.json` tells an open workbench. `move` puts the page's row right after another page's in the sidebar head, or before the first page's with `--first` (新建 and 搜索 stay where they are), through the workbench service's `head` (the running server's, as `tree.mjs` `throughServer` reaches it, else `createWorkspaceService(...).run("head")` here): the outline file's `head` under its lock. `card` names a card by its `data-card-id` (`cardsOf`; a card with none is named by the id the page gives it) and sends the page service the op its own gesture would, held to the card's base: `place` for `--move` and `--size` (one write, the cards it meets pushed down, never one up; a size the block does not give is the nearest it does, said), `settings` for `--set` (typed by `settingsGiven`, as `add --set`; the place is written first so that a refusal of the place leaves the settings alone), `remove` for `--remove` (the card's data goes with it: `page.html` as it was is in `.gallery/state/pages/<slug>/`); a card another writer changed meanwhile (`refused: "changed"`) is read again, three tries. `reset-cards` sends `restore-defaults` and says the cards it put back and the default blocks it could not. The ops go through `pageServiceOp` (shared with `add`): the running server's queue, where an open page shows the change at once, else `createPageService(...).apply` here with the catalog read again each time the service asks, so that a block of Gallery's own it puts in is there; they carry `agent: true`, so `gallery page check` does not mark them the user's.
- **The usage by subcommand** (`src/cli/usage-sections.mjs`): the usage of `gallery block` (15,000 characters) and `gallery page` (10,000) is kept as an `intro` and a list of `{ name, summary, usage, text }`, one for each subcommand. `USAGE.block` and `USAGE.page` stay the whole of it (`wholeUsage`: the usage lines of every subcommand, the intro, every text), which a mistake in a command line takes its first line from and `tests/unit/skill-docs.mjs` holds the documents' commands to. `gallery help block` (`helpText` of `usage.mjs`, called by the dispatcher's `readCommandLine`) prints the intro and one short line for each subcommand, `gallery help block pack` and `gallery block pack --help` (and `gallery component …`) that one in full with a last line saying where the rest is, a subcommand that is none a mistake that lists them (exit 2; `--help` of one that is none says the command's). A command with no sections (every other) is printed whole, whatever follows its name (`gallery help trash list`). A mistake in a command line of `try`, `pack`, `menu`, `net`, `set`, `move`, `card` or `reset-cards` is said with that subcommand's usage lines (`TRY_SYNOPSIS`, `PACK_SYNOPSIS`, `PAGE_TRY_SYNOPSIS`, `synopsisOf`). `usage-sections.mjs` imports nothing, as `usage.mjs`, so that help reads no more than those.
- **`gallery new <slug> "<title>" ["<description>"]`** (`src/cli/new.mjs`, `createReportFromStarter()` in `reports.mjs`) makes `<workspace>/<slug>/` from the starter (`starter/`): the title in the page's `<title>` and h1, its section for the Agent to replace (`data-doc-placeholder`, which `gallery check` fails while it remains), no script; the title and the description (`新文档` when none is given; `New document` on a system whose language is not Chinese) in `doc.json`, whose `type` is `report`, free text the Agent may change. The starter's `<html lang>` and the words of its section (`文档正文` / `用文档内容替换这一节。`, `Document body` / `Replace this section with the document's content.`: the marks `__REPORT_HEADING__` and `__REPORT_NOTE__` of `starter/doc.html`, filled by `starterPage()` from a table of its own) follow **the file language** (`src/shared/file-language.mjs`): the words Gallery itself writes into files are in the system's language at the moment they are written (Chinese when it is Chinese, English for any other; the locale Node gives, else `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES`, `LANGUAGE`, `LANG`), `GALLERY_LANGUAGE=zh|en` over it. The server cannot know the browser's choice, which the UI language is (kept in the browser), so only the system's is there for a file written by a command or a server start; where the workbench knows the reader's language it says it with the request (`language`: a new document's lang, a copy's suffix, an import's description) and that wins. Nothing finds the section by its words: `data-doc-placeholder` and `<!-- Starter content:` are, in either language, and `gallery check` fails while it remains. What is written stays as written: no workspace is rewritten when the system's language changes (a default home page titled 首页 stays so on an English system), the default 写作偏好 is the user's own content, and the fixed words a command prints for an Agent (`交给用户：`, `下一步：`, `问题：`) are Chinese whatever the language. The review of the Agent's changes takes that starter at once, on disk (`noteNew()` in `src/cli/hand-in.mjs`, the edit service's `noteTaken`), so that whichever server takes the Agent's first write of the document (one started only now, or one no page made look at it before) takes it against the starter: the starter filled, no change to review, and every write after it reviewed (editor.md "Agent's changes"). With `--under <document>`, `--after <document>` or `--first` it goes there in the sidebar's tree (`moveInTree` in `src/cli/tree.mjs`, as `gallery move` moves one: under it, last; right after it, at its level; first), each document named as every command names one and checked before anything is made (one not in the tree, the 写作偏好 page, an `--after` at another level than `--under`'s: exit 1, said with `Nothing made.`; one of the trash exit 2), said right after `Created …` (`Placed it under project (项目), right after weekly (检索排序周报).`; a place refused meanwhile leaves it last at the top level, said, exit 1); with neither, last at the top level, as the workbench's list shows a document it does not know yet. Then it says what an Agent reads before it writes, so that it need look for none of it: the workspace it wrote into (`工作区：…`, its absolute path), and where the user's writing preferences are, to read first (`写作偏好：以下是启用的文档，按标题和内容选择本次任务适用的规则（gallery preferences list）。`, then a line for each enabled file in their order, `  <name>：<its path>`; `写作偏好：全部已停用。按本次用户要求写作。` when every one is off): `startLines()` in `src/cli/report-start.mjs`, which reads them through `listPreferenceDocuments()` (`src/shared/preference-documents.mjs`), so that the first time they are read in a new workspace `<home>/preferences/通用写作.md` is made from Gallery's short generic default (`src/shared/writing-preferences.md`; an older workspace's `<home>/preferences.md` is moved to `<home>/preferences/项目报告写作.md` instead) and never written over after (`gallery list`, which only reads, says that it made it: `提示：preferences/通用写作.md was made now from Gallery defaults. …`); then the next steps, the first of them, when the last line gives the address, to send it to the user now, in a message of its own (`下一步：send the user http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#<link> now, in a message of its own, so that they can open the document while you work; then replace the starter section with the document, as ordinary HTML (gallery check fails while the starter section remains). The write that replaces the starter is not marked; every write after it is shown to the user as your changes (「Agent 已修改」).`: `nextLine()` and `sendFirst()` in `src/cli/server-process.mjs`, the address written out in the step, where a step naming "the address on the last line" had the Sonnet runs of the skill test of 2026-11-05 read a file first and send it a minute and more later; without an address, `下一步：replace the starter section …`; what follows the address starts with what to write and names no reference, SKILL.md's step 4 naming them, as a step starting "read references/…" had the Sonnet write runs read at once and send the address only in their handoff, the skill test of 2026-10-30; only the address is asked for first, and no order of writing: the line once had a skeleton first and a section at a time, so that the user watched the document grow (the skill test of 2026-10-29), and the user took it out, as an order that protects nothing: one write or several is the Agent's, and so is the reading before it, which "before any other step" had held back; since 2026-11-13 the line also says that the write that replaces the starter is not marked and every write after it is shown as the Agent's changes, a first writing that lasted over several writes being gone: editor.md "Agent's changes"; it asked for the first version in one write too, until the review of 2026-11-13 took that out as an order of writing), then `gallery qa`, which exports first; and last it gives the workspace's server's address as `gallery open` does (`previewOfNew()` in `src/cli/server-process.mjs`, the one implementation both use: the workspace's server found or started, running on after the command), its last three lines `gallery open`'s (Agents left to run it themselves ran it late, or gave the address only at the end), the last the address alone, `交给用户（现在就把这个地址给用户；用 Chrome 或 Edge 打开）：http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#<link>`, nothing after it. A sentence to send "as it is" in its place (2026-10-26 to 10-28) was sent at the end, minutes late, by all six Agents of the skill test of 2026-10-28: it read as words for the handoff; the address alone, with the `下一步：` line starting with sending it, is what Agents sent at once. It opens no browser, and says nothing of one: the Agent sends the user the address (its `下一步：` line), and the workbench marks a page an Agent changed that the reader is not looking at with a blue dot (workbench.md), so no tab need open by itself; a tab of the blank document opened the moment an Agent made it, before a word was written, which the user did not want (2026-11-13). `--open` opens the default browser at the document before the server's lines, as `gallery open` does (`openAsked()` in `src/cli/arguments.mjs`, `browserLine()` in `src/cli/server-process.mjs`, its line `open`'s: `Opened it in the default browser (…)` or `Not opened in a browser: <why>.`, `GALLERY_NO_BROWSER=1` the tests' why); `--no-browser` is still taken (the skills of before pass it) and does nothing more, and with `--open` is a mistake of the command line (exit 2, nothing made). Until 2026-11-13 it opened the browser unless `--no-browser`, so that the user saw the document written whether or not the Agent sent the address (the Sonnet write runs of the skill test of 2026-10-30 sent it only in their handoff, 4 times of 6). `--no-preview`, or `GALLERY_NO_PREVIEW=1` (the tests', and scripts that make documents: no server left behind), leaves the server alone, said with how to start it (`提示：The server was not started (--no-preview or GALLERY_NO_PREVIEW): gallery open <slug> --no-browser starts it and prints the address to give the user.`: with `--no-browser`, as every step the commands give an Agent that runs `gallery open` or `gallery page open`, so that no step of the Agent's opens a tab of the user's); the next step after hand-in is a `提示：` line too (`when it is written, run gallery qa <slug> (it exports first)`, and `gallery import`'s alike; until 2026-11-13 they named `gallery export` first, one command more for what qa does itself); in a workspace with no name of its own, but the default one, a line after the 写作偏好 line says how to name it after its project (`提示：这个工作区还没有名字（别处按文件夹叫它"…"）；…gallery workspaces --name "<项目名>"…`, `nameHint()` in `src/cli/workspaces.mjs`); a server that cannot be started (no port free, the workspace's server busy, a server that stopped) is said on a `问题：` line with why and how to start it once that is fixed (`  When that is fixed, gallery open <slug> --no-browser starts it …`, the message before it saying to run `gallery open <slug> --no-browser` where it said to run the command again: `serverFor()`'s `again`, the command as it was run, `--no-browser` kept, only after `gallery open` and `gallery page open`), and the document is made all the same (exit 0). A document that exists is named by its path (`Document already exists: <abs>`, exit 1), as is the 写作偏好 page's slug; a custom page's is refused (documents and custom pages share one set of slugs: "A custom page"), `pages` too, a name the workspace keeps; `exports` is no slug (exit 2). A slug of the trash is refused too, a document's or a page's, for what is there goes by it (workbench.md "A document's link": `"<slug>" is the slug of something in the trash: choose another slug.`, exit 1).
- **`gallery import <file> [<slug> ["<title>"]]`** (`src/cli/import.mjs`) makes documents of a file by the workbench's import (`src/transfer/import.mjs`: no second converter), in its own process, written as the workbench writes them (`writeImportedPages()` in `src/transfer/pages.mjs`: `createReportFromStarter()` with its blank page, then the page's blocks written in, `fillReport`, and its kit tags, its files into `assets/`, its icon into `doc.json`, then placed in the outline, as `gallery move` places one: through the workspace's running server when there is one, so that an open workbench shows them nested at once). A Markdown document (`.md`, `.markdown`, `.mdx`, `.txt`, `.text`) is read with the pictures it links to beside it (`importMarkdown`); every other kind the workbench's 导入 takes (`.html` `.htm` `.xhtml`, `.csv` `.tsv`, `.docx`, `.pdf`, `.epub`, `.zip`) as an upload is (`importFiles`): a file of several pages (a zip, a Notion export) one document a page, nested in the outline as they were, the slug and title given naming the first; another kind is a usage failure, exit 2. Its title is the argument's, else the front matter's, else its first `#` heading's (wherever it stands), else the file's name; its slug the argument's (refused as `gallery new` refuses one, exit 1), else made of the title's Latin words or the file name's (lowercase kebab-case, a number on when it is taken or the workspace holds it for a document; `doc-<a new page id>`, as the workbench names a new document (`newDocSlug`), when neither has a word of three letters, a Chinese title or one whose Latin is a version alone); `导入自 <file>` its description (one of Gallery's own documents, an export of one: the description its export carried, docs/workbench.md "Import and export"). The pictures it links to by relative addresses are found on disk from its folder and copied into `assets/` as uploads are named. Of one of Gallery's own documents it says how it came back, under its title: `As written: …` (the export carried its doc.html and was not changed since) or `Worked back from the page: …` (changed since, made before Gallery 0.3.1, a `report.html`, or what it carries unreadable), and, but for an export this computer made and signed, `Made inert: …` with how many places that would run it took out (or that nothing in it would run). A Markdown file's HTML blocks kept as written are made inert as another's page is (docs/workbench.md "Import and export"), and how many places it took out is said (`Made inert: …`). The file is converted in the conversion worker (`src/transfer/jobs.mjs`), its heap held, as the workbench converts one. It says what it made (sections, headings, paragraphs, lists, tables, formulas…, the HTML blocks kept as written counted of their own), what it could not bring as the file has it, each at its line (a picture not found, a formula KaTeX cannot draw, a display formula not closed before an empty line, a quote's list or code kept out of it, a link to a file or a place in the document, the front matter's title taking a `#` heading's place, an HTML block left out, a picture in a heading, a mermaid diagram that is no chain of steps, a footnote with no definition), the text of `doc.html` the preview leaves read-only (`readOnlyTexts`, as `gallery check` says it: an HTML block kept as written that is no block of the kit's), then gallery new's lines (`src/cli/report-start.mjs`), the next steps (the first, as gallery new's, to send the user the address now) and, as gallery new, the server's address to give the user alone on the last line, at the first document (`--no-preview` or `GALLERY_NO_PREVIEW=1` leaving the server alone), no browser opened but with `--open`, as gallery new opens it (`gallery duplicate` the same). Several documents made are listed nested, each with its slug, title, file and what it holds, and what any could not bring is said with its slug.
- **`gallery move <document> (--under <document> | --top) [--after <document> | --first]`** (`src/cli/move.mjs`, its work in `src/cli/tree.mjs` and `src/shared/outline.mjs`) moves a document in the sidebar's tree with the documents under it, for an Agent tidying the workspace as the user asks (SKILL.md "Organizing documents"): under `--under`'s document, to the top level (`--top`) or where it is (neither); right after `--after`'s document (one at that level, whose level it is when neither says one), first (`--first`), else last; at least one of the four (exit 2), `--under` with `--top` or `--after` with `--first` two places (exit 2). Each document is named as every command names one (`documentNamed`: a document of the trash exit 2) and must be in the tree: one that is not there, and the 写作偏好 page (listed apart: it moves nowhere and holds none), are refused (exit 1), as is a place under itself or a document under it, after itself, or an `--after` at another level than `--under`'s (`moveIn`'s reasons). The move is written through the workspace's running server when there is one (`POST /__gallery/workspace/move`, in the queue the workbench's own moves and the pages' saves go through, so that no change is lost and an open workbench shows it at once: workbench.md "One source"); with none, or one that does not take it (another workspace's, an older Gallery's), here, by the same function under the outline's lock (`moveDocument`: several of an Agent's moves run at once each land). Nothing in a document's files changes: links between documents are relative links between their files (`../<slug>/doc.html`), which go by their folders, not their place in the tree. It says where it went and where it was, by each document's slug and title (`Moved loose (零散): now under notes (排序笔记), right after a (甲), last; it was at the top level, first.`; `… is there already: …` when it was, exit 0), then its place as `gallery list` says it (`位置：project (项目) › notes (排序笔记)`).
- **`gallery set <document> [--title "<title>"] [--icon <icon>|none] [--description "<text>"]`** (`src/cli/set.mjs`) changes a document's title, page icon or description as the workbench's 重命名 and the page's icon picker change them, its header and `doc.json` in step (`headerChanged()` in `src/editor/source/natural.mjs`): the title the inside of its `h1.doc-title` (replaced whole, as the editor's renaming does) and `<title>` kept in step as the hand-in keeps it (`inStep`: where it holds the h1's words of before once, those, else the whole of it), and `doc.json`'s title; the icon the header's `div.doc-icon` (its value written in it, its block id kept; one made right before the h1 on a line of its own; taken away with its line by `none`) and `doc.json`'s `icon` (taken out by `none`); the description `doc.json`'s alone. An icon is any value the page icon takes (`iconKind`): an emoji, an icon of the 图标 tab (`/icons/<name>_<color>.svg`, Tabler's `/icons/tabler/…`, one the set has: `glyphSvg`; as Git Bash hands it over too, `C:/Program Files/Git/icons/…`, read as what was typed), or a picture of the document's folder (`assets/<file>`, which must be there: exit 1); anything else, an empty title or description and nothing to set are mistakes (exit 2). It writes as an Agent writes, the rest of `doc.html` as it is, not through the edit service (whose renaming is the reader's, which the review of the Agent's changes leaves out): the server hears the write as any of an Agent's, so the open page shows it, the review counts it, and the workbench's sidebar reads the list again as `doc.json` changes. `doc.html` is read again right before it is replaced (`writeAtomic`'s `expect`: a save of the page meanwhile is read and the header made again, three times at most, else nothing written, exit 1); a document still in the format's old names is written in the new ones, as the editor's renaming writes it. Refused (exit 1): a document that is not there, the 写作偏好 page (only the user changes it), a page with no `h1.doc-title` in its `main`, a `doc.json` that does not read. It says what changed, each `from → to` with where it was written (`Set weekly:` and `  title: "…" → "…" (doc.html's h1 and <title>, doc.json)`), or `Nothing to change: …` when they hold that already.

## Quality check and the preview's address

What a reader meets once a document is exported, and the address to give the user while it is written: two commands of their own (`src/cli/`), on the same browser code as the workbench's PDF (`src/transfer/browser.mjs`: `findBrowser()`, GALLERY_BROWSER or the system's Chrome, Edge or Chromium; `settle()`).

- **`gallery qa <slug>…`** (or `--all`; `src/cli/qa.mjs`, the command line read by `src/cli/arguments.mjs`) looks at `<workspace>/exports/<slug>.html`, the file the reader gets, never the preview: a document with none yet is exported first, as a stale one is (below; with `--no-export` it says `run gallery export <slug> first` and exits 2 before a browser starts). An export says which `report.html` it was made from: the sha256 of the file's bytes as the export read them, in a meta of its own right after its policy (`<meta name="doc-export-source" content="sha256:…">, named as the view copy's `doc-export-id` is`, `sourceMeta()` and `exportSourceOf()` in `src/shared/export-wrap.mjs`; never in `report.html`; an import of the export takes it out). One not made from `<workspace>/<slug>/doc.html` as the file is now (`staleExport()`: another sha256, or none said, by an export made before exports said it) is of another text: the user typed in the preview since, the file was changed after the export, or restored from a snapshot; it is judged by the bytes, never by the files' times, so a file restored with its old time, or a workspace copied, is judged right. So is one of this text that holds other versions of the custom blocks than the workspace now has (`packaged`, "A custom block on a page"). Such an export, or none, is exported first, as `gallery export` does (`exportFirst()`: `gallery export` in a process of its own, the command's launcher (`src/cli/bin.mjs`, the package's `<package>/bin/gallery.mjs`) run with the same workspace, from the workspace's folder, the hand-in and the checks with it, its output said as it says it, after a line saying why in a reader's words (`exportingFirst()`: `<slug>: exporting first: doc.html changed since the last export (<workspace>/exports/<slug>.html), as gallery export <slug> does (--no-export: refuse instead)`, or `no export yet (…)`, or `the last export (…) does not say which text it was made from (an older Gallery's)`; never the hashes, which the refusal of `--no-export` gives: the line read as an error before an export that went well, the skill test of 2026-10-29); the exports it made are those now of their file as it is, never read from its output), and the export it made is looked at even when the file changed again meanwhile; an export that fails exits 1 with nothing looked at. `--no-export` refuses such an export instead (`run gallery export <slug> first`, exit 2). It prints the sha256 of the export it looked at and when it was written, with its zone (`timeOf()`, `2026-10-25T14:03:59+08:00`: two shells of one machine may each have their own, Git Bash's `TZ` and Windows' zone), so a result names its file and its moment. Each look (`LOOKS`: 1024, 736 and 360px wide in a window 900px tall, and 1024px again in a laptop's 1024×768 window, where a frame of the kit's height, 72vh, shows fewer rows; that look keeps only what the 1024px one did not find, `findingKey()`: its kind, its element's anchor and its words) is a browser context of its own (no reading state kept), the looks run at once in the one browser (the laptop's window after the 1024px look, whose findings it needs; each look's pictures named by its look, so none share a file: the same findings and pictures as one after another, byte for byte, in about half the time, a run on one document of several Sheets 12.2 s → 6.7 s), in the light theme, with motion as it is (under reduced motion the kit's tables end at one of two widths: docs/testing.md); the page is looked at once its own document is read (its DOMContentLoaded: an embed block's frame, online, may load late or never, and is no part of the document; the kit's first drawing waits for the fonts' `ready`, which waits for that frame, so a frame that never loads makes that wait run out, said as unverified, and the page is looked at all the same), then waited for by `settle()`: the fonts, the kit's first drawing (`html[data-doc-drawn]`), then the page the same in three looks 100ms apart (its height, the styles its scripts set, its drawings), never a wait of fixed length alone. Every disclosure is opened and every picture loaded (a lazy one too), and the page is looked at; then each Sheet that was not shown and is in no detail card, its tab clicked (those of the Sheets it is in first), waited for and its panel looked at; then each detail card (a `<dialog>` closed in the page, `prepare()` numbers them, with the tabs that reach the Sheet panel it is in and the cards it is in) opened in turn, that panel shown and those cards opened first (in a panel not shown, or a card closed, it draws nothing: never opened, every run was not verified and no baseline written), as a reader's click opens it (`openCard()`: the kit's `DocDialogs.open`, which locks the page behind it, a dialog of the page's own by `showModal()`; the focus taken off what took it, its opening animation done), waited for, looked at like a panel (its findings said with `[detail card "<title>"]`), the blocks in it pictured, then each of its Sheets' other panels shown and looked at as the page's are (`lookAtSheet`; with `--tiles` the card again, `<width>-card<i>-sheet<j>-<n>.png`), and closed (`closeCard()`), with the cards opened for it: what those panels hold is pictured and taken as seen only so (since an Agent simulation of 2026-10-26; before, it was recorded as seen, shown nowhere). Each width's line says how many cards it opened (`1 detail card (closed in the file, opened in turn)`); one that does not open is said, unverified, at its place. What two looks find of one element (a picture in a Sheet, checked on the whole page and again in its panel) is one finding, said where it shows: each element is numbered once, shown or not. A wait that runs out, a Sheet whose panel stays hidden (said at its tab), a detail card that does not open, a picture not taken are findings (`unverified`), never left out; so is a browser that does not start (exit 3, as with none).
- **What it looks for, by level** (`KINDS`, `levelOf()`). *fail*: the page's errors (uncaught, or on the console from the document's own scripts: a chart of the document's own that throws says `[DocKit] chart: …`), a request that failed, what the export's policy blocked (`securitypolicyviolation`: a file not embedded, named with the element that asked for it: a `src`, `href`, `poster`, `data`, a `srcset` candidate, a `url()` in a `style` attribute or a `<style>`, not a link's `href` or a reference within the page; Chrome names a `file:` address it blocked by its scheme alone, `file`, and that is the next `file:` address the page asks for; its failed request and its broken picture are that one finding, and an address is named by its path from the export, or by its end when long, `urlName()`), a picture that did not load, a chart host (`[data-doc-chart]`) with no drawing (nothing in it but its data and the kit's error note: an svg, a canvas or the HTML a chart of the document's own draws, shown with a size, is a drawing), a chart's text cut by a clip (an svg that hides its overflow, a `clip-path` rectangle, an HTML element whose overflow is `hidden` or `clip`; one that scrolls hides nothing a reader cannot reach) or reaching past its card (the outline of a turned label as it is drawn, 1.5px allowed), TeX shown as written (`\(`, `\[` or `$$` outside the elements KaTeX leaves alone, `MATH_IGNORED_TAGS`) or refused by KaTeX (`.katex-error`), the page wider than the window (with what reaches past it outside anything that scrolls or clips: an element, up to three, or, outside those, the words of a text that cannot break, a long path or URL, which run past the box of their element while the box ends inside the window, so that no element's box says it: found by their rects, `Range.getClientRects()` on each text node, said in their nearest block, `data-block-id`, up to three, with the words past the edge, `the words "C:\Users\…" in div.doc-callout run past its edge to 457px (a text that cannot break)`, and pictured as that block; in a Sheet's panel too, where the page's width alone, unplaced, is left to the look of the page as it opens),. *warn*: at 736px and wider, a table's column out of sight at first or cut at the frame's edge, by the words of every cell of it, header and body (`tableHides()` in `src/shared/table-hides.mjs`, the kit's own measure, which qa puts in the page it looks at as its text: a column hides what its words hide, never its cells' padding, so a long word deep in the body counts though the header fits, and a cell reaching past the edge with nothing written there does not), and a table's rows below a frame of the kit's height (`table-rows`: a body row whose words end below the frame's bottom; a frame with a `--doc-table-height` of its own, the author's or the reader's drag, is meant to scroll: it is only among the tables that scroll, info), said with what shows them all (`style="--doc-table-height: none"` on its `div.doc-table-wrap`, or the table split where its content divides); narrower, a table that scrolls is what it does, so both are info, and the kit's own notes of the table's columns and rows say the same, so the measuring speaks for them. Also a code block whose code scrolls inside its frame of the kit's (`code-scrolls`: the pre taller than its frame of 40 lines by more than 4px, said with the lines below the frame) and a formula block wider than its column (`formula-scrolls`), at 736px and wider; narrower they are info, and pictured all the same (`PICTURED_INFO`): a phone's long lines wrap, so a code block grows past its frame there. What scrolls as the author or the reader chose is info at every width, never a warning, and pictured on a phone alone, as any that scrolls there is (the finding says its own level, `findingLevel()`; `picturedAt()`), as a table's frame of their height warns of nothing: a code block taller than a frame of its own height (`--doc-code-height` on the block or its pre, the author's or the reader's drag), one wider than its frame, its lines left unwrapped (`doc-code--nowrap`, the author's or the reader's wrap button: said apart from its height, as a reader scrolls it sideways), and a code block or a formula in a disclosure closed in the file (`data-qa-closed`, set as qa opens it: where a whole sample goes, as SKILL.md says; a reader who opens it scrolls in it). Also texts of one drawing over each other (`chart-text-overlap`, a chart's or a drawing's svg: two labels whose outlines, a turned one as it is drawn and each as tall as its font's size about its box's middle, reach more than 1.5px into each other, as an axis's crowded labels do), and the tick labels of a value axis crowding at any width (`ticks`: a group of `g.tick` whose upright labels are evenly spaced numbers, each a number alone with at most a sign, a currency sign and a short unit (`20%`, `$1,000`, `3万`; categories that hold digits, `第1周` or `Q1`, none: they are text over text when they crowd), two of them closer than `CROWDED_TICKS` of their font's size, 0.6em across a chart (less than a digit's width and a hair: closer, numbers read as one run of digits) and a quarter of a line down it; measured, not counted, as d3's `ticks(n)` is a hint: five labels, 0 to 8,000, along a phone's 302px axis stand 28px apart and pass, 21 labels 0 to 100 at 1024px stand 13px apart and pass, eleven 0.0 to 1.0 at 360px stand 7px apart and warn; the labels it names are not said again as text over text). Also, at any width, words past the side of their box in a whole block or in a figure's drawing (`overflow-in-block`: the blocks `blocksOf()` marks `whole`, the editor's `block` unit, and `figure`, a card's, measured by `measure()` with their ids, a card's own title, caption and paragraphs straight in it and the kit's line of a chart that drew nothing left out: a text whose rects reach more than 1.5px past the content box of the element that lays it out, its nearest that is not inline, with nothing from it up to the block that clips or scrolls;
