three boxes a block at most, the words past each said, pictured as the block; sideways only, as a tight line height lets glyphs reach above and below their line; words past the window's edge are the page's overflow instead): a year that cannot break runs out of its narrow column on a phone, over the dot or the words beside it, inside the page, where the page-wide check sees nothing and no check of the kit's measures; words and marks of the same blocks cut at the bottom of a box that clips them (`cut-in-block`, `cutsOf()`: a box of a fixed or a greatest height whose content is taller, or what is drawn past such a box and cut by the box around it, overflow `hidden` or `clip` downwards; a word's rects reaching more than 1.5px past the box's padding edge, a line set tighter than its font by more than the room the font takes beyond it, so a glyph's descent is no cut; a mark, an svg's shape, cut by the svg's edge too, or an element that paints a box and holds nothing, a bar or a dot, its sides within the box: a shape bleeding out of every side on purpose is none, and pictures are cropped as their author chose; what clips it stands from it up to the page with nothing between that scrolls, a reader scrolling to the rest of a table's frame, a code block, a formula block or a detail card, an element placed absolutely clipped from its containing block up and one placed fixed by nothing outside it; never one of the kit's own, an element of a `doc-` class but the block's own box, nor a control, so its folded and collapsed parts are none, nor a box folded to 1.5px or less, a part of the report's own that opens; three boxes a block at most, the words and marks cut in each said, pictured as the block); and the text of an svg its `viewBox` scales below 11px on the screen (`svg-text-small`: each shown text's font size, in the svg's units, times the vertical scale of its screen matrix; one finding a drawing, its small texts listed, named by its card), as a drawing 800 units wide shrinks its 14-unit labels to 6px on a phone. *info*: the kit's other layout notes, each said, a code block's title cut to fit its header (`code-title-cut`), and the tables that scroll (more than 4px past their frame, `SCROLL_SLACK`: a table a pixel or two wider than its frame scrolls by its rounding and is none), listed by their line and name (the caption, else the title of the chart card drawn from it, else the small text right above the frame, its `.doc-tab` part after a ` · `, else the first header cell) with the direction they scroll in (→ sideways, ↓ down) and the looks they scroll in. A warning never fails: a column or a label taken out to silence one is a document made worse.
- **The kit's layout notes.** The kit says what it hides to fit, or what it could not draw (`data-doc-layout` on the element it drew, one note a line, each `<name>: <message>`: a table frame's `columns-hidden: …` and `rows-hidden: …`, a chart host's `data-missing: …`), and takes a note off when it no longer applies. `gallery qa` reads them where they show, each at its level (`layoutNotesOf()` and `levelOf()` in `src/shared/qa-levels.mjs`, by the name before the first `:`): `data-missing` fails (a chart drawn from a table with nothing to draw, as a chart that drew nothing), `columns-hidden` and `rows-hidden` are a table's columns and rows out of sight (warnings at 736px and wider, info below), any other note is info, and every one is said; on a table it measured itself, the measuring (the kit's own) stands for the kit's notes of its columns and rows. The preview's note reads them with the same function (`preview-checks.mjs`, below), so what fails qa is among its failures and the rest are its notes.
- **Where a finding is.** Each finding names its place in `report.html` (`placeOf()` on `sourceIndex()`): the line of the element's nearest `data-block-id` (an export carries every block id the file has), else of its chart card's title, of its table's first header cell, of the element or what holds it found by its words (both sides without blanks: the kit spaces Chinese and Latin words as it draws; of equal elements, the one at the same count in the file's order as in the page's, so two equal paragraphs are two lines), of its nearest `id`, or of its section's heading; `the page` for what has no element (an error). A table is named by its name, as the list of the tables that scroll names it (`tableNameOf()` in the page: its caption, else the title of the chart card drawn from it, `data-doc-chart-source` on its frame, else the `p.doc-note` right above its frame, which a caption of words becomes, its `.doc-tab` part after a ` · `, else its first header cell's words). A finding seen alike at several widths is one line with those widths.
- **What it prints and leaves.** Text, in an order made for an Agent that reads only the first lines or only the last ones of a long output (it did: a shell's `head`, `tail` or `Select-Object` cut the counts, the warnings or the verdict off): after the export's line, the tables that scroll, a line each said as information (`INFO       table-scrolls: report.html:<line> "<name>": → <looks>; ↓ <looks> (a reader scrolls it: not a problem)`: read as problems once their heading was cut off), each by its line and name with its directions; then each finding by level, kind and place (info too, but what a table that scrolls does: its columns and rows out of sight on a phone are its scrolling), with the looks it was seen in (no picture named: the list below names them); then a line a look (fail, warn and unverified counted, the tables that scroll and how many each way, the charts, tables, disclosures Sheets and detail cards it went through, the page's height; its disclosures said as the file has them, `2 disclosures (closed in the file, opened for the pictures)`, `disclosuresSaid()`; the laptop's window saying only what it added); after them, of a run of named blocks, what it did not picture that a plain run would (`notPicturedLine()`, from `blocksToPicture()`'s `left`: `Not pictured by this run, which pictures the blocks named alone: 2 blocks changed or moved since the last look (…); 4 cards the change of the document's own <script> or <style> since the last look may draw again (…). gallery qa <slug> pictures them.`, the blocks changed or moved outside those named, each the outermost as a plain run groups them, and, when the document's own script or style changed, the cards outside them; with no earlier look, every card outside them, `no earlier look: 2 cards never pictured (…)`; a run of named blocks after a fix pictured the tables named and never the chart the fix redrew, and said nothing of it: the skill test of 2026-10-28; no new state: the baseline keeps them as they were, so the next plain run pictures them); then the pictures, once and named nowhere else (`checklistSaid()`, from `describeLooks()`'s `pictures`; it replaced a header, a line a block, the flagged ones' and the tiles' lines before the findings, the pictures named again after each finding, and the `Not opened yet` line that an Agent counted the pictures it had opened by: the user's of 2026-11-11, an Agent's rules for a run were too many): `要看的图（N 张）：…`, the pictures to open, numbered, in sections: `The elements flagged in the findings above`, an entry for each place a FAIL or a WARN names, its worst finding named, with one picture, the narrowest width it was flagged at (INFO needs none); the blocks a run was asked to picture (`--blocks`); the tiles asked for (`--tiles`). A plain run's pictures of the blocks changed and of the cards, and the tiles it took by itself of a document with no block ids, are the checks' to look at: kept in the run's folder and not listed, said as one line under `不用看：` (`这次给 N 个改动的块、M 个卡片拍的 K 张图，检查已经看过…`; `kept` in `describeLooks()`'s `pictures`), the user's of 2026-11-13: an Agent opened a dozen pictures and more for each change, three widths of each block and card, again after each fix, where the findings said all there was. `--pictures` lists every picture the run took instead, under `这次拍的图（N 张）：按需要打开，不用全看` (each place flagged with every look's picture of it, INFO's too, `picturesSaid()`; then each group of blocks under its heading, the changed blocks and then the cards, an entry a block: the pictures of a block that holds some in a Sheet panel or a detail card all in its entry, and a block held that no picture of its holder showed at a width, its own picture there, under its holder's; then the page in tiles, an entry a kind of tile, with its pattern, `<run>/tiles/<width>-<n>.png`), each picture by its whole path (the run's folder, those that differ by their look alone as one name, the looks in braces, `<run>/changed/{1024,736,360}-qtrl7h36.png`; a path of one piece, which a copy took whole: the blind test of 2026-11-09) and what it is (`report.html:<line> <id> <tag.class>`, a moved block with the edges of its neighbours, the blocks it holds); then `不用看：…`, what needs no look (the pictures kept, above; the cards not changed that the twelve a run pictures left out, which stay `unseen`; the blocks hidden from readers, pictured at no width; the INFO notes), or that all the rest has no problem; then where the run's folder is and that five runs are kept (also when it lists none to open but kept some). N is the pictures to open, a name of three looks counting three; `要看的图（0 张）：没有要看的图` when none is. The list has nothing of the pictures of a run that took none and has nothing to say of any. Last the verdict (`verdictOf()`), a line that starts with `问题：` when something failed (fix it, run again) and with `下一步：` otherwise, which repeats each look's failures and warnings; with nothing found, `no picture to open; hand the document over` (no look of the Agent's own, no run of tiles or of named blocks asked for: `morePictures` is gone, and so is the run of tiles a run that pictured nothing used to ask for). `qa/` is in the workspace's `.gallery/`, and each run's pictures go to a folder of its own, `.gallery/qa/<slug>/<the run's time>/` (`startRun()`: the local time to the second, `2026-11-11_14-02-33`, a number after it for a second run of the same second, `runName()`), holding `flagged/` and the folders of its kind of run: so that no rule of what a run empties is to be remembered, and the pictures of the run before are still there to compare with. The last five runs of a document are kept (`RUNS_KEPT`), each earlier one taken away as a run ends (`endRun()`, which takes away the folders of the run that took no picture too, and a run that took none whole, so that it displaces no earlier run); what an earlier Gallery kept loose in `qa/<slug>/` (`flagged/`, `changed/`, `cards/`, `blocks/`, `tiles/` and pictures beside `blocks.json`) is taken away when a run starts. `blocks.json`, the baseline, and `exported.html` stay in `qa/<slug>/`, beside the runs. Every picture is framed by what its element draws, not cut at its box (`inkOf()`, in the page, the element scrolled into view first as an element's screenshot is: its box with what it holds that shows, the words of its text nodes, and what its own and their `::before` and `::after` draw where they are placed absolutely, a timeline's dot at `left: -7px`, each cut by what clips it up to the element, so a table's frame that scrolls shows only what is in it; the whole cut by what clips the element, `INK_MARGIN` px, 8, around it for an outline or a shadow, inside the document; one in a fixed box, an open detail card, as the window shows it): an element screenshot cut at the border box halved such a dot, and an Agent moved its margins to "fix" what the page showed whole. `flagged/`, every run's: the pictures of the flagged elements (fail and warn, and `PICTURED_INFO`'s kinds at any level; one each in its look, `<look>-<n>-<kind>.png`, `1024x768` for the laptop's window); `changed/` and `cards/` (a run without `--blocks` or `--tiles`) or `blocks/` (`--blocks <id,…>`, those named; the ids split at commas and white space, `namedBlocks()`, since PowerShell passes `--blocks a,b` unquoted as an array that npm's `gallery.ps1` joins with a space; one no block of the document has is refused, `--blocks: no block of <its file> has the id …: nothing was looked at and no picture removed`, exit 2, before anything is exported, emptied or looked at, `unknownBlocks()`: it pictured nothing, emptied `blocks/` and said `nothing found`, exit 0, the skill test of 2026-10-29): the pictures of blocks at 1024, 736 and 360px (`<width>-<id>.png`, each where it first shows: on the page, in its Sheet's panel or in its detail card opened; one shown nowhere is said `not shown at any width (hidden)`), by their line and what they are, each group under a heading of its own (`blocksToPicture()`'s `groups`; the folder of each block, `out.folders` in `lookAt()`): those named (`blocks/`), each holding all it holds, so a Sheet area named is pictured with each panel; else, in `changed/`, those changed since the baseline (`blocksOf()`: each block's own markup hashed, its tag, attributes and words and what it holds that is no block, blanks as one (spaces, tabs and line breaks, as HTML collapses them: a no-break space is a character, and a space made one is a change), so a block whose own part changed and a new one count, and what holds them does not; a chart card drawn from a table, `data-doc-chart-data` on its host, holds its table's hash too, `data-doc-chart-source` on the table's frame, since what it draws changes with the table) or moved (`movedBlocks()`: in another block than it was, or, among the blocks of the one it stayed in, out of the order the others kept: the fewest whose moving explains the new order, a longest run of their places before kept; a block added or taken out moves none), but a changed block a changed card or a moved block holds (the nodes and paragraphs of a new flowchart, a figure new in new columns, what a section moved holds), which that one's picture shows (`(with the 6 changed blocks it holds)`; the outermost's), or a block most of whose blocks changed holds (not a card: three changed at least, half of all the blocks in it or more, at any depth, `MOSTLY_CHANGED`; counted are the changed blocks that would each take a picture, not one moved, nor one a changed card or a moved block holds, so a section holding a new Sheet area is not pictured for it), pictured once whole though its own words did not change (an opening summary whose paragraph and items an Agent rewrote took 8 of the twelve, an item each, and left a changed disclosure and every card unpictured: the skill test of 2026-10-29; a `--blocks` run names it so among those it left), the moved first (a move is most often what the user asked for, and fell past the twelve behind paragraphs changed earlier in the file), a moved block pictured with the edges of the blocks before and after it in its parent (`movedClips()`: from 200px above it, `NEIGHBOUR_EDGE`, no higher than the block before it reaches, to as far below it, so a tall neighbour adds no more than that; a block taller than 2000px, `MOVED_WHOLE`, as its two joins, its first and its last 600px, `MOVED_PART`, each with its neighbour's edge, `<width>-<id>-top.png` and `-bottom.png`; `(moved: with the edges of the blocks before and after it)`); of blocks swapped, the smaller is the one moved (of the longest runs kept, the one of the largest blocks, by the characters of the file each spans); then, in `cards/`, under a heading of their own (`The cards no look has pictured yet (not changed themselves), at …`: an Agent took them for blocks it had changed), the cards no look has pictured yet (`unseen`); when the changed blocks took all twelve, no card is pictured and no section is made for the cards (they are said under `不用看：`; before, a heading said `none this run (the 12 blocks a run pictures went to the changed blocks above)`, so as never to say `pictured` over no picture: the skill test of 2026-10-29); every card instead, those unseen first, with no baseline (`… no earlier look to compare with`), when no block changed (`… no block changed since the last look, nor the report's own <script> or <style>`), or when the document's own script or style changed and may draw any of them (`pageOf()`: the hash of its `<script>` and `<style>` elements no block holds, kept in the baseline as `page`; `The cards, not changed themselves (…): the report's own <script> or <style> changed since the last look, which may draw any of them`: a define changed alone was said as `no block changed` over a list of cards). The instances of a custom block whose stamp changed since (`packageStamps()` of `qa-baseline.mjs`, each custom block the report holds instances of by its stamp, kept in the baseline as `packages`; one the baseline says nothing of, as one of an earlier version, is as it was), their own markup as it was, are changed blocks all the same, pictured into `changed/` under a heading of their own after the other changed blocks (`The instances of a custom block changed since the last look (local/board), at 1024, 736 and 360px:`; a run of named blocks says those it left, `2 instances of a custom block changed since the last look, local/board (…)`): a custom block's style changed drew its instances anew in no picture, the skill test of 2026-11-05. The stamp kept is the one now once every such instance was pictured, else the one compared with. A card another card or a moved block pictured holds is folded into that one's picture, the outermost's, at a first look too (`held`: the tables of a Sheet area, the pictures of columns; `(with the 3 cards it holds)`): one place for both, where each took one of the twelve. The pictures of a block that holds some in a Sheet panel not shown at first are taken again with each such panel shown (`<width>-<id>-sheet<i>.png`, the Sheet area as it shows then), and with each detail card opened that holds some (`<width>-<id>-card<i>.png`, the card), `pictureHeld()` in `lookAt()`: its line says `pictured again with each Sheet panel or detail card that holds one`, as a Sheet area's picture shows its first panel alone; of the blocks held, those a picture showed are `covered`, and those that showed and are in none of its pictures (the middle of a tall section moved, pictured at its two joins) `missed`, at each width on its own (`missedWidths()`: a picture at another width is no picture at this one, as a moved section pictured whole at 1024 and 736px and at its joins at 360px left its changed blocks in no 360px picture, said nowhere, in the skill test of 2026-10-30), pictured on their own at that width in the same run (`run()`: a second look at the page at each width that missed some, with only those blocks, `lookAt()` given them and the findings of the look before as known; their entries in the list under their holder's, and the holder's line says nothing more; only a picture that could not be taken leaves the line with the widths and the command, `2 of them not pictured at 360px: gallery qa <slug> --blocks a,b`). Those pictured come by rank (`rankOf()`, `src/cli/qa-baseline.mjs`): what the Agent draws or lays out itself first, the likeliest to clip or crowd (cards, `figure.doc-figure`, a chart or a drawing; code blocks; a whole block, the editor's `block` unit, an element of a class of the document's own or a bare `<svg>`: the free drawing the skill leads with; an instance of a custom block (`isInstance()`), laid out by its custom block's style; a flowchart, `ol.doc-flow`; a Sheet area, `section.doc-sheet-shell`), then columns (`div.doc-columns`) and the other figures (a picture, a file, a bookmark, an embedded page), then tables (a document may hold many, and they took every place), then the rest, each rank in the file's order. Every changed block (`blocksToPicture()`'s `complete`, which the command passes; `CHANGED_PICTURES`, sixty, at most, the rest in a line that names them and gives the command, `2 changed blocks past the 60 a run pictures, not pictured (…): gallery qa <slug> --blocks …`) and the cards that are left of the twelve (`BLOCK_PICTURES`; a plain call of `blocksToPicture()`, as the unit tests make, still stops at twelve for all, the rest named by `rest`): a run left blocks to a second run that the Agent had to ask for (`gallery qa <slug> --blocks …`, "not pictured"), and the skill had a rule for it; the cards past the twelve need no look (nothing changed in them, nothing is flagged: `不用看：另有 8 个没改过的卡片这次没有拍…`) and stay `unseen`, so that a later run pictures them once a block changed; a document with no block ids (never handed in) is pictured whole in tiles instead. `tiles/` (`--tiles`, instead of `changed/` and `cards/`; with `--blocks`, besides `blocks/`): the page as it opens at each width in tiles of 1800px (`<width>-<n>.png`; each Sheet area with the panel it shows first), each Sheet panel shown in turn, after it is shown, in tiles of its own across the window's width (`<width>-sheet<i>-<n>.png`, `i` its tab's number in the page, from 1), counted apart, each panel by its number and its tab's words (`Each other Sheet panel, shown in turn: 3 tiles in tiles/ (<width>-sheet<i>-<n>.png): sheet2 "乙" 3 tiles`), and each detail card opened as the window shows it, its content scrolled a tile at a time (`<width>-card<i>-<n>.png`, `i` its number among the page's cards, from 1; `Each detail card, opened in turn as the window shows it, its content scrolled a tile at a time: 3 tiles in tiles/ (<width>-card<i>-<n>.png): card1 "敕版" 3 tiles`). A finding of a kind `ADVICE` has says once, under its lines, at any level, what to do when it matters to the reader, and, for one that fails nothing, that it is no failure (`overflow`: a long path, URL or name wrapped in `<code>`, an element wider than the window given `max-width: 100%` or a frame that scrolls, never a path cut or rewritten; `table-columns`: if the reader must see the column on a desktop, write its long values compactly (a cell of numbers stays on one line, so its longest value sets the column's width: a range as a–b, fewer digits; the skill test of 2026-10-29), move a unit out of its header into the line above the table, shorten the header, or split the table; never delete a column to silence it. `chart-text-overlap`: shorten the labels (a unit in the axis's title), move one (above its row, a leader line) or give the drawing more room at that width; `ticks`: fewer ticks at that width with `axis.tickValues`, at most four on a phone (`ticks(n)` is a hint d3 may exceed), or shorter numbers; `svg-text-small`: a larger font size in the viewBox's units, or a layout of its own for narrow widths; `code-scrolls`: `style="--doc-code-height: none"` on its `<pre>` (in the Agent's terms: no class of the kit's to write), and for code that scrolls sideways its own (`CODE_SIDEWAYS`, `adviceOf`): let the lines wrap (no `data-wrap="false"` on its `<pre>`) or break them, or leave a tree or a log scrolling, never cut a line (both, one under the other, for a block that scrolls both ways); `formula-scrolls`: break it into lines, `\begin{aligned}`, or into two formulas; `overflow-in-block`: give the box room at that width in an `@media` rule of the document's `<style>`, or a smaller font there, or let the words break, never cut them; `cut-in-block`: let the box grow with what it holds, `min-height` instead of `height` or `max-height`, give it room at that width in an `@media` rule, or let it scroll, never delete the words); every kind that warns has one but `table-rows`, whose own words say how to show every row. The verdict counts the places printed at each level (a kind at a place, however many looks saw it), not each look's findings, repeats each look's failures and warnings (`failures at 1 place (1024px fail 0, warn 2; 1024×768 fail 0, warn 0; 736px fail 0, warn 2; 360px fail 1, warn 0)`), says to fix a failure in `report.html` and run qa again, which exports first, and names the commands for more pictures (`morePictures()`: `gallery qa <slug> --tiles` and `… --blocks <id,…>`, the document's slug when it looked at one), and says which exports this run made first, with why (`It was exported first by this run (it had none yet).`); a run that took no picture, with nothing to picture (no block changed and no card) and nothing flagged, says so in place of the pictures to open and gives the run of tiles as the next step (`unpictured`: a new document with no card said "open the pictures above" over none, the skill test of 2026-11-02); after it, the last lines, each export's absolute path after `离线文件（用户要文件时再给）：` (`HAND_OVER`, `src/cli/deliverable.mjs`'s label and path) on a line of its own, as `gallery export` prints it, so that the last line an Agent hands over from holds the deliverable whole (an Agent whose context was cut short wrote a path of its own making; on the verdict's line, after its advice, one read the two as one: the skill test of 2026-10-28); a browser that does not start ends with them too. With warnings and no failure it says how many places to read (`warnings at 2 places to read, …`; "to look at" until 2026-11-13, which read as an order to open their pictures), that none is a failure, that each says what to do if it matters to the reader, and that no column or label is deleted to silence one. The browser is started by qa itself (`startBrowser()` and `startChromium()` in `src/transfer/browser.mjs`): puppeteer's default arguments, in a fresh temporary profile removed after, its DevTools port read from its first words and driven through `puppeteer.connect`, never `puppeteer.launch`, whose kill of the browser by `taskkill` on Windows (as it closed one by force, or as this process exited with a launch that had failed) printed `ERROR: The process with PID … could not be terminated. Reason: There is no running instance of the task.` into qa's output; closed through the protocol, waited for, killed silently only if it still runs, on every way out; a launch that fails is tried once more with another profile. It exits 1 when anything fails (or the export it ran first did), 3 when nothing could be looked at at all (no browser found, or one that does not start, twice: nothing is looked at, the `UNVERIFIED` line saying what to do, without puppeteer's advice on a profile in use, which an Agent cannot act on, to run qa once more and then to look at the export yourself, and the handoff says so), 4 when the pages were looked at and some places could not be verified (the `UNVERIFIED` lines among the findings, a wait that ran out or a picture not taken; the verdict `nothing failed …; not verified at N places`; before, both were 3 and an Agent told them by the verdict), 2 when an export is missing or not made from its `report.html` as it is now, with `--no-export`, else 0.
- **Its baseline** (`.gallery/qa/<slug>/blocks.json` in the workspace, `src/cli/qa-baseline.mjs`): what a run compares the document's blocks with, `{ blocks: { id: hash }, places: { id: [parent, at] }, moved: [id], unseen: [id], from?, time?, idless?, page? }`, read as nothing to compare with when it holds no hashes, cut short or not these tools' (an earlier version's, hashes alone, has no places: no move to tell; none of `unseen`, `from` or `time`): `readBaseline()`. After a run, `nextBaseline()`: every block takes its place as it is now; a block pictured (and a block it holds that a picture of it showed, or that showed nowhere: `seenOf()` over what each look said of them, `covered` and `missed`; a Sheet area's blocks in panels its pictures never showed were taken as looked at, and never pictured) takes its hash as it is now, any other keeps the hash it had, so a block changed and not pictured (past the twelve, not among those `--blocks` names, or in a run of tiles alone) is still changed at the next run; a moved block not pictured is kept as moved (`moved`, which `movedBlocks()` counts with the blocks it finds moved), so it is still moved at the next run, until a run pictures it (each run pictures up to twelve, so any number of blocks moved are all pictured in as many runs, or in one run that names the rest with `--blocks`); a first look without `--blocks` takes every block as it is (there is nothing to compare with) but the cards it did not picture (past the twelve, or held and in no picture), kept as `unseen`, as does a run that pictured every card because the document's own script or style changed (its `page` then taken as it is; a run of blocks named or of tiles alone keeps the `page` it was compared with), which the runs after picture after what changed (or first, when nothing did) until one has, instead of taking them as looked at; and a run that found a failure, a look with anything unverified, or a first look with `--blocks`, changes nothing: the run after a fix pictures what changed since the baseline again, the fix with it, and the blocks the failing run pictured that the Agent had not opened yet come back. When there is none, the tools an Agent runs anyway write one of the document as it was before the round of changes it makes, so that the first look after a revision pictures what the revision changed, with no step of its own (it pictured cards, and the changed blocks fell past the twelve, or were a flowchart or a Sheet area, which were no cards; seven revisions in ten ran qa again with `--blocks` to see their own changes): `gallery export` (`exportBaselines()`, a block of its own in `src/cli/export.mjs`: the exports there are read before any is replaced; the workspace's export, the page's 导出报告 at `POST /__gallery/workspace/export`, the same function the same way: `src/server/export.mjs`; and the workbench's 导出 of an HTML or PDF file, `src/transfer/service.mjs`, which writes no `exports/<slug>.html` and gives `keep()` the sha256 each file was made from) keeps the `report.html` each export of it is made from (`qa/<slug>/exported.html`, when its sha256 is the one the new export carries; the user typing in the preview meanwhile keeps nothing) and, with no baseline, writes one of the text the export before it was made from (`exportedText()`: that kept file, or a version the preview's edit service kept, `history/<slug>/` before a commit or `snapshots/` served to a page, found by the sha256 that export carried; failing those, the kept file of an earlier export, older still: it pictures more, never less; but the kept file first when it was kept after that export and is not of it, the text of a later 导出 that wrote no `exports/<slug>.html`), `from: "export"` with that export's time; never of the text it exports now, which holds the edits it is exported for (a baseline taken of it would hide them all). `gallery open <slug>` (`previewBaseline()`, as it starts), with none, writes one of that same text when it is known, else of `report.html` as it is, `from: "preview"`: SKILL.md opens the preview before a revision starts (and `gallery new` and `gallery import` start it), so the file is then the document before the changes; the export's text, when there is one, comes first since the preview may be run late, after the edits, where the file would hide them. A document still holding the starter's section (`data-doc-placeholder`: `gallery new`'s) gets none: a new document has nothing to compare with, and its first look pictures its cards (a baseline of the starter would make every block changed, run after run); nor does a text of no block at all. No look has seen the text such a baseline is of (a run that failed nothing writes its own, and the export writes one only where there is none), so `baselineOf()` keeps every card of it as `unseen`: the first look pictures what changed, then those cards (two exports before the first look, or a first look that failed, which keeps the baseline, left a baseline that pictured the last edit alone and none of the cards no look had seen). It takes the text as the export will write it (`shownSource()`, natural HTML made the kit's) with a stand-in id (`ge-idless-<n>`) for each block that has none (a document the preview shows before its first export), kept by its markup without the id (`blocksOf()`'s `bare` hash) in `idless`, a block in one keeping no place; a run first adopts the blocks of the document it does not know whose bare hash is one of those (`adoptIdless()`, each taken once, the cards among them unseen), so a block the export gave an id is not taken as new, nor the section holding it as changed by its blocks' ids. The run's line says which it compared with (`baselineSaid()`): `the blocks changed since the last look`, `since the export of 2026-10-25T14:03:59+08:00 (the report before these changes)`, `since gallery open of … (the report before these changes)`. Neither command says anything of it or fails for it: a baseline not written is a first look that pictures the cards.
- **Baseline.** On the editing scenarios' sample (`tests/browser/fixtures/sample/`) and nine documents Agents wrote, nothing fails. On the Component Catalog nothing fails either (any failure there is a bug of the tool, or of its own chart types), its charts drawn at every width with no `svg-text-small`; what warns there is its wide tables at 736px and wider (`table-columns`).
- **`gallery open [<slug>]`** (`src/cli/open.mjs`, `serverFor()` and `previewLines()` in `server-process.mjs`). Resolves the document in the selected storage folder, then reuses or starts the single service for `GALLERY_DATA_DIR`. Discovery verifies `<data>/server.json` against `/__gallery/workspace/info`: token, PID, home and data directory must agree. A busy service does not cause another to start; a verified older CLI version is stopped and replaced, while a development service is reused. The lifetime lease prevents concurrent starts. In the release the process started is a keeper (`src/server/start.mjs`, `supervisor.mjs`): it runs the server as its child (`dist/server.mjs --port <n> --worker`, over an IPC channel) and starts it again on the same port when it ends unexpectedly, so that open pages reconnect within seconds (their edits kept in the browser meanwhile) instead of finding no server until the next login or command. A worker logs a promise that failed with no one waiting and goes on; an exception thrown out of a callback is logged and ends it with exit 70; its package replaced (a new `release.json`) ends it with 75, and the next worker is the new package's. The keeper starts another after 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 30 s (at least 2 s after an upgrade; the count starts over once a worker served for a minute) and gives up after the sixth; never for exit 0 (POST stop, a signal), 1 (another process ended it: `gallery stop`'s kill), a worker that never served (its port taken: the command reports it), or once another server announced itself for the data directory. A worker stops when its keeper is gone (the IPC channel closing), and writes `(pid <worker>, keeper <keeper>)` in its listening line. `gallery serve` and the sandbox run the server in the foreground, with no keeper; the dev server is Vite's, whose watcher errors are logged and passed over (`src/server/index.mjs`). Logs are `<data>/logs/server.log`; the sticky port is `<data>/last-port`, then GALLERY_PORT or 8080 and the following 19 ports. Existing saved ports (including 4317) stay preferred across upgrades. The command says the server only when that is news: started now (with its log), an older one replaced, or the server on another port than `<data>/last-port` held (`serverLine()` returns null otherwise; then the addresses given before are dead, which the line says): a `提示：` line, port and pid, and that the command is done and the server runs on in the background, nothing to wait for. A failure (`serverFor()` not ok) is a `问题：` line saying what to do. The user receives the workbench address: home `/#<link>`, other registered folders `/#~<ws-id>/<link>`. Agent-only pages use `/generated/<slug>.html?agent` with the same owner prefix where needed. File ownership and QA baseline locations are unchanged. `--no-browser` only suppresses opening a tab. `gallery serve [--port]` runs this one service in the foreground; `gallery stop` stops it from any document folder. Neither discovery nor shutdown reads old per-workspace announcements.
- **The preview's note of the kit's layout notes that fail.** `checkPanel()` always writes the note (hidden while it has nothing to say) and a script that removes its own element: it lists the notes of the page's `data-doc-layout`s (one a line) that `gallery qa` fails, read with its own function at the window's width (`layoutNotesScript()` writes `levelOf`, `layoutNotesOf` and `noteInWords` into the script as their text): `data-missing` among the failures, each with what it is on (a chart card by its title, a table by its caption, else its first column's header), said in Chinese (`noteInWords`; a chart that drew nothing by its card's own line of why), the kit's words in the item's tooltip. It follows them as the kit writes and takes them off (one MutationObserver, one frame) and shows the note while it has a failure. The kit's other notes (a table's columns or rows out of its frame) are no failure and the preview does not list them (it did once, as 排版提示, and the person editing the document had nothing to do with them): `gallery qa` documents them to the Agent at its widths.

## Design defaults

The kit's stylesheet is the single source of the values: `styles/tokens.css` holds the shared ones (colors, type scale, widths, gaps) and each component's style file its own; `DocTokens` reads the color variables, and there is no second JavaScript palette. This section states the rules and names the token or file that owns a number; where a number is written here it is the one the CSS holds, and `tests/unit/docs.mjs` checks every `--doc-*` name written in this file against the stylesheets.

- The reading column is `--doc-content-max-width` centered: 712px, 44 characters of the default 16px body text to a line of Chinese (a document's look, its 正文宽度, makes it 648px or 776px, the widths of 40 or 48 of them, whatever its 字体大小: below) (long lines of Chinese read worse, and the page keeps room at its sides); a compact chart card is at most `--doc-compact-figure-max-width`. Full-width mode keeps the maintained responsive gutter (`--doc-wide-gutter`). `.doc-page` is `overflow-wrap: break-word` (`styles/page.css`), so a run with no place to break, a long path or URL in prose, a list, a callout or inline code, breaks where it would leave its line rather than widen the page past a 360px window (qa's `the page is 441px wide`); `break-word`, not `anywhere`: a word still counts whole for min-content. Its words are whole at a line's end (`word-break: normal`), in a Chinese page as in an English one (the user's words of 2026-11-07: 绝大部分的普通英文单词都不能在行尾被拆开), and a long run breaks where its line ends instead of going whole to the next line, leaving the line before it short (theirs of 2026-11-06: 中途换行都没事): a run of more than 20 characters with no place to break inside it (`src/shared/long-runs.mjs`: letters, digits and the marks of paths and URLs between spaces, Chinese characters and the general punctuation, counted by its pieces where Chrome breaks inside one already, after a hyphen or a question mark, so state-of-the-art is never long), a path, a URL, a long identifier. The kit holds each in a `span.doc-long-run` (`word-break: break-all`, `styles/base.css`; `runtime/spacing.js`, see below), so the run alone breaks at any letter, punctuation keeping its rules (no line starts with ，。）) and what the kit holds on one line (a range, `.doc-range`; a formula and its mark) staying held; a piece of inline code holding one is held whole, its words breaking anywhere with it. The page editor, whose text holds no span, breaks a text block holding one anywhere as a whole, an ordinary word in it too ([editor.md](editor.md), "Browser side"). What says its own rule keeps it: a table's headers and short fields keep a run whole as their words (`table.css`), its inline code its runs, a code block its lines (`code.css`, `word-break: break-word`: its own 自动换行 switch); the large picture's caption copies the caption's spans (`.doc-zoom-caption`), and a flowchart's runtime lays its cards' lines out the same way (`piecesOf`).
- Display settings (`ui.js`, `styles/controls.css`): one quiet button at the top right (the sliders icon, no frame, a light fill under the pointer) opening a menu card: in an offline file first, a section of their own, 复制为完整文本 and 复制为 Markdown (`data-doc-copy`: the document's words as `gallery read --format text` and `--format markdown` write them, which the file carries, "Export" above, the Markdown with a picture or a link to a file of the document folder by its words alone, `src/transfer/carry.mjs` `offlineMarkdown`, since the file's reader has not the folder; a page that carries none, the preview or a view copy of it, shows neither row), put on the clipboard by `navigator.clipboard.writeText` (a file opened from the disk has it, the click being the reader's), else by the selection's copy of a field put in for it (`execCommand`), else shown selected in a card of the menu's surface for the reader's own Ctrl+C (`dialog.doc-copy-card`, Esc or 关闭 closes it); the row says 已复制 with a check for 0.9 s, then the menu closes, and a copy that failed says 复制失败，点击重试 in the danger's color; then 外观 (跟随系统, 浅色, 深色; the reader's choice checked and kept for the document, a workbench's theme shown but not kept), 全宽显示 (a switch: `data-doc-width="full"` on `<html>`, the attribute a document's own 全宽 puts on too, below) and 导出当前视图副本 (`data-doc-export-copy`: the page as it is shown, its kit inlined; a stylesheet it inlines says whose it is, `data-doc-kit|vendor="<id>-inline"` as an export's copies do, so the workbench's 导入 leaves it out as it does an export's). The menu closes on a press elsewhere, Esc, the window losing the focus, or a copy being made; opened from the keyboard, ↑/↓ move along its rows. Nothing is pinned open, and it is never printed or exported. The button is fixed at the top right while the page's side has room for it; where it does not (below 804px, the reading column and the button's 46px at each side, a media query's 821px as it counts a classic 17px scrollbar the page's side has not; and at full width) a fixed button would lie over the text as the page scrolls, so it stays in the room the page keeps above its header and scrolls away with it (`position: absolute`). No source writes `ui.js`'s tag (`added` in `assets.mjs`): the build, the live preview and the export put it in with the kit's other tags ("Kit tags"), and a source written with it keeps it. While a page is edited the editor widens both gutters to the room its block handle takes (64px, docs/editor.md), which only shows on windows narrower than the column and its sides.
- The reader's hand on the page (2026-11-17's review of the kit): the keys' focus is one ring on every control of the kit and on links, in a document, a card and an exported file, 2px of `--doc-accent` 2px off the control (base.css, a rule of no weight that each control's own file no longer repeats), 2px inside a control its container clips (a table's frame, a pill in a strip of tabs that scrolls); the two menus show the row the keys are on by its fill and take none. Selected text is Gallery's pale blue (`::selection`, #2383e2 at 28%, 45% in dark), the words keeping their color; a native checkbox, radio, range or progress bar takes the accent (`accent-color` on the root, a card's `--gallery-accent`). Small text, a quote and the gray of the colors is `--doc-muted`, warm (`#716e69`, `#a5a29c` in dark: 5.08:1 on white, 6.97:1 on the dark page), one family with a caption's `--doc-caption` (a step lighter) and the code's chrome; a quote's bar is a share of its words' gray (`color-mix()` with `currentColor`, so a colored quote has a bar of its color: 40% in light, 46% in dark, the user's 2026-11-19: the whole of the gray was too dark). Where nothing hovers (`hover: none`: a phone, a tablet) what the pointer reveals elsewhere stands there all the time: a sortable column's arrow (the light one at rest, a 24px target around it), a code block's fold arrow and toolbar (in its header row at 420px and under, the title wrapping before it); a finger (`pointer: coarse`) gets a 44px display button, its icon where it was, and 40px rows in its menu. On a phone (640px and under) the page's sides are 16px (a 328px column at 360px), and small text's part after its tab takes a line of its own under the rest. A select is the kit's flat control with the kit's 16px chevron in place of the browser's arrow (choice.css: a background image beside `background-color`, so a page that writes the `background` shorthand on a select takes the chevron off with it). A copy that fails (an export over plain http has no clipboard) is tried the old way (a hidden field and `execCommand`), and said when that fails too: a code block's copy button shows a cross and says 复制失败 for a moment; the display menu's 导出当前视图副本, clicked by the reader, opens again with its row saying 导出失败，点击重试 in the danger's color.
- On paper (a reader's Ctrl+P, the browser's PDF, and Gallery's own PDF): light whatever the reader chose (`color-scheme: light` for print, tokens.css), a code block whole (no frame of 40 lines or of the author's `--doc-code-height`, long lines wrapped where it scrolled them, a folded block open: code.css), and as Gallery's PDF prints a document (src/transfer/browser.mjs `showEverything`) so does the reader's own print: on `beforeprint` ui.js shows each Sheet panel under a copy of its own tab (`data-doc-dev`), the row of tabs gone, and opens every disclosure, all put back on `afterprint` (nothing while the page is edited, nothing where the page is shown whole already: Chrome's PDF of a page fires `beforeprint` too). A sheet of paper never ends right after the title or a heading, nor through a card, a callout, a highlight panel or the summary, or a table's row, where the page has room for it whole (page.css). A chart drawn in the dark theme keeps its dark colors on paper (it is drawn again only in a frame, after the print).
- A document's look (字体与布局, the workbench's 更多; `src/shared/document-look.mjs`) is the document's own over the workspace's default look, choice by choice. The workspace's is `look` in its `gallery.json` (`src/shared/workspace-look.mjs`), `{ "font": "serif" | "mixed", "size": "small" | "large", "spacing": "compact" | "loose", "width": "narrow" | "wide", "full": "full", "tables": "wide" }`, each choice at its default left out and the field gone when all are; the card's 应用到全部文档 writes it (`POST looks`, [workbench.md](workbench.md)), and a workspace without it, an older one's, has every choice at its default. The document's own is `look` in its `doc.json` (`report.json` in an old one), the same object with a default allowed too (`"font": "sans"`, `"size": "default"`…: 默认 chosen on its page while the workspace's says otherwise), holding each choice that is not the workspace's and the field gone when none is (`ownLookFor`, `withLookField`). The look a document shows (`effectiveLook`, `documentLook`): for each choice its own, else the workspace's, else the default; a value it does not know is none, a `doc.json` that does not read has the workspace's whole, and the 写作偏好 page has none at all, the workspace's not either; `gallery check` takes any of both. A new document has no look of its own, so it shows the workspace's. What it shows is never written in `doc.html`: `renderReportHtml` puts it on the page's `<html>` (`withLook`, after the kit's tags: `data-doc-font`, `data-doc-size`, `data-doc-spacing`, `data-doc-measure`, the names the page's description says, `LOOK_ATTRIBUTES` in `components/page.mjs`; `data-doc-width` is 全宽's and `data-doc-tables` 宽表格's, below), taking off whatever of them the file writes there, so the preview, 演示视图, a build, an export and its PDF all show it (each reading both files as it renders; the preview's page cache keeps a page under the look's word too, so a page asked for between a write and the watcher's word of it is never one of the old look), and the 写作偏好 page nothing. A copy of the view keeps it (the page's `runtime.state`), the workbench's 导入 of one takes it off with the rest of the view's state, and no import brings it into a new `doc.json`. The kit reads it through its tokens alone: `@kit-look;` in `styles/tokens.css` stands for rules of those attributes that `kit-build.mjs` writes from the tokens' own values (`src/render/look-css.mjs`), every value a whole pixel, so a script reading a token (`DocTokens`, the editor's `margin.mjs`) reads a length as ever, and a document with no look takes none of them (kit-diff: nothing of the default changed). 字体: 等线体 is the kit's `--doc-font` as it is; 衬线体 makes `--doc-font` the system's book serifs and Chinese serifs (`SERIF_FONT`: Iowan Old Style, Palatino Linotype, Palatino, Book Antiqua, Georgia, then Songti SC, Noto Serif SC, Noto Serif CJK SC, Source Han Serif SC, SimSun; nothing downloaded, the sidebar's Gallery wordmark set in the first of them) and `--doc-sans-font` what it was, which the kit's own controls keep (the display menu, a code block's language, a diff's counts: `--doc-font` itself is given that back on them, so what in them names `--doc-font` again, the display menu's card, takes the sans), and so do the page editor's own words (a conflict card, the Agent's changes bar, the page's notice, a size's label, a gap's badge: `var(--doc-sans-font, var(--doc-font, …))`); 混合体 makes `--doc-heading-font` the serif, which the title and every `h1`–`h4` take, of no weight (`:where()`: a document's own rule for its headings wins), the rest as it is. The generated rules give tokens values and name no element; the two rules that name elements stand after `@kit-look;` in `tokens.css`. Code, keyboard keys and formulas keep their own fonts in every choice. On Windows the Chinese serif is SimSun, which has no bold: a heading in it is set apart by its size (Songti SC on a Mac has its bold). 字体大小 changes the type alone: 15px, 16px or 18px body text, every font size of the text (small text, notes, the title, the four headings, a card's title, a table's) and its line height times that factor (`SIZE_FACTOR`: a line's height is its text's, kept in proportion; 14px set small text and captions at 11px, too small for Chinese; 17px was hard to tell from 16px); a code block keeps its 14px and a chart its axis. It changes nothing else: not the gaps between blocks or the margins a stylesheet writes in pixels (正文间距's: 12px between paragraphs at 18px as at 16px), nor the column's width (正文宽度's); until 2026-11-07 it took the gaps and the width with it, so that 大号 made the column wider, which the user did not want (the two coupled). 正文间距: each line height's leading (its height less its font size) at 0.8, 1 or 1.2 (body text on 24px, 26px or 28px lines at 16px; at 15px or 18px the size's leading so taken) and the gaps between blocks at 0.75, 1 or 1.25 (`SPACING`), whatever the size, with `--doc-space`, the factor the gaps are taken at, for the margins a stylesheet writes in pixels of its own: a heading's (`flow.css`: `calc(32px * var(--doc-space, 1))` above an h1), a list's top, the title's 32px below it. 正文宽度: the reading column's width, whatever the size (`WIDTH`): 偏窄 648px, 默认 712px (the kit's own `--doc-content-max-width`, unchanged), 偏宽 776px, the widths of 40, 44 and 48 Chinese characters of the default 16px body text (40.5, 44.5 and 48.5 times 16px: the characters were the user's way to fix the widths, never a count kept at another size), narrowing with a narrow window as ever. 全宽 (the top bar's 更多, under 目录, the user's of 2026-11-09: whatever the width set before it, the page full width; `full`, `"full"` or the default left out like any other choice) is a choice of its own beside 正文宽度, not a value of it, a field of the document's look beside its font and size: on, the page is at the full width of its area whatever 正文宽度 says, off, at the width it had, which `look` keeps beside it. It is the document's own, in `doc.json` with the rest of the look, so it is the same on every device and tab, survives a reload and is in an export (the choice of the reader's browser, as 目录's is, would be none of these); and it is the one way to be wide the kit has, no rule of the look's own: its attribute is `data-doc-width="full"`, the one `page.css`'s `.doc-page` rule (the page's width less `--doc-wide-gutter` either side), the page editor's room (`margin.mjs`: the outline's and the handle's) and the display menu's 全宽显示 read, so the reader's own switch in an exported file is the same attribute (a document whose look is full opens with it on; the reader switching it off holds until the page is opened again, the choice they keep in their browser being read for "full" alone). The workspace's default look may hold it, and 应用到全部文档 carries it with the rest of the look. 宽表格 (the top bar's 更多, under 全宽, the user's 表格跨越边界 of 2026-11-20; `tables`, `"wide"` or the default left out, off for every document that has none) is one more such choice of the document's own: its attribute `data-doc-tables="wide"`, which the kit's tables read ("Table layout internals", wide tables past the text) and the display menu's switch of the same name shows; an exported file opens with it as the document was, and the reader's switch there holds either way in their browser (`tables` of the display state, null until they choose: the file's look stands). So a size and a width are two rules of their own (`look-css.mjs`); the one rule of two attributes, a size with a spacing, gives the line heights, the one thing both change. The editor's measures that read the column (`margin.mjs`'s room and the head's early margin, a table's widths) read `--doc-content-max-width`, so they follow the width alone. A page takes a new look at once, never reloaded: a `doc-look-request` event (`{ look }`: the page's session's `look` command for the workbench, the preview's live reload, `look <word>`, the look the document shows now, when its `doc.json` was written, by the workbench or an Agent, or the workspace's `gallery.json`, which tells every open page; never while the `doc.json` does not read, an Agent's half written, whose next write tells, though a document with no `doc.json` at all, one made by hand, is told the workspace's it shows: `ownLookAt`) has `ui.js` put it on `<html>` and, when that changed anything, send `doc-layout-change` and `resize`, so what lays itself out by its text or width does it again (a table, whose measures are kept under the look too: `contextOf`; a flowchart, on `layout`; a chart, on its width; the page editor's overlays and its column's room, docs/editor.md "The outline and the right side"), then `doc-look-change` for the page's session. A look the file says (`from: "file"`, the live reload's) within 2s of one the page was asked for is held, the last of them shown once that time is over: it is the echo of an earlier write of the asker's (小号 clicked, then 大号 before 小号's write came back), older than what the page shows, and it never steps the page back. `gallery set <document> --font sans|serif|mixed --size small|default|large --spacing compact|default|loose --width narrow|default|wide --full on|off --wide-tables on|off [--all]` (`src/cli/set.mjs`) is the menu and the 全宽 and 宽表格 switches from the command line, by the workspace service's own `look` (one field a time: the choice named written into doc.json's `look`, every other own choice kept, a choice that is the default kept as a choice) and `looks` (`--all`: the look the document shows becomes the user-level default, `appearance.json` in the data directory, no document's `look` touched), through the shared server while one runs, so that an open page takes the look at once, else by the same functions in the command's own process. The values are `LOOK`'s (`--full on` is `"full"`, `--wide-tables on` `"wide"`, `off` the default).
- A table's numeric columns (`cells.js`: centered, tabular figures, sortable by their header's arrow) take numbers as tables write them: a qualifier (约, ≈, ~, <, ≥), a sign (a Unicode minus too), a currency, commas in thousands or ten-thousands or spaces, a decimal part, an exponent, a percent, a multiplier or a unit (千, 万 and 亿 scaling it), a range (its first number), an accountant's negative ((12)); full-width characters as their ASCII; a dash alone is a missing value. A sortable header's control (`sort.js`) is a small empty button after its words (`.doc-table-sort-button`, width 0: the header's words stay its own, only a press on the arrow sorts), kept in line with the headers whenever the table's content or its numeric reading changes (`makeSortable` syncs: a control on each sortable header, none on any other; an export takes them out). A press goes round three states (the user's 2026-11-20: the arrow had pointed down whatever was pressed): no sort, the rows as the file has them and the arrow showing both its triangles, one over the other, light (`data-doc-sort-arrow="none"`); ascending, the upward triangle alone, darker (`asc`, `aria-sort="ascending"`); descending, the downward one alone (`desc`); then no sort again, the rows put back in the order they had before the first press (rows put in since after the others). A number's first press is ascending too (it was descending); a header's `data-sort-default` still opens the table sorted that way, and the ring goes on from there. The arrow's `aria-label` says the state and what the next press does (`words.js`: 点击按升序排序, 当前升序，点击改为降序, 当前降序，点击取消排序), `aria-sort` follows the state (`none`, `ascending`, `descending`), and the status line a table names in `data-sort-status` says the sort or 原始顺序; the hung arrow sits 5px off the words (2px further out than it was: `styles/table.css`), in the cell's right padding, which a header with a control has 6px wider so that the arrow never reaches the cell's line (see "Table layout internals"). On the page editor's page (`html[data-ge-order="file"]`) the rows are the file's own, so a press is an edit: the kit asks for the rows in the new order with a cancelable `doc-table-sort-edit` event on the table (`detail.rows`, the body's rows in that order, and `detail.direction`, null for the first order put back, which the kit remembers from before the sort began and takes again as the rows stand when a sort begins), the editor writes them (one undo step, editor.md "Tables") and cancels it, and an editor that is not editing leaves the event alone, the kit then sorting the view as in a file. There the kit writes nothing else (no ranks, no status line, no key's state), keeps the arrow's state only while the rows are in its order (`sync` takes it back to no sort when an undo, a typed value or a row put in breaks the order), and `runtime/table-reading.js` takes the page's rows as the file's whatever `aria-sort` says. They are read unless the table or its frame carries `data-number-columns="off"` (the page editor's 识别数字列 off), read again when that attribute changes, and a column is not read as one when its header says it holds text or dates (`data-sort-type="text"` or `"date"`: dates written 10.16 and versions written 1.10 stay left-aligned, sorted as written). A footer's rows (`<tfoot>`: a total) are never read for which columns hold numbers, nor sorted (the body's rows move under it), but a footer's cell in a numeric column takes the column's look where it holds a number or nothing (a total centered under its column; words, and a cell spanning columns, stay as written), and the layout measures the footer's cells with the body's (`layout.js`), so a total wider than the column's numbers widens it. A cell may carry `doc-valign-top|middle|bottom` (the kit's own is top). A frame that scrolls down holds its scroll (`overscroll-behavior-block: none` while its layer has `data-y`: no bounce past its top or bottom on a touchpad), the wheel at its top or bottom scrolling the box around it or the page (`scrollbars.js passOn`; where that is at its end too, the frame's right thumb gives way: "Scrollbars giving way"); one that does not scroll down hands the wheel on as the browser does.
- A disclosure (`details.doc-details`) stands as far from the text around it as a paragraph does (`--doc-paragraph-gap`), two together a little closer. Block colors (the nine `doc-text-<color>` and `doc-highlight-<color>` classes, on the block itself) reach every kind that takes one (`COLORABLE`, docs/editor.md "Block colors"): a paragraph or heading colors its text or gets a background lying 2px past it, taking no room; a list item's text color is its marker's (`--doc-list-marker`) and its background lies behind it, its marker and the items it holds (two shadows, nothing moves); a disclosure's background takes the place of its panel's and its text color is its title's and its blocks'; a highlight panel's background replaces its gray (a text color gives it a thin border instead); a callout's is its fill taken 30% toward the page's background (`color-mix()` with `--doc-bg`: a little lighter than the color's own, the user's of 2026-11-07), its line that color's deep shade (its text color) as it is, the palette's blue when it has none (a text color is its words' only); a code block's background replaces `--doc-code-background` and its text color is that of the code the highlighter leaves plain; a formula block's color is its text's and its background has room at its sides; a file's and a bookmark's fill is the figure's, round as the row or card in it. The color class itself gives only the fill (`--doc-fill`, which does not inherit), its deep shade for a line along it (`--doc-fill-deep`, the color's text color, which does not inherit either: a callout's line), the fill of words in a line (`--doc-mark`, not inherited either, a step deeper than the block's fill: inline colors are kept apart from block colors, a highlight on words reads deeper than a block's background, and the user found the words' fill too faint on 2026-11-06) and the background (`styles/base.css`: a span's is `--doc-mark`, the format bar's 背景颜色 and an Agent's `<mark>`, every other host's `--doc-fill`); a kind's corners are its own, declared once where it is styled (a paragraph or heading 2px, a list item 4px, a file, a bookmark and a formula block 10px; a span square; the rest square or round as they are without a color), so a new kind that takes a color needs one declaration and no rule that undoes another's. A paragraph's or a heading's alignment and indent (`doc-align-center`, `doc-align-right`, `doc-indent-1` … `doc-indent-8`: `TEXT_ALIGN_CLASSES` of `src/shared/components/paragraph.mjs`, the page editor's 对齐与缩进, docs/editor.md "Alignment and indent") are drawn by `styles/flow.css`, on the block itself in any flow: its text centred or at the right (the left is neither), and the block moved in by a margin of two body characters a level (32px, whatever its own size), its background moving with its words; a cell's own `doc-align-*` stays the table's (`styles/table.css`); a table narrower than the text takes the same two classes on its frame (`div.doc-table-wrap`, or a bare `table`), placed by auto margins in `styles/table.css` at the left (neither), centred or at the right, as a paragraph's words are (the page editor's 对齐 ›).
- Flows: body text straight in the page (`main.doc-page`) or a Sheet panel, the content of a section, the summary, a highlight panel (`.doc-panel`), a callout (`.doc-callout`, its blocks spaced as a highlight panel's: `highlight.css`'s first and last margins), a disclosure, a dialog and a column (`.doc-column`), and of a plain `<div>` (no class) grouping blocks inside any of them but the page and a panel (there it is one block of its own). There is no wrapper for text (`.doc-prose` is gone; `gallery check` says to write the text straight in its flow). The flows are the components' descriptions' (`flow`, and the parts edited as flow: `FLOWS` in `src/shared/components/`, which the checks take as they are and `math.js` asks the runtime for, `DocKit.flows`); `styles/flow.css` spells the list once, as the `:where()` selector every rule of the flow nests in (native CSS nesting: `&` has no weight of its own, so the rules still have none), and a unit test holds it to the descriptions' `FLOW_BLOCKS`, the same list as one selector, the flows and a plain `div` grouping blocks in one (the runtime gives an item its line by it, below). A container's first and last block take no outer margin in a column, a disclosure's content and a detail card whatever the block, and a Sheet panel's last block none below it (a framed panel's room under its blocks is its 16px padding alone; its first block keeps its margin under the tabs, a heading's too), in a highlight panel only text does (a component there keeps its own margins). Nested lists in a flow take their markers by depth, whatever kind of list they hang from: bullets disc, circle, square, numbers decimal, lower-alpha, lower-roman, round again every three levels (eight levels deep, zero specificity). Their color is `--doc-list-marker`, a blue of their own (`light-dark(#2e7cd1, #5492d4)`: the user's #2e7cd1 of 2026-11-07, a purer blue than the palette's #387dc9 the links take, and in dark the same hue at the dark palette's lightness, where the palette's #5e87c9, theirs until then, leans gray; `tests/unit/clipboard.mjs` holds it), a list item's text color in its place. The lists the kit draws the markers of are a flow's own lists without a class, and those straight in a flowchart's step (its content, counted from the first depth again, as in a highlight panel), with the lists their items hold; no depth runs on through a flowchart (the rules nest in that list selector, `&` the list; a chain's `:is(ul, ol:not(.doc-flow))`). Three promises hold in every flow, so blocks can be written, and moved on the page, anywhere in the document without a wrapper changing how anything looks: (1) body text reads the same: the paragraph, heading, list, quote, rule and code rules reach a flow's own blocks (child combinators) and the text structure inside them (list items, a quote's paragraphs, strong), and the headings, quotes and dividers a list item holds after its text look as in the flow (the list's gap above each and none below, none at a quote's or a divider's sides), with zero specificity (`:where()`), so any component or document class wins; (2) a component (card, table, code block, disclosure, document widget) looks the same in any flow, because the flow rules stop at its edge; (3) formulas render (`math.js` scans every flow; a container with `data-doc-math-dollar` renders first, on its own) and code blocks are highlighted (`code.js` takes every `div.doc-code`). Verified in a real browser by rendering the same blocks in a section, a column and a disclosure and comparing the computed styles of every element (only the disclosure's indent differs), and by `kit-diff.mjs` over the Component Catalog after each change of the kit. Not part of `kit:check` (jsdom does not cascade styles).
- First paint: `styles/base.css` keeps `body` (when it holds `.doc-page`) at `visibility: hidden` until `ui.js` sets `html[data-doc-ready]` on the animation frame after `DOMContentLoaded`. By then the stored theme and width are applied, the document's inline script has mounted Sheets and created charts, and the kit's `DOMContentLoaded` work (formulas, tables, code) has run, so the page appears once in its final layout instead of flashing the first Sheet panel or jumping. A CSS animation reveals the page after 1.2 s if that never happens (a script error, or no `ui.js`). The room below the last block is 30% of the window's height (64px at least, 64px in print), so the end of a document scrolls up toward the middle. The top padding that makes room for the shared controls is fixed (52px, 60px at 760px and below) rather than switched on by `ui.js`, which used to move every page down by 12–24px after the first paint. The browser export removes `data-doc-ready`, so an exported copy holds its first paint too.
- Type (`styles/tokens.css`): each role has a size and a line height as a token pair: the body (`--doc-body-*`) with `--doc-paragraph-gap` between paragraphs, four heading levels (`--doc-h1-*` … `--doc-h4-*`; the fourth is the body's size, set apart by its weight, and all are 700), the document title (`--doc-title-*`), card titles (`--doc-card-title-*`, 16px on 22), document notes (`--doc-note-*`), compact figure captions (`--doc-small-*`) and the table (`--doc-table-*`: a header cell and a body cell read the same pair, normal weight). Bold is 700 (the body's: a `<strong>`, a cell's `.doc-bold`, a step's own words), and so are the title, the four headings and a card's title (a design review, 2026-11-17: at 600 Windows drew the Chinese of a heading Bold, Microsoft YaHei having no 600, and its Latin and figures Segoe UI Semibold, a level lighter beside them; at 700 both are Bold, `Doc Segoe`'s 700 face); their tracking is their size's, tighter the larger: the title −.02em, h1 and h2 −.015em, h3 −.01em, h4 and a card's title none (−.025em on every level before). Where the system has PingFang SC the family's own weights apply, and it has no Bold, so there 700 reads as its Semibold, as the headings did. The kicker over the title (`.doc-kicker`, 600), a disclosure's title (500) and the cards of a custom page keep their own weights. The text is `#2d2d2d` on a white page (`--doc-text`, `--doc-bg` `#ffffff`, the same white as `--doc-surface`; until 2026-11-06 `#1d1d1f` on `#fcfcfc`, until 2026-11-08 `#2c2c2b`, which the editor's own words (`--ge-tx`) and the workbench keep); in dark `#f3f3f3` on `#181818`, unchanged. On an Apple platform (macOS, iOS, iPadOS) the light text is `#262626` and the body's type is smoothed (`-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased`, which a document in the workbench's iframe does not inherit from the workbench's frame): the user's of 2026-11-08, the type being drawn thinner there. `runtime/index.js` judges the platform as the page opens (`navigator.userAgentData.platform`, else `navigator.platform`, against `/mac|iphone|ipad/i`: Chrome says "macOS" there) and puts `data-doc-platform="mac"` on `<html>`, or takes the mark off; `tokens.css` and `base.css` hold the two rules under `:root[data-doc-platform="mac"]`. It is judged where the page is opened, never when it is made, since an exported file is read on another machine: a copy of the view ("导出当前视图副本") lets the mark go with `data-doc-ready` (`page.mjs`'s runtime root attrs), and the copy's own kit sets it again. The page is held hidden until `data-doc-ready`, so the color never flashes from one to the other. `src/shared/colors.mjs`'s `BODY.text` is the default (`#2d2d2d`) and `tests/unit/clipboard.mjs` holds it to `:root`'s value; the rest drawn from the text's old color (borders, grids, scrollbars: 8% to 42% of it, where the three shades cannot be told apart), the code block's text (`--doc-code-foreground`) and the workbench's own colors are unchanged. The lines drawn from the text (`--doc-border`, `--doc-grid`, the scrollbar thumbs) are it at a strength, 12%, 8%, 24% and 42% in light. The workbench writes the page's color behind its frame (`--pageBac`) to the same value. The font is PingFang SC first (`--doc-font`, the user's feedback of 2026-11-06): where the system has it (macOS, iOS) the whole text, Chinese, Latin and figures, is set in it, whatever the page's `lang` (with the system's UI font first, Chrome took PingFang for Chinese alone and Safari chose the Chinese face by the page's language); elsewhere the system's UI font, on Windows Segoe UI as the system has it under the kit's alias `Doc Segoe`, `@font-face` rules in `styles/tokens.css` over its own regular, semibold and bold faces, nothing downloaded, with `ascent-override: 100%` and `descent-override: 25%` so a line's ink sits in the middle of its line box (Segoe UI's own metrics leave room above its letters, and the text, Chinese on the same baseline, sat a pixel low in a heading's selection fill and a colored block's background), and Microsoft YaHei for Chinese; on Linux the alias finds no face and the stack goes on as before. Links are the palette's blue in every block (`--doc-link`, `.doc-text-blue`'s `light-dark(#387dc9, #5e87c9)`, the format bar's 蓝色; `tests/unit/clipboard.mjs` holds it to `src/shared/colors.mjs`) with no line under them but under the pointer, a thin one there (`--doc-link-line`, the link's own blue, 1px, .2em below the text; `a:where(:hover)`, which weighs what `a` does, so a link of a look of its own, a bookmark, a file, a detail card's, never takes it): the user's of 2026-11-07, where until then they were gray on a line always shown. A link shown with its icon and a page block (`a.doc-link-mention`, mention.css) keep that look: the muted gray (`--doc-mention`) on a thin gray line (`--doc-mention-line`, .05em) under the pointer as anywhere. Inline code is red text on a faint fill (`--doc-code-inline`, `--doc-code-inline-fill`), 85% monospace, 4px radius, raised .065em, with no margin of its own: an ordinary space parts it from a word beside it, the text's own, the one the page editor types there or the one `runtime/spacing.js` puts in where the text has none (its padding keeps a caret beside it clearly outside); code blocks keep their own colors. Underlined text (`u`) has a 2px line .3em below the baseline, clear of Chinese characters, in inline code's red. An underline's line may take another color and another weight with classes of the `u` itself: `doc-underline-{orange,blue,purple,plain}` ("plain" is the text's own color; light and dark) and `doc-underline-{thin,thick}` (1px, 3px). Inline formulas are 1.21em of their text (KaTeX's own size reads small beside Chinese).
- Columns (分栏, `styles/columns.css`): `.doc-columns` is a flex row of 2 to 5 `.doc-column`, 20px apart (`--doc-column-gap`), each `flex: calc(100 × share) 1 0%` with `min-width: 0`, so the columns divide the row less its gaps in proportion to their shares (`--doc-column-ratio`, `@property … inherits: false`; unset, 1 each: equal); a column's first block has no top margin and its last no bottom one. Below 640px the row is a column of its columns, each the full width. A card in a column fills it. (The card row, `.doc-grid`, is gone: `gallery check` names columns instead.)
- Sizes (`styles/sizes.css`): a card or table wrapper may carry sizes as custom properties in its `style` attribute: `--doc-span` (its share of the width it stands in, the reading width or its column's; a picture's share of the reading width wherever it stands, `100cqw` of `.doc-page`, at most the width it stands in: a picture's width is kept in pixels, so one moved into a column keeps its size, never wider than the column; registered with `@property … inherits: false`, so it is never a nested block's), `--doc-chart-height` (read by `charts.js` as the plot's height, a chart's `height` in its draw) and `--doc-table-height` (the wrapper's `max-height`; unset, the kit's: `min(72vh, 720px)` for a long table of data, none for one shown whole, "Table layout internals"). `--doc-span-width: calc(var(--doc-span) * 100%)` is invalid while no span is set, so `var(--doc-span-width, default)` gives the default without a class or attribute selector. A table wrapper keeps its natural width unless it has a span; with one, `tables.js` fills it instead of setting the wrapper's width. A wrapper never goes past its container: with a span over 1 it stays at the reading width and `tables.js` allocates the columns over that many times the width, so the table scrolls sideways inside it, the kit's usual wide table. `charts.js` and `tables.js` also redraw on a `doc-layout-change` window event, which the editor sends after a size change.
- Block spacing has no single rhythm: each kind of block stands a measured distance from the text around it, and each distinct distance is one token in `styles/tokens.css` that every component's stylesheet reads (a change of one is a change of its token). Above and below unless said: `--doc-component-gap` 20px for a card (chart card, figure), a table wrapper, a callout, a row of columns, the state widgets (fields, a filter and its row) and a divider (`hr`); `--doc-block-gap` 16px for a highlight panel and the opening summary, a code block and a diff (from the text around them), a Sheet frame, a quote, an instance of a custom block, whatever its state (`styles/installed.css`; a custom block's own style may change it), and below a list (10px above it); `--doc-media-gap` 14px for an image, a bookmark and an embed (a file row 10px: its own 4px padding makes up the rest); `--doc-paragraph-gap` 12px below a paragraph and a paragraph of the header, above and below small text and a disclosure; `--doc-group-gap` 8px between two disclosures in a row, two code blocks (or diffs) in a row, and for a formula block (which also has 8px of padding of its own inside); `--doc-list-gap` 5px between the items of a list, from an item to the list it holds and from a list to one right after it. Prose headings own their spacing (`styles/flow.css`: more above than below, less as the level goes down: 32, 28, 24 and 18px above an h1, h2, h3 and h4, 10, 8, 8 and 8px under, each times `--doc-space`; from a design review, taken in at the user's measure of 2026-11-17, "可以多留，但不用留太多": 26, 22, 20 and 16 above before, the proposal's 36, 32, 26 and 20 too much). Two rules after them: a heading right under a heading of a level above it is its sub-heading, 12px under it (`h1 + h2`, `h2 + h3`, `h3 + h4`…; two headings of one level are two sections and keep their room), and a framed block right under a heading is the heading's own, a paragraph's gap above it (`--doc-paragraph-gap`, 12px: a card, a table's frame, a callout, columns, a Sheet frame, a highlight panel, code, a diff, a flowchart, an image, a bookmark, an embed, a quote; weighted (a class and an element), so it wins over the block's own margin, and so over a document's own class of one class on that block too, the one rule of the flow with any weight; a file's row is not one, its 10px and its own 4px of padding that gap already), where its own 16 or 20px had made it stand as far from its heading as from the paragraph before the heading; a section's title is one of those headings and the line under it that says what the section is an ordinary paragraph, spaced by the same rules as anywhere (the heading's 8px below it, the paragraph's 12px after it): there is no wrapper for the two (`.doc-section-header` is gone, and `gallery check` names the heading with a paragraph below it as its replacement), so the title has no gap of its own and nothing in the kit or the editor knows a section's title as anything but a heading.
