- A table is lines on the page: the wrapper's 1px frame with an 8px radius (`--doc-table-radius`) and a 1px line between every two rows and every two columns, all one color (`--doc-hairline`: `#e6e5e3` light; in dark a light veil, `rgb(255 255 240 / 10%)` (`#2f2f2e` on the page's background, `--doc-hairline-solid`; 13.9% until 2026-11-17, `#383836` there: the design review of 2026-11-17, the dark document's lines and grays at the light one's strength) and that much lighter on any fill, so a line between header cells of the palette's gray, or the frame's line where it passes over them beside a gray highlight block, still shows; in a block with a fill of its own, a highlight block of its own gray or any block of the palette's background colors, the frame's line is opaque, `--doc-table-frame-filled`, `#41413f` in dark, the veil as it lies on the palette's gray: the frame lies over the table's body, whose fill is the page's, where the veil comes out the gray of the block around it); the table has no fills of its own (`--doc-table-fill`, the page's background). A header cell (`th`) looks like any other cell, in the same size, weight and line height; the kit only makes it stick, filled with the table's fill when it has none of its own. Its look is a cell's look like any other: `.doc-bold` (weight 700, the words typed in it later too) and `.doc-highlight-gray` (`#f0efed` light, `#2c2c2b` dark, `#383836` until 2026-11-17, opaque as every cell fill, so it sticks over the cells under it), the look the page editor's 标题行 gives (its 标题列 gives none: a header column, each body row's first cell a `th scope="row"`, only sticks; one a file has with the look keeps it). Borders are separate: each cell draws its own line on its right and at its bottom, so a cell that sticks while the frame scrolls takes its lines along and a merged cell has them where it ends; the frame and the table are one: the table fills the wrapper to its edge, the wrapper's line is an outline on its inner edge (`outline-offset: -1px`, painted over the table), drawn instead, once the kit runs, by its scrollbar layer (`.doc-scrollbars-frame`, as big as the frame: a header row or column that sticks is above the outline; the outline is then not drawn, and comes back on paper, where the layer is hidden), and its rounded corners clip the table. Every line is drawn once, for a veil drawn over another would be lighter there: in a frame the last column's right lines and the last row's bottom lines are the frame's line and draw none of their own (the row's last cell, unless a cell of the last column reaches down past its row: such a table keeps them, from the first paint; and in a table with a cell spanning rows the kit marks the cells on those edges, which only the table's slots tell, `data-doc-edge` "right", "bottom" or both, `tables/edges.js`, again when its content changes: a cell spanning down to the last row, the last column's cells beside one spanning in it) (no border of its own, so nothing stands between them; a table outside a wrapper draws its top and left edges itself). The header row sticks at the top of a frame that scrolls down, a header column (each body row's first cell a `th scope="row"`) at its left when it scrolls sideways, the cell where they meet at both. A cell left empty is one line high (`:empty::after`, a zero-width space, as an empty text block in a flow), so a row of empty cells is as tall as a row of words from the first paint on. A cell may hold a list (the page editor's 单元格里的列表): the browser's markers, its items 2px apart, no margin at the cell's edges; the column measurer takes each item as a line of its own, indented as the list, and a formula (KaTeX's `.katex`) as wide as it is drawn, not its TeX. Sideways a frame never bounces at its ends nor hands the scroll on (`overscroll-behavior-inline: none`: a touchpad's bounce pulled the table off the frame's edge); down it hands the scroll on to the page. A table's scope line is small text above it (`p.doc-note`, its unit after a `span.doc-tab`); `.doc-table-meta`, which older files write right above the frame, reads as small text too (`styles/table.css`): its words, code and bold one line, never columns, and after a plain span (no class) its last plain element (a span, or the unit in bold) at the line's right end as small text's tab, plain spans between 18px apart; below 640px that last one is a line of its own.
- The neutral fills, three steps of one warm gray, and the hairline are tokens written once (`styles/tokens.css`: `--doc-hover` 5% under the pointer, `--doc-hover-row` 7% for a row of a menu card under the pointer or the keys, `--doc-press` 10% pressed, `--doc-hairline`): the display settings' rows and button, a choice's rows, the sheet tabs, a file's and a bookmark's card, a code block's buttons and inline code's fill, a table's lines, a Sheet's frame and an embed's border all read them, so that changing the gray again is one edit. The kit's controls and what floats take the workbench's and the editor's numbers (the finish of 2026-11-17): a control's corners 8 (`--doc-radius-control`: the plain button, a select, the display button, a filter's buttons, 允许访问, the picture viewer's close), a menu card's 14 with 5 inside it and its rows' 9 (`--doc-radius-card`, `--doc-card-pad`, `--doc-radius-row`: the display menu, a choice), a detail card's shell 16 (`--doc-radius-shell`), a card's shadow (`--doc-shadow-card`: its 1px outline at today's strength, never half a pixel, which smears at 125%, a close shadow and a soft deep one). Under the pointer a fill comes at once and goes over 100ms ease-out; pressed it is a step darker at once; nothing scales. A switch (全宽显示) is the product's one size, 30 × 18 with a 14px knob that widens by 3px while pressed, its track `--doc-switch-track` off and `--doc-control-blue` on (`#4a81d4`, `#4f7fcb` dark: calmer than the accent, a checked to-do's blue too), moving over 160ms (`--doc-ease-sheet`). The tooltip, the sheet tabs, the summary panel, tables, code, callouts and quotes keep their looks.
- Six light/dark series pairs (`--doc-series-1` … `6`); semantic tones are tokens of their own (`--doc-success` → 3, `--doc-danger` → 5 “Cursor red”, `--doc-warning` → 2, `--doc-info` → 1, `--doc-neutral` → muted), and `DocTokens.read().tone(name)` reads `--doc-<name>`, so a document that overrides one changes the chart colors too. The opening summary and a highlight panel are a light gray panel (`styles/highlight.css`: `#f1f1ef`, `#2a2a29` dark, `#383836` until 2026-11-17, when the dark summary was the brightest slab on a page's first screen); a block color replaces it.
- Code blocks (`styles/code.css`): a soft rounded panel with a translucent fill (`--doc-code-background`, following the theme) and no border, a header (the fold arrow, the title in body text's size and line height, 5px above and below its words as a disclosure's title, `--doc-block-title-padding`, an excerpt's range of lines beside it in the same size and line: 第 120–139 行 from `data-line-start` and the line count, a line break ending the code ending its last line as in the editor, not one more, the toolbar shown while the pointer is over the block or the focus is in it, a folded block's too, its language and copy with no bar, a diff's counts always) above numbered lines in monospace (`--doc-code-font`; numbered from 1, or from the block's `data-line-start`, an excerpt numbered as in its file: `src/shared/code-lines.mjs`, read alike by `code.js` and the editor's gutter, its numbers as wide as the last one; `data-folded-lines` counts the block's lines from 1 all the same); the colors are the `--doc-code-*` tokens, the highlighter's twelve `--doc-code-token-*` among them. A frame that scrolls keeps the room under its last line clear of its bottom edge when the caret or a focus is brought into view (`scroll-padding-block` on the pre and the diff's body, a little more than their padding: the caret's box is a few pixels short of its line), so the caret on the last line takes the code to its very end. At 420px or below a block shows its code whole (no frame height but one of its own), its title wraps and its toolbar lies over the header's end.
- Detail cards (详情卡片, `styles/dialog.css`, opened by `dialogs.js`): a modal card holding nothing but its blocks (no title bar, no line, no close button; a heading first in it is an ordinary block), the workbench's settings dialogs' card (写作偏好, `.pref-dialog`, a centred dialog; the user's word of 2026-11-06, which retired the card that was the reading column and 40px each side, as tall as its content), a little smaller than 写作偏好's 880 by 86% (the user's word of 2026-11-07): `--doc-dialog-width` (760px) wide and `--doc-dialog-height` (80% of the window, at most 760px) high whatever it holds, at least `--doc-dialog-margin` (24px; 12px at 640px or below, where a tap above or below it closes it on a phone) from the window's sides (the window less a scrollbar's room, given to the page's padding while it is open), in the middle of the window, the same while edited and for a reader. In a workbench that window is the workbench's, as for its own dialogs (组件列表, 写作偏好), not the document's frame right of the sidebar and under the top bar: the editor's session (`src/editor/page/session.mjs`, `centreCards`) says where the workbench's window's middle falls in the page's (`--doc-dialog-centre-x`, `--doc-dialog-centre-y`, from the frame's place: `window.frameElement`, of the same origin) and how high that window is (`--doc-dialog-window-height`, which the 80% is of), in a sheet the document adopts, so none of it is in the page's markup (a copy of the view) or the file, said again as a card opens or closes and as the frame changes size while a card is open (the window, the sidebar opened or closed), never while none is (a new rule on the root is a style recalculation of the whole page: the sidebar dragged or sliding changes the frame's place every frame, and on a page of 8,000 elements that was 40ms a frame, the user's 2026-11-23 drag lagging behind the pointer; the card's opening is heard before it is drawn, so its first frame has the rule); `.doc-dialog:modal` moves the card that far from the frame's middle by its insets (and so does `.doc-dialog:not([open])`, the card closed and fading in the top layer, which is no `:modal` then: a card moved toward a wide workbench's middle jumped back to the frame's, 87px across and 22 down, at full opacity, and faded there, 2026-11-17; shut, it is not shown) (the box it is centred in, as large as the frame, shifted; never by a transform, which would make the card the containing block of the editor's fixed tools in it), clamped so that it stays `--doc-dialog-margin` clear of the frame's sides: where the frame is too narrow or low for the window's middle, the card stands as near it as that leaves it (in the middle of the frame where the frame has no more room than the card needs), never cut off. The workbench covers its own bars meanwhile, the whole window under the frame, whose edge it puts on a whole device pixel (docs/workbench.md, `card`). Unsaid (a page on its own, an export, 演示视图), the card is in the middle of its own window; a shell's 16px radius (`--doc-radius-shell`, the workbench's dialogs' since 2026-11-17; 12px before), the page's surface (a chart card in it takes that surface for its ground: `--doc-figure-ground`), the shadow of a window that takes the screen (light and dark: `0 24px 48px` at 24%, `0 4px 12px` at 14%, the 1px line; deeper than a menu's, so that the card keeps an edge on the dimmed page), its backdrop the settings' overlay (`rgb(15 15 15 / 60%)`, dark 80%, plain colors, as a `::backdrop` inherits nothing in some browsers: the workbench's `.card-underlay` takes `--overlay`, so the two are one color), the card and its backdrop coming and going as one fade (a design review, 2026-11-17, as the workbench's dialogs): in over 180ms, out over 140ms, on a curve that moves at once and settles (`--doc-ease-out`), never moved or scaled (a scaled card's text shimmers at 125%); closed by Esc, a press outside or `close()`, it stays in the top layer while it fades (`display` and `overlay` transitioned with `allow-discrete`; a browser without that closes it at once), the pointer passing through it, and the page is unlocked only once it has faded (`dialogs.js` waits the closed card's transition), so the page's scrollbar does not come back beside the fading backdrop; where motion is reduced it fades all the same, over the same times (a fade moves nothing: the workbench's dialogs and every menu keep theirs so; since 2026-11-18, at once before). In a workbench the cover over its bars fades in over the same 180ms on the same curve, and out over the same 140ms as the card closes, from the time the card's own fade started (the session's `cardAt`, which the page says once the browser has started it, a card's first frame taking a while to draw: heard sooner, the cover had started 40 to 60ms after the backdrop, the window dimming and lighting again in two steps, 2026-11-17), the document area kept over it till then (docs/workbench.md, `card`), so the two stay one dimming. Its text is the reading column, centred, at most 712px, at least `--doc-dialog-pad` (48px; 16px at 640px or below) inside its sides (`--doc-dialog-inset`, the larger of the two and half of what the column leaves); what is taller scrolls in `.doc-dialog-content`, whose scrollbar where the system's takes room (a fine pointer) is a thin one of a known width (`--doc-dialog-scrollbar`, 10px; `::-webkit-scrollbar`, as a formula's), always kept (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`) and taken from the padding at its side, so the text is as wide with it and without it; a touch screen's floats over the text. Meanwhile `<html>` carries `doc-dialog-open` (`overflow: hidden`, on screen only: it prints as the page, with no card) and, where the page had a scrollbar, `doc-dialog-gutter`: the room the scrollbar took becomes the page's padding on its right (`dialogs.js` says how wide, `--doc-page-bar`, in a sheet the document adopts, so none of it is in the markup), not kept as the scrollbar's as under a picture opened large (`scrollbar-gutter`, which a browser with no constructed sheets keeps): kept, it is outside the window a backdrop covers, and a strip of the page's color stood bright at the window's right edge beside the dimmed page (the user's 2026-11-13). So the backdrop reaches the window's edge, and nothing on the page moves as a card opens or closes: across, the card is sized and centred in the window less that room (`--doc-dialog-across`, its right inset that much more), and what is fixed against the window's right side stands in by as much (the display button, `.doc-display`; the editor's outline, its strip and card, its bottom panel `.ge-panel`, and, centred, its toast and the bar of an Agent's changes), each with `var(--doc-page-bar, 0px)`. It closes by Esc or a press outside it (on its backdrop: on the press, so selecting its text out past its edge keeps it open), the focus going back to what opened it; these are the document's listeners (the backdrop's mousedown, `close` heard on its way down), so a card added later or put back works the same. As it opens, `dialogs.js` names it (`aria-label`: its first heading, else the words that open it, unless the file names it itself; a mark of the kit's, which an export of the view takes off) and gives the focus to its first editable text where the page is being edited, else to the content (`tabindex="-1"`, the same), at its top, to be scrolled by keys, where `showModal` would give it to the first link in it, wherever that stood. The content (padding 32px above, 40px below; 20px and 28px at 640px or below) keeps that padding clear of the card's edges when something is brought into view (`scroll-padding-block`: 32px above, 48px below, the caret's box being a few pixels short of its line): the caret in its last block, a block made at the end or ↓ into it takes the content to its very end, the padding under it shown, where the browser stopped with the block against the card's bottom edge. (While it is edited, a component as its last block, which holds room of its own under its last line, is taken to the end by the editor: `showEnd()` in docs/editor.md.)
- Disclosures (`styles/details.css`) are drawn as a code block is: one design for the two. A soft rounded panel (`--doc-code-background`), a header row with a small filled triangle that turns from right to down when it opens (the code block's own arrow), its title (body text's size and line height, as a code block's title, 5px above and below, `--doc-block-title-padding`, the code block's too: the label's own padding, on the text one edits, so in the page editor a press beside the words is in the title and only the arrow's column opens and closes the block; the arrow in the middle of its first line) and, under it, an indented content region (a flow). Disclosure blocks, columns, and nested table wrappers use `min-width: 0; max-width: 100%` so a table's min-content width cannot widen the page.
- Flowcharts (流程图, `ol.doc-flow`: `styles/flowchart.css`, `runtime/flowchart.js`) are lists whose items are steps. Each step is a node, a container as a highlight panel is, drawn as a card (1px `--doc-border`, 8px round, `--doc-surface`, padding 10px 14px around its blocks and 42px left of them; a word too long for it breaks inside it, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`): its number in a 20px circle of `--doc-flow-badge` on the first line's middle (`counter(list-item)`: from the list's `start`; the middle is `--doc-flow-mid`, half the step's own line height worked out on the step, a registered length; a number of three digits a pill growing to the left, its right edge 8px from the words; no rule of a list's markers in `flow.css` reaches it, where the flowchart stands in a list item too), or its icon there instead (a lead `div.doc-icon`, a highlight panel's kinds: an emoji 16px, a picture or an icon of a set in its color 20px; `li:has(> .doc-icon)::before` is `content: none`, so Tabler's number icons show a number of their own that nothing counts); then its blocks: its first a paragraph, its title (`<p><strong>…</strong></p>`, bold by the `<strong>` alone: the kit puts no weight on the paragraph, so a title un-bolded reads as body text), on the number's line (no gap above it, after the icon too; an empty one, `<p><strong></strong></p>`, a line high), the blocks after it its content at the body's weight (a heading its own), spaced as any list item's blocks (`flow.css`: the list's gap above each), a component (a picture, a table, code, a callout, a disclosure, a formula block, a flowchart) 8px under what comes before it and none below whatever its own margins, and the step's last block none below it, whatever it is (the card's padding is the room under its content, as a highlight panel's is), a list there of any kind with the kit's markers from the first depth again (a numbered list 1., its nested lists a., i.; a to-do's boxes), a paragraph body text in the step's color, a picture, a code block, a flowchart (cards in a card, 8px under the words above it). A step written the short way, its own words before its blocks (`<li>收集数据<p>…</p></li>`, which the editor's natural HTML rewrites as a node), is drawn the same: its words at the title's weight (700, the li's own: a `<strong>`'s), its blocks at 400, the list's gap above its first block (a rule of the runtime's: the stylesheet cannot tell its words are there). The steps stand in rows that fill the flowchart's width, 28px between two steps of a row and 34px between rows: `runtime/flowchart.js` works them out (`flowLayout()`) and writes them into one stylesheet of its own, constructed and adopted by the document (`document.adoptedStyleSheets`), each flowchart's rules under its `data-doc-flow-key` (a runtime attribute on the list, the only thing the kit writes there; an export of the current view takes it off, and the copy's kit lays the flowchart out again; nothing is written on the steps, so the page editor sees no change of them): `flex-flow: row wrap` on the list, each step's basis its share of its row less the row's gaps, a pixel less, given back by its growth (so a row is full and the next step cannot join it), its share its weight among the row's (`--doc-flow-ratio` on the step, a positive number: a step with none weighing as the row's weighted ones on the whole, equal where none has one, one alone in its row all of it). Without the kit's rules, and for a flowchart of one step a row, the stylesheet's own column. Where a row ends is the author's (`data-direction` on the list for every joint, `row` or `column`, and on a step for the joint after it, the step's own winning) or the kit's (自动, neither saying): a step goes on the row of the one before it while the row reads well (counted at equal widths: the weights a reader dragged change no row, they count only where a card would be narrower than 116px or its widest word, so a row made wider or narrower stays the row it was and a narrow card is as tall as its content makes it), five at most a row in a flowchart 520px wide at least, each card at least 116px wide (five fit the 712px reading width) and as wide as its widest word needs (its title's and its content's, a list's at its indent), each title on two lines at most and no card taller than it is wide, its content included (the words laid out at the card's width as the browser breaks them, `linesOf()`, a long run (`src/shared/long-runs.mjs`) at any of its letters, each its own piece, as is every run of a block that breaks anywhere (`word-break: break-all`: one the page editor marks), an ordinary word one piece (`piecesOf`), the card's least width still its widest whole word, the title, then each block of the content, a paragraph's or a heading's lines, a list's items with what each holds at the list's indent, a quote's paragraphs, a divider's line, `cardHeight()`, the margins between two blocks collapsing as the browser's do, a block with no box, an empty media block a reader is not shown, left out; the fonts measured with a canvas, a title in the bold it is drawn in, inline code in its own font with its padding (`http://localhost:5180` in a step's paragraph is as wide as its monospace draws it, so a row that cannot hold it whole wraps rather than break it inside a word); a step holding any other block, a picture, a table, code, a disclosure or a flowchart, reads beside no other, so it takes a row of its own); else the next step starts a row. `column` ends the row; `row` joins whatever the count and the lengths, as long as each card of the row keeps 116px and its widest word, else the row ends there all the same (a row too narrow for its cards wraps; it never falls back to one step a row). What the layout goes by (five, 116px, 28px, 520px, two lines: `FLOW_ROW`) and how a row's weights become its shares (`flowShares()`) are the list description's (`src/shared/components/list.mjs`), which the page editor's gaps go by too (a node dragged narrower stops where the kit would wrap it). An arrow (a 1.5px line and an open chevron, `--doc-flow-line`) leads from each step to the next, the step's `::after`, never text: across on the titles' line to the next step of its row (clipped to `--doc-flow-right`, a polygon), down the numbers' column to a step right under it (the default: a step alone in its row, the next row's first under it), or, from a row's last step with steps before it, turning: down half the gap between the rows, across, and down into the next row's first step, the number's (or icon's) column to the other's, its two corners 6px round (`--doc-flow-turn-shape`: a `shape()` outline the box is clipped to, as the arrows across and down are clipped to their polygons, so all three draw equally crisp and of one weight; where a browser does not take `shape()`, `--doc-flow-turn`: two mask layers drawn in CSS pixels, softer where the box stands between pixels; either way the turn reaches as far as its box; the box's left and width a `calc()` of the step's own width, `reachOf()`, so the turn holds at any width the row is drawn at). Its layout has no print rule (the user's decision of 2026-10-25: a flowchart prints as it is shown): print draws the rows, widths, directions and arrows a screen as wide as the paper draws, its widths shares and its turns a `calc()` of the steps' own widths. Printed by the browser itself (Ctrl+P, a PDF it prints), the kit works the layout out again as the print media comes (`matchMedia("print")` changing: Chrome has laid the page out at the paper's width, in its pages, by then, and prints what the listener leaves; a browser's print once kept the rows worked out for its window, a row of five 98px cards on A4), and laid out for the screen again after; the workbench's PDF lays the page out at the paper's width before it prints (docs/workbench.md, "PDF"), so 自动 measures where it prints either way (scenarios `flowPrint`, `flowPrintPages`). On paper a flowchart that fits on a page is kept on one (`@media print { … break-inside: avoid }`, a rule of the runtime's: one drawn at most 840px tall, `PAGE`, a Letter page's height inside its 1-inch margins, the shortest the workbench's PDF prints on, a little to spare), so no arrow from a row's end stands at a page's foot pointing at nothing or is cut by its edge (its mask anchored at a fragment's foot drew it awry); one taller breaks between its rows where it stands, no page left empty before it, and printed by the browser the arrow a page's break parts from the row it leads to is left out (`pagedApart()`: a row whose next one does not stand the 34px row gap under it, as the page is laid out in pages; `flowRules()` `cut`, a `content: none` on that step's `::after`; in the print pass, a flowchart kept on one page that a break still parts is taken for one taller than a page). Its margins have no weight (`:where`), so a highlight panel's trim of its first and last block reaches it as any text block's. The list is named 流程图 for screen readers when the author gave it no `aria-label`. A step's background fills its card; a text color colors its words, its content's (a block's own color its own), its number (on a tint of it) and its border. Steps coming, going or moving after the page was drawn (a node the editor adds, deletes or drags; before steps were nodes, an item its Tab put in the step before it, after which Chrome kept the next steps' old numbers) have the numbers drawn again: the kit sets `data-doc-flow-recount` on the list for a moment (its numbers `content: none`) and works the style out, so only the numbers are drawn anew and the page's height and scroll position never change. The page editor wraps its selection and frame around a step's card (`--doc-box-radius`, `--doc-box-reach` on it: 8px and 4px), and the fill of a whole flowchart selected around its cards' corners (the same two on the list), alone or among other blocks.
- **Cards** (the card tokens, `styles/tokens.css` "Cards"; the look of the official pack 「Gallery 风格」, drawn to its mockup of 2026-11-09, which replaced the first cards of the same day): one language for a custom page's cards, which Gallery's own blocks draw in and an Agent's block may (skills/gallery/references/pages-and-data.md). Quiet and led by its type: each card one hero (a big number, a field of squares, a list), its head saying only whose it is, the rest small and gray, one accent on the data and on today alone. The ground of a custom page (`--gallery-page-fill`: white, `#191919` dark, on its `html`, `body` and `main.gallery-page`, board.css; the workbench's top bar of a page the same, `--galleryPageBac`) and each card's frame (board.css: `--gallery-card-fill` white, `#202020` dark; no border, its line `--gallery-card-line` drawn as the first ring of `--gallery-card-shadow`, outside the card, so that the card's cells are all its own, with a close shadow and a soft one under it, deeper in dark; `--gallery-card-radius` 12px, `--gallery-card-pad` 16px): white cards on the page's white set off by the ring alone in light, a card a step lighter than the ground in dark. Its ink is five steps: `--gallery-text-strong` (`#1d1d1b`, the big numbers and a greeting), `--gallery-text` (`#37352f`, the words; white at 86% dark), `--gallery-muted` (a label or a unit), `--gallery-faint` (a caption, a head's quiet meta) and `--gallery-faintest` (a separator, a day to come); `--gallery-hair` a line between rows, `--gallery-fill` a soft gray (a chip, an empty day), `--gallery-track` what a bar or a ring stands on, `--gallery-hover` a row under the pointer. One accent, a quiet blue (`--gallery-accent` `#4084cf`, `#508fd6` dark: the bright `#2383e2` and `#3b8ee8` at four fifths of their OKLCH chroma, lightness and hue kept; the user's word of 2026-11-09 that the cards' blue be quieter went first to a grey-blue `#5781b7`, which the user found overdone the same day: only a little less vivid than the original. Every shade below, the heatmap's levels, the weather's rain and cool, today's evening glow and 柔光风格's own tokens, `#3e7ac6`, `#7eade2` dark, are their originals so calmed, alphas as they were, the previews too): its ink for words in it (`--gallery-accent-ink`), a pale fill behind a primary button's words (`--gallery-accent-soft`, the interface's rule for a primary button: pale, no line), a middle shade (`--gallery-accent-mid`: the days before today's in a week's bars, the Agent's share) and a wash (`--gallery-accent-wash`, a field's ring while typed in) and a fill under white words (`--gallery-accent-fill`: today's cell, a day done, the countdown's day; the accent itself in light, white on it 3.87:1, and a deeper `#3973b8` in dark, where `--gallery-accent` is the lighter of two tuned as an ink: 4.86:1 rather than 3.4); green only for a thing done (`--gallery-good`, `-ink`, `-soft`), red for a bad state, and a warm orange (`--gallery-warm`, `-soft`) for the sun by day, a streak's flame and a stale answer's sign. The cards' own type, `--gallery-font` (Segoe UI, PingFang SC on a Mac, Microsoft YaHei for its Chinese, as the system draws them, not the document's metrics-tuned Doc Segoe), 13px on lines of 1.5. A card's head is a row 20px high: its chip (`--gallery-chip-size` 20px, `--gallery-chip-radius` 6px, an icon of lines `--gallery-chip-icon` 13px in the muted gray on the soft fill, its line 1.9 of a 24 grid (a picture drawn on another grid keeps that line in proportion to it: 1.27 on a 16, 1.58 on a 20, as 柔光风格's 1.8 on its chips; the 2026-11 pictures of 天气, 我的待办, 习惯打卡, 便签, 编辑热力图 and the stats' 文档 and 待办完成 cells are the owner's picks, flat lines on a 16 grid or Apple-style on a 20); warm for the sun, the habit's chip and a streak's flame, the accent's pale for the moon), its title (`--gallery-title-size` 13px, 600, cut with an ellipsis) and right after it a quiet meta (`--gallery-meta-size` 12px, faint: the week's number, a habit's days in all, 今天 3 篇, how many to-dos are open, a note's characters), 8px apart, the row's end clear of the grip (`padding-inline-end: var(--gallery-grip-room, 0px)`). A big number is `--gallery-numeral-weight` 600 at `--gallery-numeral-tracking` −0.025em in figures of one height, its unit a 13px muted word `--gallery-unit-gap` after it (`--gallery-unit`; 3px, 6px where the page's language is English: 6 days, not 6天, as the static card chooses its words, `data-ge-language` or the lang; `--gallery-unit-size`, a share of the number, is what a document's metric takes). Two sizes, `--gallery-hero-size` (44px) a card's one main number (a countdown's days, a streak, the weather's temperature) and `--gallery-figure-size` (28px) a number among others (each of the stats, the to-dos' open count, a compact heatmap's total; the stats' 32px on a 12-wide card, the calendar's 56px day, the ring's percent and a full heatmap's 36px total are their own). The pale button in a card (打卡, and the kit's 允许访问 on a card) is `--gallery-button-height` 30px and `--gallery-button-radius` 8px, its fill 20ms ease-in as every hover fill. A line of information (a time, a count, a source, a hint: what a reader may need, not a separator or a placeholder) is set in `--gallery-muted` (4.4:1), never `--gallery-faint` (2.4:1), which is for the glyphs, the separators, the placeholders and a day's number inside its cell; a day to come in the habit's weeks is `--gallery-faint` in a ring of `--gallery-faintest`. A card with nothing to show has one empty face under its head: a line of what is so (13.5px, 600) over what to do (12px, muted), in its middle under a 28px soft square holding the card's picture where the card has no head row or is four cells high or more (the to-dos, the documents, and the static card where it is 100px high or more: a container query of its instance, the chip its `::before`), or at its left with no picture under a head row in a short card (the weather's and the countdown's, 4 × 2 and 2 × 2), where there is no room for the chip; where something waits for the reader, one pale blue button; no picture, no exclamation. The keys' focus on a card's control (the habit's button, a document's row, a to-do's box and words, a group's document) is the kit's ring in the pack's accent, 2px solid, 2px off the control (inside it, `-2px`, where the card clips what is past it: the documents' rows); the to-do card's list keeps 4px at its sides and 3px at its top for it (the list scrolls, so it clips), and 柔光风格's 6px at its sides. The card's tooltips are the kit's (`data-doc-tip`, and `data-doc-tip-cut` on the words of a row or a name that an ellipsis or a two-line clamp may cut, which shows them whole only then), never a `title`; a heatmap's own bubble (it shows at once and follows the pointer across the squares) has the kit tooltip's look, dark in both themes. A card's top-right corner, a 28 × 28 box 6px in from its top and right edges, is the board's grip's: `.gallery-card` sets `--gallery-grip-room` (`40px − --gallery-card-pad`, 24px), how far that box reaches into its content with a little air, which each of Gallery's blocks keeps its first row's end clear of; `--gallery-grip-shown` is 1 while the grip stands in the card (`data-ge-hover`, `src/editor/page/board/`), for a head that would fade something there. The documents' own framed blocks (a metric, `gallery/card` and `gallery/chart-card`) keep the document's look, as a figure has (`--doc-border`, the chart series' colors), and the framed ones the kit's card's since 2026-11-17 (after a design review: version 1.1.0 of the metric, the card and the chart card): a 12px radius, the ground they stand on (`--doc-figure-ground`, the page's color, a detail card's surface in one) and no surface of their own, `--doc-surface` inside them being that ground as in a figure, the kit's 14px above and 18px at the sides, a title (the card's, the chart card's) at the card title's 16px on 22, bold, no tracking, 10px over a chart (the card's text 4px under it, as a heading's paragraph): the metric's number alone takes the numeral tokens.

## Charts

- Every chart is declared in its card, of a type the document's script defines: `<div data-doc-chart="slope"><script type="application/json">…its data…</script></div>`, the type defined once with `DocCharts.define(type, draw)` (`declared.js`; a type is lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, a letter first, defined once). The kit has no chart type of its own: what a chart draws, its marks, labels, scales and words, is the document's code, and the kit gives it its host, its data, the theme's colors (`tokens`, `DocTokens.read(host)`), its width and its card's plot height. A type is drawn once defined, for every host declared with it, `draw({ host, data, tokens, width, height })` called with the host emptied and again on every width change, theme change and `doc-layout-change` (`height`: the card's `--doc-chart-height`, or null), as the runtime tells it ("Runtime"). A chart card put back on the page (an undo) draws again; one taken off is destroyed. Drawing (and `destroy`) empties the host but for its data script (`clear` in `charts/core.js`), and a browser export ("导出当前视图副本") keeps only the declaration and draws it again on open, never twice. A declaration that fails (bad JSON, a draw that throws) is told on the console and, in the preview, next to its chart, and leaves the others drawing. A type defined with a part, `DocCharts.define(type, { mount })`, keeps what it starts (listeners, timers, controls of its own in its host) until its host leaves the page: `mount({ ...draw's context, signal })` returns `{ update, destroy }`, told as a drawing would be drawn again (`createPart` in `declared.js`). A host with no JSON and no table key is a host the document's script draws into itself (`startDeclared` starts nothing there). `DocCharts.get(target)` returns a chart's handle, `{ redraw(data?), destroy() }`, or null. Nothing refers to a chart by id, so its card is not kept (see "Deleting blocks" in [editor.md](editor.md)): the reader can delete and move it; a card a free script refers to by id stays. The bundle needs `runtime.js` and `tokens.js` before it, never D3: a document whose script names `d3` loads D3 too (`usedAssets`, by the global's name).
- The kit's look of a chart (`styles/chart.css`): the host is `.doc-chart` (the kit's class on every declared host), a block with room (280px) until something is drawn in it (`:not(:has(> :not(script)))`, so a define's first frame does not jump), its `svg` a block as wide as the host, its overflow visible; text in it is the document's font at 16px, and a group of class `axis` takes the axis look (`--doc-chart-axis-font-size`, 13px, `--doc-muted` text, `--doc-border` lines), the one look of the kit the document's drawing opts into. Card headings use the separate 18px heading scale.
- Chart cards are framed (`styles/figure.css`; the finish of 2026-11-17, the user's pick): every `.doc-figure` has a 1px border (`--doc-border`), 12px corners and the color of the ground it stands on, no surface of its own (`--doc-figure-ground`: the page's background, `--doc-bg`, and a detail card's surface in one, `dialog.css`), padded 14px above, 18px at its sides and 12px below (12, 14 and 10 at 420px or below); `data-surface="bounded"`, which older files write, says the same and nothing more. Inside a card `--doc-surface` is that ground, so a chart's tokens (`tokens.surface`, read at its host) and a drawing that fills with `var(--doc-surface)` to stand out of the card or knock a line out draw as they did when the card was the surface's color. A card holds any figure, a chart or a drawing of the document's own in HTML, CSS or SVG, framed once: its title is its own `h3` (`.doc-figure > h3`, the card's styles never reaching into the drawing's headings: 16px on 22, `--doc-card-title-*`, bold, no tracking; an `h2` at the h2's size), first; then what it holds; its caption `.doc-figure-caption` (or a `<figcaption>` of a figure the intake made a card, read the same: 13px, muted) under the picture, last. The file keeps the caption where it was written, right under the title: the card is a grid of one column laid out in that order (`order`: the title −1, the caption 1), so no document changes and the page editor's fields, the title and the caption, are still reached in the order they show (↓ from the title goes to the caption, a chart being no text). A grid's items keep their margins, so the card sets the room between its parts: 10px under the title, 6px above the caption (4px for a caption alone under a title), and the first of what it holds takes no margin above it and the last none below it (weighted rules, as a drawing's own class may give it one: until 2026-11-17 a drawing's margin collapsed with the title's 12px, and would now add to the 10px); between two of them, their own margins. `.doc-figure--plain` (the page editor's 显示边框 off, the user's of 2026-11-20, as a Sheet area's `.doc-sheet-shell--plain`) takes the frame away and the room it made inside with it: no border, corners, ground or padding at any width (the rule comes last in `figure.css`, after the narrow widths), so that the title, what the card holds and the caption stand at the edges of its width, level with a paragraph's; its width (the reading width, `--doc-span`, `.doc-figure--compact`) and `--doc-surface` are as they were; the preview, the export and a read-only page all draw it from the kit's style. A picture block (`figure.doc-image`) is no card and has no frame. Its picture (`styles/image.css`) is as wide as it is, at most the width it stands in, or a share of that width (`--doc-span`, which it then fills); without a span it is at most `80vh` tall, its width following (`max-height` on a replaced element keeps its ratio, and the figure, `fit-content`, follows), so a portrait scan shows whole on a screen, centered (a picture wider than tall reaches the limit only on a window less tall than 1.25 times the picture); with a span it is as tall as its width makes it (`max-height: var(--doc-image-fill, 80vh)`: with a span the fill is `100%`, which against the figure's height, its content's, is no limit). It is `object-fit: contain`, so a width of the source's own on the picture (`style="width: 300px"`) meeting that height limit draws it whole inside its box, never stretched. An `<img width>` an Agent writes becomes the span (editor.md, "Natural HTML").
- Layout notes on the page: what a table hides at its current width it says on its frame, and a chart drawn from a table that draws nothing on its host, `data-doc-layout="<kind>: <message>"` (several joined by a line break), written again with every drawing and taken off when nothing is to be said; the preview and the QA tool read them, and it is a runtime mark (the components' descriptions), so an export of the view leaves it out. A chart's host carries `data-missing: …` (`writeLayoutNotes` in `charts/core.js`, "Charts from a table"); clearing the host for a drawing (`clear`) takes the notes off with it, so a chart destroyed, or one whose drawing threw midway, keeps none of an earlier drawing's. A table's frame writes its own ("Table layout internals").

## Charts from a table

A chart may take its data from a table of the page instead of its JSON, and is drawn again whenever the table changes: the link is a key, `data-doc-chart-data="t1"` on the host (which then holds no JSON) and `data-doc-chart-source="t1"` on the table's frame (`CHART_DATA`, `CHART_SOURCE`, `CHART_KEY` in `src/shared/table-chart.mjs`: a letter, then letters, digits, `_` or `-`, 32 at most), never an id, an `href` or the blocks' order, so the editor keeps neither block for the other and a card and its table may stand apart (a card in a column, its table folded in a disclosure). The type is one the document's script defines, like any chart's.

- What the Agent writes, `<table data-doc-chart="slope">` with a `<caption>`, the intake (`src/editor/source/natural.mjs`, [editor.md](editor.md) "Natural HTML") makes two blocks: the table as the table rule makes it, whose frame carries the key (the one written on the table, else the first of `t1`, `t2` … no table or host of the file carries), and right after the frame's end tag a card, `<figure class="doc-figure"><h3>{the caption's words}</h3><div data-doc-chart="slope" data-doc-chart-data="t1"></div></figure>` (no caption: no `h3`, and the checks note "this chart card has no title"); a table of the kit's written with `data-doc-chart` (an Agent charting a table while revising) gets the same card after its frame, the frame the key (or keeping its own). A table with `data-doc-chart` inside a `<figure>`, a cell or a whole block is left as written and fails, saying how to write it.
- What a table gives a chart (`tableRows` in `src/shared/table-chart.mjs`, read alike by the kit from the page and the checks from the file, each through its `tableCells`: the header row's words, the header cells' `data-sort-type`, the body rows in the file's order with each cell's words and `data-sort-value`, and whether a cell spans rows or columns): d3.csvParse's shape, an array of one plain object a body row, keyed by the header's words in order, and `data.columns` those words. The header is the head's first row, else a first body row of `<th>` alone, else the first row; a header cell with no words is the key `""`, and of two alike the later column's value is kept (both noted by the checks, never refused). A value is read as the table's sort reads it (`number-reading.mjs`): a cell's `data-sort-value`, else its words (`cellWords`, a formula its TeX); `""`, `-`, `—`, `n/a` and the like are `null`, as is a cell a short row lacks; a column whose header says `data-sort-type="text"` or `"date"` gives its words; else one number gives that number (`12.5%` 12.5, `3.6亿` 360000000, `1,234` 1234, `(12)` -12, `120 ms` 120), and anything else (a range, `3 / 4`, words) its words. Cells past the header's count are not read; `<tfoot>` rows never are (a total belongs there: a total row in the body is data like any row). Merged cells give nothing to draw (`fail(error, key)`: the error for the checks and the layout note, the key the kit's words), and neither does a key no frame carries.
- The kit (`charts/from-table.js` `createBound`, started by `declared.js` for a host with `data-doc-chart-data` whose type is defined) reads the frame of its key anywhere in the page (the first, when two carry it; none in what the preview adds, `data-doc-dev`) and draws the chart with its rows as `data`, as any chart of its type (a draw or a part), its handle's `setData` drawing it again with new rows; `DocCharts.get(host).redraw()` reads the table again, and `redraw(data)` throws (it draws its table's rows: change the table). The kit's one watcher of the tables a key names (`runtime/table-reading.js` `createKeyedTables`, `DocKit.tables`, the kit's own: one `MutationObserver` while any chart or view follows a key) tells each such chart of what touches its own table (its cells' words, a `data-sort-value` or a header's `data-sort-type`, a `rowspan` or `colspan`, a link's `href`, a row added or taken away, the frame deleted or put back, a key put on, off or changed) or its host's key (a commit's answer may give it another, [editor.md](editor.md) "Copying blocks"), each table read once a change for every chart and view of it (`readTable`, the reading that `context.rows` and `context.table()` take too, each cell and row read again only once a change touches it), and each chart draws again only when what it reads changed (its rows and columns as JSON); a chart drawn until then keeps standing while its table reads as nothing for a moment (cells merged and split in one edit) and says so once that has lasted 800ms (`SETTLE`), so an edit never takes the card away under the caret. Rows are read in the file's order: a reader's sort (the kit's sort buttons: the rows moved, a header's `aria-sort`) leaves the chart as it was, while in the preview, where a sort is an edit of the table's order (the page editor's arrows), the chart follows it. With nothing to draw the card says why in a muted line (`.doc-chart-message`, the kit's words: "这张图的数据表格不在页面上了。" when its table is gone, "数据表格有合并的单元格，画不出这张图。") and its host carries the layout note `data-missing: …`, which the preview's note counts among its failures (`preview-checks.mjs`, as `gallery check` and `gallery qa` fail it).
- The checks (`src/render/chart-declarations.mjs`, `gallery check`) read the table from the file as the kit reads it from the page: a bound host fails when it holds a JSON script (its data is the table's), when no frame carries its key or two do, when the frame holds no table, and when its table has merged cells; a card with no title is a note (its title the first `h2`–`h4` of its figure's own: `cardTitle`), saying what titles it where the Agent writes: `write a <caption> on its table` for a card the intake made in memory of a table the file holds (`gallery check` reads the file so, `loadReports` keeping the file's text as the document's `written`, which `notesOf` and `layoutNotes` pass on), else `write an h3 in the card's figure`; so is a header with a blank or repeated name. What each such chart reads of its table is said in a line of its own at its line (`tableChartReadings` and `rowsReading`, which `gallery check` and `gallery export` print after the notes as information, `chart <slug>: …`, `reportNotes`; no layout note, so neither the preview's note nor a document's example counts it): `slope (line 40) reads its table (line 44): 3 rows; "类别": words; "之前": numbers; "之后": 2 numbers, 1 empty (null)`, so the Agent sees how its cells read before its code meets them; what in it is worth a look is a `note` (a column read as numbers in some rows and words in others, the rows of a `<tfoot>` left out, which also takes the table tools from the table, cells past the header), so a document as it should be, the skill's example among them, prints none (`tests/unit/skill-docs.mjs`). A document's script types the rows with `/** @type {DocTableRows} */` (`skills/gallery/sdk/doc-charts.d.ts`: `DocTableValue`, a number, its words or null; `DocTableRow`; `DocTableRows`, the rows and their `columns`).
- An export works offline (the kit reads the table as the page opens); the Markdown export writes the card's picture and the table, as two blocks. Copying relinks: a card and its table pasted together take a key of their own, a table alone keeps its key while no table holds it, a card alone keeps its key, all within the document they were copied from; in another, a reader (a card, a view) or a table pasted alone loses its key, and a pair takes one of its own there (`chart-keys.mjs`, [editor.md](editor.md) "Copying blocks").
- A view of a table is a reader too: an instance of a custom block whose needs hold `tables` names the table by the same key (`data-doc-from`, `VIEW_FROM`; `TABLE_READERS` are both attributes), reads it as a chart does and asks the page editor to change it (`context.table()`, "A custom block on a page"; [editor.md](editor.md), "Table views"). A table has any number of readers, charts and views of it together, each following it by itself. The checks hold a view to its table as a chart is held (`chartDeclarationFailures`: a key on an element that is no instance, one that is no key, no frame carrying it or two, a frame with no table) and note what is worth a look (`chartDeclarationNotes`: merged cells, which no view reads; more rows than `VIEW_ROWS`, 500, the table the scenario `tableViewLatency` holds typing to its bound with three views; a block whose needs hold no `tables`, which reads no table).

## Table layout internals

`tables.js` (`tables/layout.js`) reads visible cell text, measures it with the table font through Canvas, and writes one `colgroup` with percentage widths plus `table-layout: fixed`. `data-column-key` on a header is optional; otherwise the column index is the key. The allocator (`tables/auto-column-widths.ts`):

1. Compute the P90 cell width and protect the header width. For compact columns, also fit the longest actual value within the compact protection budget (160px by default, including padding); percentile interpolation must not split short labels such as `Agent`. Explicit `maxWidth` still takes precedence.
2. Classify each column as `compact`, `narrative`, or `token-heavy` using deterministic text/token rules. Unspaced CJK prose remains narrative. A column at least half of whose words are numbers (as `src/shared/number-reading.mjs` reads one: the whole value or each word of it, so `+8,214 +19,877 +31,402`, a list of figures, is three) is compact however long its cells are: a list of figures is no prose, so it gives no short field beside it the floor of a field beside prose (below), nor itself a prose column's 160px floor, and whole.js reads no words in it (until 2026-11-24 such a column read as prose by its width, and the label column beside it was squeezed to its words, a label of three words on three lines in a table of experiments the user showed).
3. Establish preferred, soft-minimum and hard-minimum widths. For paths and prose, raise the readable floor to allow roughly three lines of P90 text: `padding + (P90 width - padding) / 3`, capped at 320px and never above preferred width. The existing per-kind baseline and explicit bounds still apply. A column of long identifiers (`token-heavy`, not numeric: a dataset's name, a hash) keeps each whole on its line: its floor, and its preferred width at least, is its widest word (`cellWordWidths`, words as `WORD_BREAKS` splits them) no wider than `longWordMaxWidth` (640px, about eighty characters: every such word, as a short field's values within their protection, never P90's interpolation a pixel short of the widest); a wider word breaks where its column ends whatever its width, so it asks nothing. That floor is given up (to the one above) only to fit, step 4.
4. Give up only to fit (`giveUp`). A table whose readable floors are wider than its room gives up, cheapest first and only as much as brings it inside: (1) the words of its short text (`words`): a short field beside prose (below) down to its median word, and a compact text column whose values are longer than its protection (its P90 past 160px: a list of figures, one to a line then, `+8,214` / `+19,877`) down to its words (the P90 of its cells' longest words, at least its header's longest word and its widest piece); (2) its headers (`headers`): a compact column whose header is wider than what its values keep (平均响应延迟（毫秒） over 12, `Requests per second (p95)` over 840) down to its header's longest word and its values (a number whole); (3) its long identifiers (`identifiers`, step 3), whose floor goes back to the three lines of their text. Within a stage the widest floors go first (water filling, `lowerWidest`); the earlier stages are given whole before a later one is touched, except that once identifiers must break they break whole (a column a little narrower than a name breaks it as surely) and the cheaper stages give only what is still missing. A table that would still be wider than its room with every stage given up scrolls with all of them kept: it gives nothing for nothing (a matrix of scores keeps its headers whole and scrolls, as before; a table of experiments that scrolls anyway keeps its dataset names whole). In such a table a list (a column the first stage would lower) is still made as narrow as its words where that makes no row taller (`narrowFree`): a row is as tall as the fewest lines its other cells can take in their columns (`fewestLines`: their one line packed edge to edge, so no browser lays them in fewer), the list as it fills its lines with its words whole (`linesOf`, a pixel to spare), and the list's header no taller than the header row; so a cell of four figures beside a cell of four dataset names takes them one to a line, level with the names, and the table scrolls no further than it must, while a row where the list stands alone keeps it on its lines. The option `keepLongWords` keeps identifiers out of it (the layout's question whether a table is wider than the text, 宽表格 below). The user's table of 2026-11-24 past the text: its list of figures one to a line beside its dataset names, each name whole on its line and level with its figure; before, a prose column's 160px floor kept the figures two to a line, and the names were broken anywhere at 497px, mid-name and run together.
5. Protect compact fields first. Token-heavy and narrative columns share compression using the same retained fraction of preferred text width (excluding cell padding), so paths are not squeezed to three lines while prose retains a full line of space. When a column reaches its readable (`softMinWidth`) floor, redistribute further compression across the others; compact columns participate last. This balances estimated wrapping pressure, not exact browser line counts. Do not compress below readable floors.
6. If the P90 layout leaves unused viewport space, complete actual compact values that fit in that space, smallest additions first. Values that do not fit and explicit `maxWidth` limits are left alone.
7. Compare the sum of readable floors (as step 4 left them) with the available container width. If it does not fit, keep those column widths and scroll horizontally. The threshold depends on the content, not a fixed column count. Return widths and normalized ratios.

Defaults: P90; 160px narrative / 140px token-heavy baseline readable floors (never wider than preferred content); up to 160px of whole-cell preferred-width protection for compact columns; 24px horizontal cell padding by default (the DOM adapter passes the cells' own: 9px at each side, and their line); a 48px absolute floor for tiny fields. Short headers include their measured font, padding, and sort indicator. A table is as wide as its columns need, whatever their number (no column count fills the reading width); while it fits, no column is narrower than a new column, 120px (`COLUMN_FLOOR`): the room left raises the narrower ones to one level, the narrowest first, so an empty or short column does not shrink to its few characters, and a table never scrolls for it. Below 420px (`matchMedia('(max-width: 420px)')`, the kit's narrowest breakpoint) the DOM adapter passes `maxReadableFloor`, a third of the frame's inner width: no column's readable floor is more than that, whatever its kind (a prose column's 160px baseline and its three lines of P90 text, a path column's, a short field's protected width alike), and no column's code run or number range asks more than half of it (below), so a table of two or three columns wraps their text before it scrolls sideways (a phone's 328px gives each about 109px; a column of short phrases, `nowrap` in the kit, then wraps as a wider cell does, `data-column-wrap`); a numeric column (its cells `.number`, `cells.js`; the adapter passes `numeric`) keeps its floor, as a number never wraps. Pure allocation accepts optional `kind`, `minWidth`, `maxWidth` and `numeric` overrides and optional `cellWidths` aligned with `cells` (including padding), and the options `maxReadableFloor` (positive; unset, no limit; the identifier floor of step 3 is under it as any other, so a phone breaks a long name), `longWordMaxWidth` (nonnegative; unset, 640) and `keepLongWords` (a boolean; unset, false); invalid numeric options, contradictory bounds, and duplicate keys throw. `columnKind(header, cells, measure)` gives a column's kind as step 2 reads it (whole.js reads columns of words by it).

The DOM adapter measures text runs in their own fonts, including inline code padding, and reuses those measurements for overflow detection. Digits in a `tabular-nums` cell (numeric columns) count as the font's tabular digit, measured once per font with a hidden probe, since Canvas cannot set `font-variant-numeric`; every width is rounded up to a whole pixel, so a column exactly as wide as its text does not wrap it by a fraction. A header's sort control takes no width of its own: it is an empty button of width 0 after the header's words, its arrow its `::after` (from `data-doc-sort-arrow`), hung in the cell's right padding, which a header holding a control has wider (`.doc-table th:has(> .doc-table-sort-button)`: 15px, not 9: the arrow's 5px off the words and its 7px, then 3px at least before the cell's line, 2026-11-20, the arrow had touched the line of a column as wide as its header's words), so a column whose width its header's words decide is 6px wider and the layout reads that padding off the cell like any (a table without sort controls, an export of the view that takes them out, lays out with 9px again), and a header wraps only where its column does. The other cells and the headers without a control keep their 9px; a centered header's words stand 3px left of the numbers' centre below it. Inline code is measured for its runs too (`measure.least`): its longest run, in the code's font, with the code's padding and border and the cell's padding, up to 320px of the run (`CODE_RUN_MAX`, the cap on a content floor, about forty characters of code); a run ends at a space or after a slash (`cells.js` `codeRuns`; slashes before a name start its run, so `/__local/` is one), never at a hyphen, a dot, an underscore or inside a Chinese word, so a path breaks only at its folders and `--doc-table-height` is one run. Code the kit does not draw in runs (in a cell the page editor edits, or code holding a link) is measured where Chrome breaks it (`NATIVE_BREAKS`: after a hyphen, around a Chinese character). A number range the kit holds on one line (`.doc-range`, `runtime/spacing.js`) is a run too. The kit draws code in a cell in those runs as the page renders (`cells.js` `splitCodeRuns`: each run a `span.doc-code-run`, `white-space: nowrap`, a `<wbr>` after each slash, its spaces left between them, and in each run a `<wbr>` between its names (`runParts`: after a dot, before underscores but leading or trailing ones, where a lowercase letter or a digit meets a capital), hidden while the run is kept whole (Chrome breaks at a `<wbr>` even in `nowrap`); not in a cell the page editor edits, nor code holding elements; an export of the view joins them again, `joinCodeRuns`). A column's largest is its `minWidth`, so the allocator never makes it narrower, and the frame scrolls sideways instead; below 420px half the frame's inner width at most (the narrow screen above: a run whole there, where words take a third). A cell whose longest run (`measure.run`, uncapped) is wider than its column (past 320px, half a narrow screen, or in a column the reader sized) is marked `data-run-wrap`, and its runs break where the column ends: code between its names first (`window.` / `__demo`, `request` / `Animation` / `Frame`), anywhere only in a name wider than the column (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`; a range at its dash); a table the browser lays out (merged cells) makes each column as wide as its runs itself (their min-content).

Headers keep their words whole (`word-break: keep-all`: a line breaks at a space or a punctuation mark, never inside 标准差 or a bracketed unit), and so does a short field that wraps, where its column holds its longest word (`data-column-wrap="words"`, `measure.word`, words split by `WORD_BREAKS` of `auto-column-widths.ts`: at a space, after a closing or separating mark or a slash, a hyphen before a letter, before an opening bracket, never before a closing mark, nor after a comma or a slash between digits: 12,345 and 11/24 are one word, as the browser keeps them; until 2026-11-24 +19,877 was two words, +19, and 877, so a column of such figures could be made narrower than one); narrower, they break between characters as Chinese text does. A run of inline code starts a word (a line breaks between runs, after a slash), so a path in code is as long a word as its longest run. A cell of a column of identifiers (`token-heavy`) that wraps keeps them whole the same way where its column holds the longest stretch the browser keeps on a line once its long runs are not broken anywhere (`measure.held`, `HELD_BREAKS` of `layout.js`: a space, after a hyphen before a letter or a question mark, around a Chinese character; a slash, a dot or an underscore is none, as in Chrome), so that each dataset name starts a line of its own where it does not fit after the one before, never broken where that line ends and run on into the next (`keepsWords`; 替用户定的, 2026-11-24: in a column of names the page's rule for a long run, which fills each line to its end, cut every name after the first in a cell even where the column held each; a URL there now breaks at its hyphens and its question mark, not inside a word); a column narrower than such a stretch breaks them where its lines end, as before. Every other cell's words break as the page's prose does (`word-break` inherited from `.doc-page`: an ordinary word whole at a line's end, a long run anywhere in its `span.doc-long-run`, `styles/page.css`), a column of prose (`narrative`) and of paths (`token-heavy`) included; a short field (`compact`) keeps `word-break: normal` (its widest piece, a number or （1606）, is its column's floor), and a header and a short field keep a long run whole as their other words (the span takes its cell's `word-break`, `table.css`; the adapter measures a held run as the text it is), inline code its runs. Beside a column of prose (a table with a `narrative` column), a short text column (`compact`, not numeric) has its words as its readable floor instead of its whole values: the P90 of its cells' longest words (`cellWordWidths`, `headerWordWidth` from the adapter), at least its header's longest word and its widest piece no line breaks in (`cellPieceWidths`, `measure.piece`: pieces split by `PIECE_BREAKS`, as Chinese text breaks, so （1606）， or a number is one; narrower, the cell would cut it anywhere, a closing mark starting a line); if the table is still wider than its frame at its floors, such columns give up their longest words, the widest first, down to their median word, never below that piece, when that brings the table inside the frame (`giveUp`'s first stage, step 4; a table that scrolls anyway keeps their words whole). So 卷数 (六十卷、目录一卷) added beside 看点 wraps at its punctuation rather than push the prose out of the frame; a table of short fields alone keeps its values whole and scrolls. A layout measures each cell once (the measurer remembers it for one pass: the widths, the runs and the height below read the same numbers), and reads each element's style once, not for each of its words. A cell is not measured again in the next layout while what it is measured from stays the same (`cellMeasures`, its key: its content as HTML, its classes and style, its computed font, letter spacing, numerals, padding and side borders, its table's and frame's classes but `doc-table--auto`, and the page's theme and width; the page editor's rebuild of a cell marks every element in it, so a cell of words alone is not measured again for it), so a word typed in a cell measures that cell alone and the widths come out as a layout measuring every cell makes them; a cell holding a formula is measured every time (KaTeX's drawing as laid out), and `DocTables.layout()` measures every cell. The Canvas measurers keep their words from one layout to the next too, one per font, until a face of a family they name loads (`loadingdone`, or all of them when the page's fonts are in); a page zoomed (its device pixel ratio changed) measures every cell again, in new measurers. What a layout writes (the colgroup, a cell's marks, the frame's least width) it writes only when it changes, so a layout that comes out as the last one gives the browser nothing to lay out again.

No scrollbar takes room from a table. The frame (`.doc-table-wrap`) hides the system's scrollbars (`scrollbar-width: none`, and `::-webkit-scrollbar` for older WebKit) and tables.js draws its own, each one of the kit's scrollbars ("Scrollbars the kit draws" below): a `.doc-scrollbars` layer (aria-hidden, no text) as the frame's first child, `position: sticky` at its top-left with no size, holding a rail along the right edge and one along the bottom for each axis that overflows (`data-y`, `data-x`), each thumb 3px in from the frame's edge (clear of the page editor's handles on its right and bottom edges). A track runs only along the part that scrolls: the vertical one from below the header (sticky, so it stays) to where the bottom corner's curve begins (the radius less the border), the horizontal one from after a sticky first column (`.doc-table--sticky-first`) or a header column or the left corner's curve to the right corner's, each stopping short of the other rail. A thumb is as long as the rows (columns) shown of those that scroll. Each rail's place, its track's length and its thumb's are set from the frame's client size and its table's size (not the frame's scroll size, which counts the rails themselves) whenever the frame or its table resizes and after every layout, never as the frame scrolls: where a thumb is along its track is the scroll's own, drawn by the browser (the frame names its two timelines, `scroll-timeline: --doc-scrollbar-x x, --doc-scrollbar-y y`, its rails inside it); a rail's place and its track's end against the frame's inside to the sub-pixel (its computed size less its borders), never by `clientWidth` and `clientHeight`, which are rounded: a frame 1051.56px high (rows between pixels: a zoom, a screen at 225%) whose bottom rail stood at the rounded 1052 less its 12px ended 0.44px below the frame and made a frame that scrolls only sideways scroll down that far once a cell had the caret, its rows under the header row that stays, the wheel over it stopped there (the user's 2026-11-23). They show while the pointer is over the frame, while it scrolls along either axis (one mark for both, `data-active` on the layer) and during a drag. A press on a rail is the scrollbar's: on the thumb it drags, beside it the thumb first goes there. On an edge the editor resizes the table from (`html[data-ge-resize]`, the frame's outer pixels), the press stays the editor's. The wheel, touch and keys scroll the frame natively. Pushed on past an end (the frame never bounces), a thumb gives way instead and springs back the moment the pushing stops, as "Scrollbars giving way" below says (`squeeze`, each wheel event handed to the rail of its axis): the bottom thumb by the wheel sideways at either end and by a drag; the right thumb by a drag, and by the wheel only where nothing around the frame takes the push (`passOn` found the page, or the box the frame stands in, at its end too), since the down axis is the page's at its ends (below). A view copy (导出当前视图副本) leaves the layer out and draws it again on opening. The line is as wide as the frame by the stylesheet (the layer 100% of the frame's inside and no height, the line 100% of the layer), laid out with the frame in the same pass: no width read afterwards (an `offsetWidth`, whole pixels, of a frame resized since, before its observer has heard it) can draw the line wider or narrower than the frame, the edge a reader saw run out to the room's right and back; its height is the frame's computed height, to the sub-pixel. The frame and its table are measured before the line's height is set (the layer, out of the flow, changes none of those sizes, only how far the frame scrolls), then everything placed is written only where it changes, so placing them lays the page out once, not once for each write; the scrollbars are placed again after every layout (`refreshLayout`, whether or not the frame's size changed), so the line never keeps a size the frame had (a wider frame's would reach past the table and let the frame scroll into nothing), and first by the table's first layout, not before it has its columns. While words lie past the frame's right edge, that edge fades out (`data-more` on the layer, said again as the frame scrolls, its place read once a frame: a 16px gradient to the table's fill, drawn by the line layer over the cells and taking no room; the layer's right line is then opaque, `--doc-hairline-solid` in tokens.css, the hairline as it lies on the page (`--doc-table-frame-filled` in a block with a fill of its own), because the dark hairline is a translucent veil and the columns past the fade showed through the one pixel under it: the user's of 2026-11-09, scenario `tableEdgeOpaque`), since the rails show only under the pointer: until the frame is scrolled to where the words past its edge at rest end (`reach` of `tableHides()`, below), so words it covers always have more past them. On paper (`@media print`) a frame is whole: no height limit (one written on it neither), nothing clipped, no rails, no fade. What the frame hides at rest (scrolled to its start) it says in a layout note on itself (`data-doc-layout`, "Chart rendering"; `noteHidden` in `scrollbars.js`, again whenever the frame or its table changes size and after every layout, written only when it changes), measured as `gallery qa` measures a table (`tableHides()`, `src/shared/table-hides.mjs`): `columns-hidden: of 6 columns, 1 cut at the frame's edge ("结论"), 1 out of sight ("备注") (scroll sideways)` (a column whose words, of any of its cells, header or body, reach past the right edge: cut while some start before it, out of sight when none does; a cell's words are its text's lines and its pictures, drawings and controls as drawn, `wordsBox()`, never the box of a list or a paragraph in it, which is as wide as the cell; a cell reaching past the edge with nothing written there hides nothing; a first column that stays never counts) and `rows-hidden: the frame shows 12 of 40 rows (scroll down)` (of the body's rows shown, not those a filter hides, one shown while its words end above the bottom edge). A cell's text starts its padding in from its edge, so a table a few pixels wider than its frame (columns at their least widths) hides no text: no note and no fade, and neither for cells past the edge with nothing written there. So allocation uses the frame's whole inner width, read to the sub-pixel (its computed width with the frame at 100%, the width put back at once in the same pass, before anything else is read or written: `roomOf`, so that 100% is never drawn nor left on the frame by a failure after it; `clientWidth`, whole pixels, left a filling table a fraction of a pixel off its frame at a fractional zoom); full-width and overflowing frames stay at 100%, compact frames are their table plus borders and padding, each written only when it changes. Layout is recalculated when the container or the frame changes width (a column) by half a pixel or more from what the last layout left them at (`leftAt`, their content boxes read once its writes are done, in the unit the observer reads: a width the layout wrote, or one that followed from it, is no change, so a layout never asks for the next one; a chain of layouts the observer alone asks for, frame after frame, whose room comes back to the one two layouts before three times, a frame feeding its own width back into its room, is held for a second and then laid out once: `followChain`; the scenario `tableEdgeSteady` measures it), fonts finish loading (a font it measured its words in, or KaTeX's for a table holding a formula: "Runtime"), table content changes (once the page is idle for a change made through `DocKit.quietly`), or a hidden Sheet/disclosure becomes visible. A sticky first column (`.doc-table--sticky-first`) and a header column share one rule in `styles/table.css` and no script: the cell is `position: sticky` and filled with `var(--doc-fill, var(--doc-table-fill))`, its `doc-highlight-*` color (always opaque) or else the table's own fill, so sorting or a theme change has nothing to redo. That one declaration is `!important`, the kit's only one besides the reduced-motion rule's: a style attribute beats any selector, and a source style that made a cell of that column see-through (a translucent or a transparent background) would show the cells scrolling beneath it. So no background of the source's, written in a first-column cell's `style` or by a stylesheet rule (a row's tint among them), reaches that cell, and nothing is blended into the kit's fill; the cell is colored by a class.

**Scrollbars the kit draws** (`scrollbar.js`, `styles/scrollbar.css`: one scrollbar for all of them, a table frame's two, `tables/scrollbars.js` above, and a database's sideways one under its table view and its board, `database/ui/rail.js`; the user's of 2026-11-24, a slow sideways swipe on a touchpad showing the bottom thumb step and stall). A rail is `.doc-scrollbar.doc-scrollbar--x` or `--y`, 12px thick (`RAIL`) and as long as its track (`--doc-scrollbar-track`), holding its thumb (`.doc-scrollbar-thumb`, 6px, 3px in from the edge it runs along, `--doc-scrollbar-thumb` and `--doc-scrollbar-thumb-active` under the pointer or while dragged), placed by its owner (where its track starts, against which edge) and given its geometry by it when a size changes (`set`: the track's length, the thumb's, `--doc-scrollbar-thumb-size`, and how far the content scrolls); what is written is written only where it changes (`setStyle`). **Its thumb's place is the scroll's, drawn by the browser**: a scroll-driven animation (`animation-timeline: --doc-scrollbar-x` or `--doc-scrollbar-y`, `doc-scrollbar-along-x` and `-y`) moves it from the track's start to its end, its `translate` from 0 to `--doc-scrollbar-travel` (the track less the thumb), linearly over the whole of the scroll range, along the timeline its scroller names (`scroll-timeline: --doc-scrollbar-x x, --doc-scrollbar-y y`: a table's frame in `table.css`, a database's `.no-native-scrollbar`), the nearest of that name, the scroller scrolled on the compositor too (a table's frame, opaque, is by the browser's own choice; a database's, see-through and masked at its edges, asks for it, `will-change: scroll-position`: on a screen at 100% the browser repainted it on the main thread as it scrolled, the thumb then ahead of its columns, in 16% to 25% of the frames headless, up to 26 px); a rail not inside its scroller (a database's, beside it in the view's frame) has the frame hoist the name to it (`timeline-scope` on `.tbl-frame` and `.board-frame`). So it moves on the compositor, in the same frame as the content, and no script runs as it scrolls: placed by the script on every scroll event, as it was before, the thumb lagged its content by a frame or more whenever the main thread was busy (the editor's observers, a long table's layout). Measured on a headed Chrome at 225%, wheel events of 1.5 px every 8 ms sent through the browser's input and every composited frame read from its pixels (the screencast: the content's place by the edges of a row's cells, the thumb's by its color), a table frame's and a database table view's bottom thumb were out of step with their content by more than 1.5 device px in 57% to 79% of the frames, up to 17 px of content behind, with the page's own work alone and with the main thread kept busy 45 ms in every 100; in none of them since, within 0.72 device px, and none at 100% either (`scrollbarInStep` measures the same in the tests' headless browser). The script and the stylesheet agree on which moves it by one mark: the script asks the browser once (`timelines`: `animation-timeline: scroll()` and `timeline-scope` both known) and marks each rail (`data-timeline`), which is what the stylesheet animates it by. **Where the browser has no timelines** the script places the thumb instead (`--doc-scrollbar-thumb-at`, the same `translate`): the scroll's place read and the thumb written once a frame (`requestAnimationFrame` asked by the scroll event, which itself writes nothing). It shows while what it scrolls scrolls or is pushed at an end (`data-active`, 900 ms after the last of it, `SHOWN`: one timer, re-armed only when it runs out, so a scroll event writes nothing once the mark is there; a frame's two rails share one mark, on its layer), while its thumb is dragged (`data-drag`) and as its owner's stylesheet says (under the pointer over the frame or the view). A press on it is the scrollbar's, as on a native one (`press`, caught on `window` while capturing, so neither the page nor the preview's editor hears it, and it selects no text; on an edge the editor resizes a table from, `html[data-ge-resize]`, the editor's): on the thumb it drags (the content by the thumb's travel, `range / (track − size)` px a pixel; the rail captures the pointer), beside it the thumb first goes there, centred under the pointer. Each bundle that draws rails (`tables.js`, `database.js`) has the module and its listener, each answering for its own rails.
