Pushed on past an end (`push`: every wheel event along its axis, the owner saying whether the content is at the end it pushes against; a drag past an end) its thumb gives way as "Scrollbars giving way" says, drawn as two custom properties of the rail: `--doc-scrollbar-squeeze` (that much shorter) and `--doc-scrollbar-squeeze-shift` (its own `transform`, so its far end stays against the end it is pushed to). The squeeze is the thumb's `width` and `transform`, its place the timeline's `translate`: the two compose, and the spring, drawn frame by frame while it lasts, never stops the thumb following the scroll; neither has a transition. On paper there are no rails (`@media print`).

**Scrollbars giving way** (`scroll-squeeze.js`, the squeeze of the kit's one scrollbar, `scrollbar.js` above, so of a table frame's two and a database's sideways one under its table view and its board; the user's of 2026-11-23, and his after trying it on a database: the further it is pushed the harder it gets, let go it springs back at once, only a hand still moving at the end squeezes it, never a swipe that coasts there; the thumb dragged past an end and every rail alike). What scrolls never bounces; pushed on past an end, its thumb is squeezed from the end it is pushed against, that end staying where it is. **The curve**: `rubber(pushed, most, half) = most · pushed / (pushed + half)`, a rubber band's: halfway to its most after `half` px (`SQUEEZE.half`, 120 px of the wheel's pushing), each px giving less than the one before, never quite at its most; its most is half the thumb, at most `SQUEEZE.most` (96 px) whatever the thumb's length, never leaving less than half the rail's smallest thumb (`roomOf`). What the wheel pushes is what of each event the content could not take at the end (its px less how far the content moved since the last one), at most `SQUEEZE.step` (30 px) an event, so that a mouse's notch (100 px) squeezes a little and a run of them more. A drag of the thumb past an end gives way by how far the pointer is past the point where the thumb reached it, by the same curve with `half` twice the most (`SQUEEZE.give`: the free end follows the pointer at half its speed at first, ever slower). **Only a hand that is still moving squeezes it** (`coastReader`): a touchpad goes on sending wheel events for up to a second after the fingers lift (its momentum, the content coasting), and nothing in an event says so. Every wheel event along the rail's axis is read, at the end or not, so that a swipe's momentum is known before it reaches the end: its speed (its px over the time since the last, its time stamp's, read as 4 to 40 ms) is a hand's while it goes up and down; momentum's only falls, at one steady rate. A run of events none faster than the one before (a pixel's rounding allowed in a delta of whole pixels) is read as momentum once one exponential decay explains it: from some event of it, 4 events more (5 in whole pixels), each between within 2% of the curve through the first and the last (and half a pixel), the last 15% slower than the first (25% in whole pixels), by the speed against the events' times or by the px against their count (events one a frame, their time stamps a little off). A steady hand's speed never falls that far that steadily, a jittery hand's seldom does (`tests/unit/scroll-squeeze.mjs` measures it: at most once a second of pushing for a jittery hand, none for a steady one or one in whole pixels), and a hand easing off may, and is let go of then. A swipe that comes to the end within its gesture (the content off the end at an event of it, then at it, or brought there by the scroll between two of its events: `arrive`, from the scrollbar's scroll listener, sideways only) gives one bump: the thumb squeezed by as much as the speed it came at says (`bumpOf`: 3 px at least, toward 28 px, two thirds of the way at 2 px/ms, never past its room), held 70 ms, sprung back; nothing more for the rest of that gesture, hand or momentum, so that the same speed gives the same bump every time (the user's 2026-11-24: a swipe that came to the end squeezed the thumb now and then as the reading of its run fell out, and once held it squeezed until its momentum was spent: "根据速度设置的固定效果"); a gesture begun at the end squeezes by the rubber band as the reading says. An event slower than the one before, which may be the first of a coast, is held back (`hold`): it squeezes nothing until the next ones tell, added to the push when a faster one follows, dropped when the run is read as momentum. Read so, momentum is momentum until an event is 20% faster than its slowest by 3 px or more (a hand on the pad again), a pause of 120 ms (`SQUEEZE.gesture`) or a turn the other way. **It springs back at once**: when the momentum is read (the fourth or fifth event after the fingers lift, about 40 ms at 120 Hz and 80 ms at 60 Hz, and drawn from the next frame; the thumb held still meanwhile, the steps it held back never shown), when no push has come for `SQUEEZE.gap` (60 ms: a hand at rest, a lift with no momentum, a notch), when the drag is let go, and when the content scrolls away from that end; momentum still coming never squeezes it again. The squeeze is drawn each frame (`createSqueeze`, as the rail's two custom properties, apart from the thumb's place: "Scrollbars the kit draws") by a critically damped spring, solved exactly for any time between frames: towards the push with a response of 40 ms (a notch's step drawn over a few frames, a touchpad's push followed a frame behind), back with one of 250 ms (half way in 70 ms, within half a pixel in about 280), from rest, never overshooting; a push while it springs back takes it from where it is (`unrubber`), not from whole. With reduced motion (`prefers-reduced-motion`, the kit's rule) the thumb follows the push and is whole again at once, with no spring. Not tried on a real touchpad nor on macOS: the readings above are the browser's input driven through its own protocol (`scrollSqueezeTable`, `scrollSqueezeDatabase`) and simulated hands and momentum.

A frame's height (`styles/table.css`, `tables/whole.js`): a long table of data scrolls down inside its frame, at most `min(72vh, 720px)` tall, under the header row that stays; a table read from top to bottom is shown whole, its frame as tall as its rows, read with the page (`data-doc-whole` on the frame, a runtime mark: `max-height: var(--doc-table-height, none)`), so that an Agent splits no table to show it and needs no size to write. It is shown whole when its body has at most 20 rows (`WHOLE_ROWS`: its tbodies' rows, those a filter hides among them, so that filtering never changes it; a footer's do not count, nor a first row of header cells alone in the body of a table without a thead, its header row written there), or when at least half of its body's text stands in its columns of words (`mostlyWords`): each body cell's text by its column (a merged cell in its first, `slotsOf`), each column's measured on one line in the table's font (Canvas, its cells together) and read as the allocator reads it (`columnKind`, step 2 above): a column of words is a narrative one, its cells four words or a line as wide as the Latin alphabet on average (about thirteen Chinese characters), and not mostly paths, links or long identifiers, which make a column token-heavy, nor mostly numbers (a list of figures, step 2). So how much a table's sentences say makes it whole, not how many of its cells hold one: a glossary or a list of findings, a term and a sentence to a row, is whole at any length, and so is a table of a date, a title, a group and a note a sentence long (an Agent's year table: a quarter of its cells, two thirds of its text); a long table of names and numbers, of paths or of links is not, nor one whose column of notes holds less than half of its text. A log written as a table, a time and a message in words to a row, is whole too. A short table's cells are not read for it; a long one's text is read after every layout (`markWhole`, `refreshLayout`) and what it said kept until that text or the table's font changes, so a resize reads nothing again; a row put in or words typed in the preview can change it. A frame that has just taken the kit's height keeps the caret in sight (`keepCaret`: scrolled down as little as brings the caret's cell over its bottom edge, as Shift+Enter on the last of 20 rows puts the caret in the 21st), and the page editor's corner + has the kit lay the table out before it scrolls the frame to where the rows went in. A height written on the frame (`--doc-table-height`, px or `none`: the reader's drag of its bottom edge, or the author's) holds either way. The editor's `scrollHeight()` asks the frame's `max-height` with its own height taken off, so 一键优化 and the bottom edge's drag read the kit's decision (no limit for a frame shown whole: a drag to its bottom writes nothing). The frame's `rows-hidden` note (above) and `gallery qa`'s `table-rows` therefore speak of long tables of data alone.

A table whose columns the reader sized in the page editor (`--doc-column-width` on a cell of each column, written on the first row's cells) is laid out at those widths instead (`manualLayout`): one `colgroup` of pixel widths, the table as wide as their sum and its frame as wide as the table up to the reading width (scrolling past it), its text wrapping in its columns (no `data-column-kind`); a column without a width there is as wide as its widest cell, 120 to 240px. In a frame with a width of its own (`--doc-span`) those widths are proportions: the columns fill the frame, past the reading width as much wider as its share says. Columns are read by slots (`src/shared/table-slots.mjs`, the one reading the page editor and the server share: a span past its section, a hole or an overlap tiles no rectangle and leaves the table to the browser): a column's width is on its first cell alone (one column wide) that has one, top down, and a merged cell holds none, so a table with merged cells is laid out at the reader's widths too. A table with merged header or body cells is never sorted nor read for numeric columns (`mergedCells`: a header cell is then not one column's; a footer's merged cell changes nothing). A table the kit leaves to the browser's layout (a header of more than one row, or spans, without widths of the reader's; a table without a header row takes its columns from its first row) fills such a frame too (`data-doc-filled`), the browser sharing the room among its columns. A cell's look classes (`doc-align-left`, `-center`, `-right`; a text color or a fill, `doc-text-*`, `doc-highlight-*`, the fill over the whole cell) are in `styles/table.css`. A picture amid a cell's words (单元格里的图片, an `<img>` of `assets/`: the page editor's "Pictures in a cell") is as wide as its `width` (pixels) or, with none, 240px at most, never past its cell, its height by its ratio and its middle on the line's, so its row grows with it (`styles/table.css`); the export embeds it as any reference to the folder.

Wide tables (宽表格, the document's look `tables: "wide"`, `data-doc-tables="wide"` on `<html>`; `tables/across.js`, what it reads of the page `src/shared/table-across.mjs`; the user's 表格跨越边界 of 2026-11-20): a table wider than the text, its frame standing in the body itself, stands centred on its line and goes past the text's edges on both sides, as far as the page's area at full width; only one wider than that scrolls in its frame, the frame then as wide as the area, centred as far as the area lets it. "Wider than the text" is the layout's own judgment: an allocated table whose columns at their readable floors are still wider than the frame's room in the text (the one that scrolled there before), a table of the reader's column widths (`manualLayout`) or of a width of its own past the reading width (`--doc-span` over 1) wider than that room, a table the browser lays out (merged cells) whose width with the area for its frame is more than the text's (read with the frame at the area's width, put back at once); a table that fits the text, by wrapping its prose too, is as it was. An allocated table's width for that question is read with its long identifiers whole (`keepLongWords`, step 4 above): one that fits the text only by breaking its dataset names goes past the text, where the area may hold them whole (and breaks them there only if that brings it inside the area); one that fits it by wrapping its headers or its lists stays. An allocated one past the text has its columns allocated again over the area's room (the frame's room less its borders and padding), so they wrap as little as that lets them: one a pixel too wide for the text takes the room its columns want, up to the area, not the least past the text. The frame placed so (`placeAcross`) has its width, `max-width: none` and its left margin written on it (inline, as the layout's widths are; centred on its parent's content box, moved in where that would take it past the area's edges, the margin in whole device pixels) and `data-doc-across`, a runtime mark (`components/table.mjs`); back in the text (`clearAcross`) they are taken off and the layout's width written again. "In the body" (`inBody`): every element between the frame and `main.doc-page` a `section`, `div` or `article` of no class but `doc-section` whose content box is the page's text to the pixel; a container (a highlight block, a callout, columns, a disclosure, tabs, a list item, a card, a detail card) keeps its tables in its own frame, as ever, scrolling there (替用户定的: the container has a frame of its own). The page's area is the page at full width's: the body's content box less `--doc-area-start` and `--doc-area-end` (`styles/page.css`: registered `<length>`s, so the root's computed style gives them in px; `--doc-wide-gutter` each in a page of the kit, its 全宽 gutter), the page editor giving its own beside the outline and the cards of 批注 (`margin.mjs` `roomCss`: the side's room at the left, the outline's and the side's or the cards' at the right as its rule of a page read full width has them; beside the cards of a page read in its column, the column's right edge, as they stand right of it there, so such a table goes past the text on the left alone; never inside the column). On a narrow screen (the kit's 760px layout) no table goes past the text, and where the area gives no more than the text (a narrow window in the workbench, a page at 全宽, whose column is the area) nothing changes. The look's attribute sizes no word (`contextOf` leaves it out of a cell's key), and a change of it is `doc-layout-change` (the look's request, the reader's switch), which lays every table out again. What stands on and around a table goes by its frame as it stands: its scrollbars and fade, its header row that sticks in a frame that scrolls down, the page editor's handle, grips, strips and frames, an Agent's change bar at its left edge (`fills.mjs`, by the block's box), its 批注 marker, bar and button right of it (`annotate/` `pastText`: right of the table where it reaches past the column, not over it), and the bar of the section it stands in at the text's right edge as ever, dotted across the table, which keeps the pointer there (`handle/` `crossings`, `table-across.mjs` `placedIn`; editor.md "The room past the text"; from 2026-11-23 to 11-24 it stood right of the table). A column's strip dragged (`page/table/widths.mjs` `follow`) keeps the column's edge under the pointer, the table growing on both sides (its width brought on until the edge is where the pointer is, three layouts at most, each reading how far the edge went), and the table's own width dragged from its right edge (`resize/`, `--doc-span`) puts its right end at the pointer past the text too (twice the pointer's way from the line's middle, or from the area's left edge where the area stops it: the less of the two; the notice says `超宽表 · 表宽 …px，居中超出正文` where it fits the area). On paper a frame placed past the text stands in the paper's column as every other (`styles/table.css`). An export of the view takes the placing off (`dehydrate`): the copy's kit places it again by the copy's look. **A table's own word** (`data-doc-wide="on"` or `"off"` on its frame, in the file; the page editor's block menu writes it, its 宽表格, and natural HTML moves one written on a bare `<table>` onto the frame it makes; the user's of 2026-11-23: 相当于是直接控制这个表格的，之前的右上角的那个是控制这个页面的) is over the page's look for that table (`wideTable`: "on" goes past the text where it is wider, "off" stays in it, none goes by the page), every rule above as it was; the table is laid out again as it changes (`tables/index.js` watches it on the frame). The page's own switch sets every table, the blocks' words taken away (the workbench's 更多, `gallery set --wide-tables`: [workbench.md](workbench.md)); an export's reader turning its display menu's 宽表格 on or off sets every table too, whatever their words (`data-doc-reader-tables` on `<html>` beside the look's attribute, kept in a copy of the view: `READER_WIDE`, `components/page.mjs`), except while the page editor edits the page, where the file's words show (`html[data-ge-editing]`).

Manual acceptance for a horizontally scrolling sticky-first table: set one first-column cell to `background-color: transparent` (or `rgba(255, 0, 0, .2)`), scroll horizontally, and confirm its computed background is the table's fill and no underlying text shows; the scenario `tableStickyFill` draws this for the sticky first column and the header column, light and dark, and compares the pixels. Also confirm generated column ratios sum to 1 and respect readable floors.

## Opening a picture large

图片放大 (`runtime/zoom.js`, `styles/zoom.css`): a reader clicks a picture of the document and it opens large over the page, wherever the page editor is not editing the page: an exported file (offline: the viewer shows the export's own `data:` picture), a build, the preview's 演示视图 (`?present`). Nothing is written for it: a document gets it without a word in its markup, and `report.html` never holds any of it; on the page's elements the runtime writes only the marks that offer a picture to the keyboard (below), while the page is read.

- **Which pictures** (`src/shared/zoom.mjs`, the page editor's rule too): an `<img>` of the page (`main.doc-page`), whatever holds it: an image block, a table's cell, a card (one of the kit's or a drawing of the document's own), a column, a flowchart's node, a list item, a detail card. Not one inside a link (`a[href]`), a button, a `summary`, a `label`, a `select`, a role of a button, a link or a tab, or a detail card's trigger (a click there is theirs); not an icon (`.doc-icon`: the page's, a panel's, a node's, emoji pictures among them; a link's icon, `.doc-link-mention`; a bookmark's pictures; a file's); not one shown smaller than 48px each way (`ZOOM_LEAST`: an icon's or an emoji's size in a line of text), nor one that did not load (an SVG of no size of its own counts as loaded). A click with a modifier key, or one a script already handled (`defaultPrevented`), stays the browser's or the script's.
- **The keyboard**: a picture that opens is a tab stop while the page is read, a button that opens a dialog, named by what it does in the kit's words (`open-image-named`, 放大查看：its `alt`, else `open-image`, 放大查看图片; `src/render/kit/words.js`): the runtime marks it `data-doc-zoom` (`ZOOM_MARK`, `src/shared/zoom.mjs`) with `tabindex="0"`, `role="button"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-label`, and the rule takes that role as no control of the author's own. Enter or Space on it opens the viewer (Space scrolls nothing; a key with a modifier, or while composing, stays the browser's), and Esc gives the focus back to it. From the keyboard it shows the kit's focus ring (`:focus-visible`: 2px of `--doc-accent`, 2px out, as a disclosure's summary), from the pointer none. Whether a picture opens is the rule's, its size and its load among it, so DocKit watches the page's pictures (`ZOOM_PICTURES`, the component `zoom`: their width, their load, an element around them shown) and the marks come and go with it; a picture the author made a control of their own (a `tabindex`, a `role`, an `aria-label` or `aria-haspopup` of its own) is left as it is. An export of the view takes the marks off (the image description's `runtime.attrs`).
- **The cursor**: zoom-in on a picture that opens and on no other. Whether one does is the rule's (its size among it), which no selector says, so the runtime marks `<html>` with `doc-zoom-hover` while the pointer is on one (`:root.doc-zoom-hover .doc-page img:hover`).
- **The viewer** (the page editor's viewer, its reader's part): a modal `<dialog class="doc-zoom">` put at the end of `<body>` as it opens and taken away as it closes, so the page under it is inert. The window under a veil of `rgb(15 15 15 / 80%)` in either theme (the dialog itself covers the window: a click on it is a click beside the picture); the picture in the middle, fitted to the window with 48px around it from 1020px wide (16px below that), its caption's room taken from the height, never past its own size (`naturalWidth` CSS pixels, as the browser shows a picture; an SVG of no size of its own as large as the window lets the shape it is shown in be: `zoomFit`), again as the window changes size; under it the caption of the image block or card it is the one picture of (`figcaption` or `.doc-figure-caption`: its words as the page shows them, a copy; none when it has no words or the figure holds several pictures), 14px in light gray; a close button at the top right (32px, the viewer bar's dark square and white cross, a lighter fill under the pointer, a white focus ring, no blue). Its name for a screen reader is the picture's `alt`, else its caption, else 查看图片.
- **Closing**: Esc (heard on the window before anything else and stopped there: a detail card under it stays open, and the preview's 演示视图, which ends on an Esc its page hears, goes on), the close button, or a click anywhere but on the picture (a link in the caption is followed). One viewer at a time: another picture's click (a script's) closes the first.
- **Focus and scroll**: the viewer takes the focus as it opens (no ring; the close button is the next stop of Tab, the page inert behind it), and the focus goes back where it was as it closes. Meanwhile `<html>` carries `doc-zoom-open` (`overflow: hidden` on a screen: the page keeps still under the wheel) and, where the page had a scrollbar, `doc-zoom-gutter` (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`: its room kept, nothing shifts).
- **Motion and print**: it fades in over 150ms, the picture growing from 96%; with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` neither. It never prints: `beforeprint` closes it, a print rule hides it (`display: none`), the page is held on a screen only, and a click while the print media applies opens nothing.
- **Editing**: while the page editor edits the page (`html[data-ge-editing]`) a click is the editor's (it selects the block), no cursor is shown and no picture is marked (the marks come off as the editor starts, and Enter opens nothing); the editor opens its own viewer (a double-click on a picture the reader's rule opens, 展开 and 全屏查看 on an image block: [editor.md](editor.md), "Images").
- **An export of the current view** taken while it is open leaves it out: the image description's `runtime` names the dialog (`generated`) and the three classes on `<html>` (`root`), which `DocKit.dehydrate` takes off.

`tests/unit/runtime.mjs` checks the rule, `zoomFit`, the viewer's ways to open and close, the focus, the page left as it was, nothing while editing or printing, and the styles; the browser scenario `imageZoom` checks it in an exported file, in 演示视图 and in the page editor.

## Links between documents

A link from one document to another is an ordinary relative link between their files (`../<slug>/doc.html`, with `#<block id>` for a block: [workbench.md](workbench.md), "A document's addresses"), which no one has to write anew while a document lives in its folder. Nothing in a preview does: the page and the link stay as the file says, and a click is the editor's. What leaves the workspace does (`page-link-rewrite.mjs`, `rewritePageLinks`, on the page's tree by the places of its elements, so that a code block showing such a link, a script's text and a comment stay, and nothing else of the page changes by a byte): an export's file links to the file of a document the same export holds, relative (`exportLinks`: in `exportReports`, for `gallery export` and 导出报告, the documents of that one run, each `<slug>.html` beside the others; in the workbench's 导出, `src/transfer/export.mjs`, its pages), and a link to any other keeps its words alone (a chip's icon goes with its link). A PDF does that with all of them, for it has no place to be relative to. 复制页面内容 and what is copied to another program write the full address. A link is read as a click reads it (`fromFile`), and one the parser reads twice, carrying a link left open into the next block, is edited once, by where its tag starts. An address with a host may be another program's on this computer, or another workspace's: it is a document's only if the workspace holds it (`documentLinks`) by its folder name or the link it has now (`addressOn`), and stays as it is if not. 导出当前视图副本 (`ui.js`) stands alone: a link to another document keeps its words there too, a workbench's address as a path alone among them, whatever it names, for from a disk it would open the disk's root. It is done on what the render made and kept nowhere: a preview, the built previews and a document's own file never hold a link written for an export.

**Page blocks** (`a.doc-link-mention` with a document's address: `shared/components/mention.mjs`, [editor.md](editor.md) "Page blocks"). The file holds a page block's title as it was when the block was made and an empty icon, so an Agent reads words and no rename writes the documents that link to the one renamed. What a page shows is one step of `renderReportHtml`, the last, shared by the preview, a presentation and every export (`page-chips.mjs`, `drawPageChips`, on the page's tree by the places of its elements): the title and icon the target's `doc.json` has now, the icon by the icon route in the preview (in its own color, as a browser tab's) and a data: URL in an export; an emoji the pack lacks is its character drawn in the system's emoji font in an SVG (`iconImage`), never none. What reads the page as text takes the same step for the title alone (`mode: "words"`, no picture): 复制页面内容, an export as Markdown and search. An empty icon stays for the kit, which draws a page (`mention.css`), and a table counts a link's icon in its column's width (`tables/layout.js`). A target that is not there keeps its words, marked `data-doc-gone`, which no file holds. The export's rewrite comes after it and takes a chip as any link: its words and icon kept and its address rewritten, or its link and icon gone and its words left. Since a page now shows what other documents say, the preview's kept pages all go when a `doc.json` changes or a document comes or goes (`routes.mjs` `changed`); a page open stays as it was served, and shows the new title the next time it is opened.

`pageLinkNotes` (`pipeline.mjs`), reading a link as a click does, notes a link to a document or a block that is not there, within a page a `#<id>` no element has, and a relative link that reads like one to a document and leads to none (`strayLink`: `../目标页/doc.html`, a title for a folder, `../target`): a note, for a draft may link to what is still to be written. A workbench's address is read by the folders beside the page, a page id being a folder name's. Of a document that is not there an address with a host says nothing, for it may be another program's.

## Links to blocks

A block's id is no element's id (`data-block-id`), so a browser's own jump finds nothing. `runtime/reveal.js` goes to a block for a reader (an exported file, 演示视图): the hash, on load once the page is drawn (`data-doc-drawn`) and as it changes, finds the block, closes a detail card that does not hold it, opens the disclosures, the tabs and the card around it and brings it to the middle of the window. Nothing is selected and nothing saved. A click on a link `#<block id>` to a block of the page (with no key held; a link to an element's own id stays the browser's jump, a step of its history already, and one to a detail card is `dialogs.js`'s) is a step of the history, as the browser's jump to an anchor is, and the way back comes to where the reader was (decided 2026-11-06): the entry left keeps `docPlace` (`src/shared/reading-place.mjs` `placeIn`: the scroll position; the first element of the page's `main` that starts in the window, none in a detail card and none that sticks or is fixed, named by where it stands in `main` and checked by the id of the block it is in, an Agent's blocks carrying none until a commit or an export, with how far down the window it stands; the tab each Sheet area shows; and the detail cards open, in the order they opened, each with how far it is scrolled), the entry made `docBlock`, its address the link's (`history.pushState`); the way back puts the cards back as they were, the tabs, and that element where it stood, what the jump opened above it moving the rest (`goBackTo`; the scroll position where that element is not found), and the way forth goes to the block again; neither goes anywhere else on the hashchange of that step. A link to the hash the page is at makes no entry, and goes to it again. 演示视图's link to a block of its own page by the page's address goes the same way (`doc-jump-request`, from `present-links.mjs`). The page editor makes its own ([workbench.md](workbench.md) "One document open"; a page alone in a tab keeps `docPlace` and `docBlock` the same way, its address as it is). `DocKit.reveal(target)`, the part that opens what hides an element, is the page editor's too (its outline's jumps, and `revealBlock`, which selects the block): one rule for what hides a block. While the page edits (`html[data-ge-editing]`) the rest is the editor's. An export keeps every block's id, which is why such a link opens on disk (scenario `pageLinkExport`).

## Sheets

The runtime starts every Sheet area once the page has loaded (and one put back later): its tab is kept in a state named after its `data-doc-sheets` (`sheets-<name>`, one per name), and `DocSheets.get(target)` gives a script `{ get, set }`. It validates matching unique tabs and panels and fills in ARIA references; Arrow Left/Right, Home and End move along the row. Nothing stands beside the page: there is no copy of the tabs at the window's edge, so a page of Sheets lays out as any other. The runtime removes its listeners when the area leaves the page. The `.doc-sheet-shell` styles own tab padding, gaps, and panel spacing.

## Databases

A 多维表格 (`div.doc-database`) is a block of records the reader sees in views, filters, sorts and edits where it stands; its file format is `src/shared/database.mjs`'s, its drawing `kit/database/`, the editor's side [editor.md](editor.md) "Databases".

**The file.** Two parts, read by `readElement` and `readDatabase` and written by `writeDatabase` and `tableMarkup` (the page, the server's checks and `gallery read` alike): a `<script type="application/json">` holding `{ columns, views }` and a `<table>` whose header cells name the columns (`th[data-column]` the column's id, its words the column's name) and whose rows are the records, a cell its value in words. A column is `{ id, name, type }` with `options` for a select or multi (`{ name, color, done }`, the nine colors of `src/shared/colors.mjs`, an option's id written only where it is not its name), `formula` for a formula (columns by name in what an Agent writes, read as ids; it has no cell in the table), `numberFormat`, `width`, `align`, and `notes` for the column of the records' notes (words). A value is written as words: a number as its value, the shortest words that are it (`String`, every digit it holds: `0.1234567890123456`, `1e-13`, `1e+300`; `numberText`; `60%`, `1,234.5` and `¥12` read as numbers, a percent by moving its point, `numberOf`, so that `12.3456%` is `0.123456` as that reads), an option by its name and several apart by `, ` (what parts names is one rule, a file's cell's and the kit's wherever a reader types, pastes or fills them, `optionNames`: a comma, a Chinese one, `、`, a line break, a tab; a semicolon is a name's, so `研发;测试` is one option however its words go. A name holds none of what parts names: each side that names an option makes it a space, `optionName`, so that it reads back as itself, a view's filter or board naming one by such a name, `C、D`, finding the option `C D`), a date `2026-11-12`, a day of the calendar (other spellings of a date read as one; `2026-13-45` and `2026-02-30` are none, `realDay`, wherever dates count: no date filter keeps one, no earliest or latest, no `DAYS`, its day's words none, a calendar showing its record under 未排期), a checkbox `✓` or nothing, a URL as it is, a line break `<br>` (`cellValue`, `cellText`). What is no value of its column's kind is kept as written and drawn gray (`v-dirty`); an option a cell names that its column does not list is added as the file is read and written only once the reader changes that column (`added`). A cell is written back as the file had it (`raw`: its markup, where its value written anew would not give it back) until the reader changes its value or what its value says (an option renamed): two names in a single select, `1,234.5`, words that are no date, a url cell's one link `<a href>` (its value the address, its words kept with it until the cell changes, which writes the address alone). What the format cannot hold it never rewrites: JSON that does not read and a table holding more than a header row and rows of cells of words, line breaks and a url cell's link (a `<tfoot>`, a `<caption>` or `<colgroup>`, a second header row or `<tbody>`, cells past the header, `colspan`, an attribute, a comment, other markup, a link in another column) make it read-only (`problems` as `{ code, detail }`, `readOnly`; their words `problemSaid`, in Chinese and English), drawn with a note under its head (`.db-note`), and `gallery check` fails JSON that does not read (with the line it stops at: the position JSON.parse names, else the first comma before a closing bracket, two values with no comma or a comment, `jsonErrorLine`) and says the rest among the notes to act on first (`fix … database read-only:`, `databaseFixes` of `component-checks.mjs`), each problem at its line and column (`databaseProblems`, on parse5's tree as on a DOM; `problemPlaces` of `database-checks.mjs`). It notes too, each at its line, what of a database an Agent wrote that the format reads otherwise than meant (`database-checks.mjs` `databaseWarnings`: a field, a kind of column or a layout that is none, a view naming a column by its name or naming none, a filter whose condition or value is not of its column's kind, totals of no kind or hidden without `showCalc`, a board, a calendar or a chart of no column or the wrong one, an option of no color, a formula naming a column or a function there is not or naming formulas round, a header cell of no column and a column of no header cell, rows of fewer cells, cells that are no value of their column, options the cells name and the column does not list, what the div holds besides its two parts, a `boardHidden`, which the format keeps and the board does not honour: it draws a column for every option): it still shows and takes edits, so each is a note, never a failure; the filter conditions, totals and formula language it holds them to are the kit's own (`data/query.js`, `data/formula.js`), and what the page writes is never noted (none on a database as `writeDatabase` writes one). And for every database it prints a line of information (`database <slug>: …`, `databaseReading`, pipeline.mjs `notesByKind`'s `databases`): its records and columns, whether the page edits it, how wide it is drawn (its own `data-doc-wide` over the document's look, said where either makes it wide or it says so itself), and what each view shows as the page works it out (the kit's `applyView`, `calc`, `groupRows`, `boardColumns`, `recordsByDay`, `chartData`, `getValue`: each view's filters in words, how many records and a filtered view's titles, a table's totals and its groups with their counts and totals, a board's columns and their counts, a calendar's records with a date and without, what a card shows, the title first, and its size, a chart's numbers and its color), then its number columns drawn as bars or stars, the records that have a body (those keeping their notes beside it said), and each formula's values (every record's where there are 20 or fewer, its failures or none), so that a database written as meant is still said and an Agent sees a view without a browser (the blind test of 2026-11-24, an Agent who could not tell whether a body, a width or a group's total it wrote was there); `gallery qa` counts the databases it saw in its summary. Its JSON is a script of type `application/json` alone, in any case (`isJsonType`: as a browser's selector `[type="application/json"]` reads a type, which the page and the kit find the part by; spelled as the file has it), an `ld+json` one none of its, on the page, in `readElement` and on the server. Attributes of its table's sections, rows and cells that change nothing it holds (`harmlessAttribute`: `scope`, `class`, `id`, `lang`, `dir`, `title`, `data-*` but `data-column` and Gallery's own) are kept where they were (a header cell's on its column, `headAttrs`; a row's and its cells' on the record, `attrs`; the head's, header row's and body's, `shape`), on a cell the reader changed too; `colspan`, `rowspan`, `style` and any other make it read-only, and so does one of those that no write of the table could carry, what the page and the server refuse in a data part (`refusedInData`: `data-block-id`, a value that is an address of code, `javascript:` and the like, a cell's link's too: problems `attribute`, `code`), rather than leave it looking editable with every change refused. The page writes a row it rewrites as a browser writes it; the server keeps every row and section the change did not touch as the file has it, byte for byte, one laid out a cell a line too (the blanks between them the page's) (editor.md "Databases", `keptInside`). Each header cell is a column of its own: one naming its column (`data-column`) is that column, any other the JSON's column of its words that no other cell is of yet, else a column of words, so that two cells of the same words are two columns and a write gives back every column the table holds; two cells naming one column (problem `same-column`) and cells under a formula's header (by its id, or by its name where no other column has it: `formula-cells`) make it read-only, each such cell drawn as a column of words of its own meanwhile. A view is `{ id, name, layout }` (`table`, `board` with `boardBy`, `list`, `gallery`, `calendar` with `dateBy`, `chart` with `chart: { kind, x, agg, y }`) and what it says of `hidden`, `filters`, `sorts`, `groupBy` and `calc`, its defaults left out. A table with no script (or a script that does not read: `problems`) is read as columns of words named by its header, the first the title, and a table's view; the first change writes the script. A change writes both parts, the table in the layout its first lines had (`layoutOf`, its indentation, and its rows' where they stand further in than its sections, as an Agent writes them), so that a database read and written again is the same bytes. **Its records' bodies.** A record may have a body, the document's own blocks: its row says its id (`data-record`, `RECORD_ATTRIBUTE`: letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `RECORD_ID`; read as the record's id where it is one and the first row to say it, `named`, the others by their place, `r<n>`, none another row's id) and the `div.doc-database-pages` after its table (`PAGES_CLASS`) holds a `<div data-doc-slot="record:<id>">` (`recordSlot`, `slotRecord`) for it, the first of its name its body (`databasePages`, `bodyOf`, on a DOM or parse5's tree alike), a record with none having no slot. The format's parts never hold it: its blocks are the page editor's (editor.md "Databases"), the kit shows them in a record's details (below), and with no drawing they stand after the table as written (the kit's CSS hides the pages while it draws). `gallery check` notes (`database-checks.mjs` `bodyWarnings`) a body no row names, a second of one record, what in the pages is no body, words straight in one, a database in one, a row's id that is none or another row's, and bodies together more than one commit writes, and (`repeatedNotes`) a body that opens with its record's notes word for word, which the details then show twice (an Agent copied them in and left the cell, where the page moves them); a slot in the pages named otherwise than `record:<id>` fails (`component-checks.mjs`).

**The drawing.** A built-in component, `DocKit.component({ id: "database", owns: true, on: ["source", "editable"] })` (`kit/database/index.js`): the kit's own modules (plain ES modules, no UI framework, as the kit's tables, charts and code blocks are), bundled by esbuild into one file, minified (`assets.mjs`: `database.js` after the runtime, state, tokens and `database.css`; about 200 KB, 70 KB gzipped), loaded only by a page that holds one (the description's `uses`) and written whole into an export. Its description's `data` parts give it the package context an instance has (`context.edit`, `readJSON`, `canEdit`, `state`, `listen`: the kernel's `writesData`). It mounts a `.doc-database-app` in the host (generated: the export's dehydrate takes it out), holding its view (`.db-view`) and its layer (`.db-layer`: where its cards, a record's details and its words of a moment stand, each in the top layer), and hides the file's script and table while it stands (`[data-doc-database]`). The kit's passes over the page's text leave all of a database alone, its parts (data, never shown as text) and its drawing alike: `runtime/spacing.js` (the spaces beside code, ranges, long runs, an item's line) takes it as inert and `math.js` does not look for formulas in it (`DATA_ONLY` of `src/shared/components/database.mjs`); its reading goes through a `span.doc-long-run` or `span.doc-range` all the same (`readElement`: neither is the file's markup, nor in a cell's `raw`), so that no decoration of the kit's makes it read-only or reaches the file. It draws the views as tabs (`app.js`: a tab each, dragged into their order, the round + after them adding one, its layouts a grid of cards as the view settings' 布局 shows them, a click switching to it or, on the one shown, opening its menu, as a right click does on any; the row fading at an end past which tabs lie, a tab pressed that stands partly past the row's edge or under that fade slid in whole, `reveal`: the row glides there, at once where its menu opens, and at once for a reader who asks for less motion), the tools at the right (filter, sort, search, the view's settings: a tool whose rule is on has its icon in the accent, no fill), the filter bar (`filters.js`: a chip per filter or a row of buttons with counts, the sorts' chip), the view shown in its body ("Databases: views" below), a record's details at the side or in the middle (`peek.js`), the columns' menus (rename, type, options, hide, sort, filter, delete: `props.js`; a kind changed, `convertProp`, reads each value's words, every digit of a number kept, as a file's cell of the new kind reads them: an option for every name there is, `Done` and `done` two, however many; a box checked, unchecked, or the words that are neither kept gray; a single select from a multi-select the first of its options) and a formula language (`data/formula.js`: arithmetic, `&`, comparisons, `IF`, `ROUND`, `SUM`, `MAX`, `MIN`, `DAYS` and more over columns named `{name}`, a failure said in the cell, never `Infinity`). The view shown, each view's filter bar open or shut, the details' width and each layout's own reading state are the reader's (`context.state`), never the file's. A change is one write of both parts (`store.js` `commit`); one made by typing (a cell's words, a filter's, a record's title or notes in its details, a column's, a view's or an option's name) is written at once, a character a write (what an input method composes once it is chosen), the writes of one gesture one step of undo (the edit's `merge`: editor.md "Databases"), so that nothing typed waits unwritten; a drag (a column's width, `merge` "drag:…") is drawn as it goes and written as it ends, and a date typed in its cell is held until it reads as a date and the reader is done (`store.hold`); while it holds something back the drawing says so (doc-holding on the host, bubbling: true, then false), and writes it at once on doc-flush, which the page sends before an undo or a redo, a close and a hide (the contract any drawing of a database keeps: editor.md "Databases"). A write the page does not take (more than one commit holds) takes out the empty parts it put in for it. What the file says is the truth: its parts are read again as they change (an undo, the Agent's write), the drawing's own write known by its signature and not read twice. Where nothing takes edits (an export, 演示视图, the page before its editor) the reader still switches views, filters, sorts and opens records, for this reading alone, and no control of editing is drawn. **宽多维表格** (the document's look `databases: "wide"`, `data-doc-databases="wide"` on `<html>`; the top bar's 更多 under 宽表格, an export's display menu, `gallery set --wide-databases`): a 多维表格 standing in the body itself whose table views are wider than the text is placed as a wide table is (`kit/database/across.js`, by `shared/table-across.mjs`'s `inBody` and `areaOf`): its drawing, head, bar and view together, centred on its line past the text on both sides, at most the page's area at full width, its view scrolling in its own frame only past that; the width and left margin written on `.doc-database-app` (class `is-wide`), placed again as the look, the page's width or the window change (`layout`, `resize`). Every view of one database takes one width (`viewsWidth`; the user's of 2026-11-23: 看板什么的还是和之前的一样窄的，就是这些要同步，都是一样的宽的), that of its widest table view (`table.js` `tableWidthOf`, the columns it shows and the room past the last, whether it shows or not; the table shown as drawn, `width()`): its board, list, cards, calendar and chart as wide as its table, so that going from one to another never makes it wider or narrower, a narrower table view filling that width; one whose table views fit the text, and one with no table view, stays in the text whatever it shows (替用户定的). The drawing says the width its views take, wide or not (`data-doc-views-width` on `.doc-database-app`, a runtime mark), by which the page editor's block menu offers its own switch where it would go past the text (`databaseWider`); nothing of the page is measured for a database that stays in the text, whose every change (a cell typed) places it again. A database's own word (`data-doc-wide="on"` or `"off"` on its `div.doc-database`, in the file: the block menu's 宽多维表格, `editor/components/database/page.mjs` `menuItems`) is over the page's look for it (`wideDatabase`), the drawing placed again as it changes (`kit/database/index.js` watches it), and the reader's own 宽多维表格 in an export's display menu over that again (as for tables, below). What the page editor draws beside it goes by its drawing as it stands (the user's of 2026-11-23: 手柄没有同步过去…右侧分区的竖线也是直接贯穿了，批注的位置也是没有在正确位置): its box is its host's out to the drawing (`editor/page/geometry.mjs` `extentOf`, `table-across.mjs` `drawnPast`), so its handle stands left of the drawing and its fill and an Agent's change bar go by it; a section's bar and its 批注 marker and bar stand right of it, as a wide table's. One in a container (a disclosure, columns, a highlight block, a custom block's slot) and every one on a narrow screen stay in the text; on paper it stands in the column. Offered only where the page holds such a database (`holdsBodyDatabases`). Its words are `w("中文", …values)` (`kit/database/words.js`) with their English in `words-en.js`, by the kit's language (`kitLanguage()`: the UI's where Gallery shows the page, the page's `lang` in an export).

**A record's details** (`peek.js`) stand in the top layer, at the side or in the middle (the view's `open`): its title, its values and its body. A record with a body is drawn in its database's pages themselves, made the details while they show (`data-doc-peek` "side" or "center", a popover, `data-doc-surface` so that the page editor draws its tools over them, the slot shown `data-doc-shown` and the others hidden, the details' head drawn before it as the kit's own: `.db-peek-pin`, sticky, its small bar and its edge, and `.db-peek-chrome`, its title and values, each `doc-database-app` and `data-doc-dev`), the slot staying where the file has it; the pages scroll as one, head and body; a record with none yet is drawn the same way, around the body the page makes for it where an editor takes edits (`slotOf`: `doc-database-body` with `provisional` { record, the id its row is to have, `newRecordId`; text, its notes; write }, editor.md "Databases"): a slot holding an empty paragraph of the editor's, or one of its notes (its notes then not among its values), marked as the page's (`data-ge-provisional`), nothing written by opening it; the first change there makes it the file's, the drawing's `write` of that step (`giveId`) renaming the record to that id in this reading (`store.renameRecord`) and in the details, so that they are not drawn again and nothing on the screen moves. The details going (once slid away), or going to another record, the drawing asks the page to take it away again where it is still the page's (`drop`, `letGo`); its details are drawn again only where the slot they stand around changes (`index.js` `watchBodies`: the pages made or taken away, a slot made or taken away; `slotNow`), its `dehydrate` takes it off a copy. Where the page makes none (a database made a moment ago, its first save on its way) its notes are drawn read only where its body would be (`.peek-body-new`, asked again as it is pressed). Records deleted with bodies are asked of the page the same way (`store.deleteRows`), a record duplicated carries no body nor id (`duplicateRow`). Where nothing edits (an export) a body shows read only and a record with none shows its notes, if any, in the layer (`.peek`). A record with a body shows its notes, where it has them, as a value among the others. At the side the details slide in from the window's right edge and out again as the workbench's sidebar does (280 ms, `cubic-bezier(.32, .72, 0, 1)`, their transform and opacity; their opacity alone over 120 ms for a reader who asks for less motion), and while they stand there the page's own scrollbar is out of sight (`:root.doc-peek-side`, its room the page's padding meanwhile, `--doc-page-bar`, so that nothing moves), the one scrollbar at the window's right the details' (the user's of 2026-11-24); the page under it still scrolls by the wheel (替用户定的). Esc in the body is the editor's; ↑ ↓ there too.

**Its look** is the one the user approved in the drawing's first version (2026-11-23), its colors, radii and fonts the kit's and Gallery's: its tokens (`--doc-*`: the text, the lines, the hover and pressed fills, the card's radius and padding), the nine colors of an option as a fill, a mark and an ink (`--db-fill`, `--db-mark`, `--db-ink`, light and dark: `ui/colors.js`), dark mode by the theme's tokens. Its cards (`ui/menu.js` `pop`: a `.popover` in the database's layer, in the top layer so that nothing of the page, nor a record's details, covers it; one stack for the page, a press outside closing those that do not hold it, Esc the top one, one opened from anywhere but an open card taking the place of those open) hold Gallery's menu rows (`.menu-item`: 28px, a 20px icon at 1.25px, the row fill under the pointer, a small gray heading, a hairline between parts, a box for what is on or off); no card is wider than Gallery's menus (250px, `ui/menu.js` `MENU_WIDTH`, every card's unless it says otherwise: a view's settings and its pages, a column's menu, a filter's, the sorts', adding a view or a column) but those that need more: a formula's editor (420px), a filter's card of a date (284px: its five relative days on one line above the month) and the list of a cell's options (as wide as the cell it hangs under, 240px at least); the small menus stay as narrow as their words: a tab's and a row's (200px), a column's kinds (200px), an option's (220px), a sum's (180px) and a filter's conditions (170px). A list in a card shows so much before it scrolls: a database's columns fourteen rows (a filter or a sort added, the columns shown, edited), a filter's options twelve. A view's settings have small gray headings for titles, no × and switches for what is on or off. A filter drawn as a row of buttons (分段) is one light fill with no line (lighter than a row's hover); the option picked a near-white part of it in light, a lighter gray in dark, its words full and medium, raised by no shadow; one under the pointer half way to it. An option's colors are picked as the editor's color pickers pick theirs (the block menu's 颜色: `ui/colors.js` `swatches`): a swatch of the option's mark each, five to a line, in its color's ring (`RINGS` of `src/shared/colors.mjs`, the editor's own), two pixels under the pointer, the strong ring around the one chosen, its name in a tip. Its buttons have no border (no fill at rest, gray under the pointer); a tool whose rule is on (a filter, a sort, a search) says so by its icon's color alone; a focus ring is drawn inside what it rings (nothing that clips it cuts it off). Its tooltips are the kit's (`data-doc-tip`). The icons that mean what Gallery's do (plus, copy, duplicate, trash, rename, check, the chevrons, more, the handle, close, a link, search, the pointer) are `src/shared/ui-icons.mjs`'s, the one copy the page editor's menus and the workbench draw too; its own (the layouts, the columns' kinds, filter, sort) are drawn with round ends and joins (`ui/icons.js`), the board's, the chart's and the gallery's layouts on Gallery's grid of 20 with nothing pointed.

## Databases: views

A view's layout is drawn by a module of its own (`kit/database/views.js`), one at a time in the body of the drawing (`app.js`), the one the view shown says; it is made anew when another view shows (or the view's layout changes) and told of every change of the store in between. every layout is drawn: `table` (`table.js`), `chart` (`views/chart.js`) and the layouts of cards, `board`, `list`, `gallery` and `calendar` (`views/`); a layout no module draws would show a placeholder (`LAYOUT_VIEWS`, `placeholder`). A layout is a function `(store, db) => view`:

- `store` (`store.js`): `store.doc` (`{ props, rows, views }`: the columns, each `{ id, name, type, options?, numberFormat?, width?, align?, formula?, notes? }`; the records, each `{ id, values: { [propId]: value } }`, a value as `data/values.js` reads it: words, a number, an option's id or a list of them, `"2026-11-12"`, `true`, a url, null; the views as the file says them), `store.byId` (the columns by id), `store.view` (the view shown), `store.rowsOf(view)` → `{ rows, stale }` (the records the view shows, in order: its filters, the search and its sorts already applied, with those kept where they were although a change took them out of the filters, `stale`, drawn faded until the view changes; the same object while nothing it depends on changed), `store.ui` (`peek`: the record open; `search`), `store.editable` (whether a change is written: false in an export, a presentation, a block the format cannot hold, where a view draws nothing to change it with), `store.layer` (the element its cards and ghosts stand in), `store.layoutState(key, fallback)` and `store.setLayoutState(key, value)` (the layout's own reading state of the view: a calendar's month, a board's folded columns; never the file's). What changes the records, each one step of the document's undo: `setCell(rowId, propId, value, newOptions?, merge?)`, `writeCells([{ rowId, propId, text }], newRows?)` (words read as each column reads them: a paste, a fill), `addRow(prefill, { after?, before?, open?, focus? })` (the view's filters fill a new record), `moveRow(id, targetId, side)`, `deleteRows(ids)`, `duplicateRow(id)`, `updateView(viewId, patch, merge?)`, and `commit(fn, merge?)` for any change of the whole database at once (`fn(doc)` → the database as it is to be; several changes made one step). `snack(words)` says something for a moment; `flush()` ends a gesture (what typing or a drag held, written).
- `db` (`app.js`): `view()`, `rows()`, `updateView(patch, merge?)` (the view shown's), `openRow(id)` (a record's details: at the side or in the middle as the view's `open` says, ↑ ↓ going through `rowsOf(view).rows`), `closePeek()`, `openRowMenu(id, anchor)`, `addRow(prefill, options)`, `filterBy(prop)`, `cycleSort(propId)`, `sortFromHeader(sorts)`, `closeMenus()`, `openSettings(start, anchor)` (the view's settings card opened at a page of its, `"layout"`, under `anchor`: the chart's own button).
- `view`: `{ element, update(change), destroy(), focusRow?(id), headOf?(propId), width?() }`. `element` goes in the body once; `update(change)` draws as the store is now, `change` saying what changed (`{ views, props, rows: true | a Set of the ids of the records whose values changed, ui: the words of what of the reading changed, reread }`, none on the first draw), so that a record's change redraws what shows that record alone; `focusRow(id)` a record just made (its title edited), true where the view drew it; `headOf(id)` the element standing for a column (a new formula column's menu opens on it); `width()` (added after the first contract, optional) the px its content would take where nothing held it in (a table its columns' widths, a board its columns'): 宽多维表格 places the drawing by it, a view with none staying in the text; `destroy()` takes off what it put anywhere but `element` (listeners on the window, cards).

A view writes nothing itself: what the reader changes goes through the store, which writes both parts as one edit. It builds its elements with `ui/dom.js` (`h`, `keyed`: children kept by key, `kids`: what may go in an element, arrays and nulls taken care of), its cards with `ui/menu.js` (`pop`, `item`, `checkItem`, `heading`, `sep`), its icons with `ui/icons.js`, an option's colors with `ui/colors.js` (`paint`; colors to pick, `swatches`), a value as it shows with `ui/values.js` (`valueView`, `pill`, `visibleProps`), a value edited with `editors.js` (`openEditor`, `calendarPanel`), a drag in a row of things with `ui/reorder.js` (a view's tab, a board's column, a filter's chip, a sort's line, a column's line in the settings, a select's option in its options' editor: the one held lifts and follows the pointer along the row and the others make room, each giving way as the held one's leading edge passes its middle, half a gap before it, which `src/shared/drag-place.mjs` works out from the thing held and never the pointer, so that a wide column held by its edge does not cover most of the next before that one moves (the user's 2026-11-24: 我甚至和后面的看板卡片重叠了，都没有触发后面的看板卡片的移动), and a narrow tab passes a wide one only past that one's middle; what the row stands in scrolls as the pointer comes near its edges, `ui/edge-scroll.js`; let go, it lands and each slides from where it was let go, an element drawn anew by the drop told by its key, `keyOf`; Esc, the window losing the focus or the page hidden put everything back and write nothing, the click the release then makes swallowed; for a reader who asks for less motion nothing slides), a sideways scrollbar with `ui/rail.js`; its styles in `database/styles/database.css` (listed in `styles/index.css`), every rule under `.doc-database-app`; its words with `w()` and their English in `words-en.js`.

**The table view** (`table.js`) is a grid of rows (`.tbl`: a `.tr` per record, a `.td` per column shown, as wide as its columns), the room at its left (`.gut`, 32px, left of the text: `.tbl-frame`'s negative margin) holding each row's handle, its first column staying as the columns scroll sideways, its right edge fading over 10px to nothing at the very edge while columns lie past it, by the scroll's own progress as the board's fades are (`.tbl-wrap.more-end`, said again as it scrolls and as it or its columns change size), its own thin scrollbar under it (`ui/rail.js`: the system's hidden, the kit's one scrollbar drawn along its bottom, "Scrollbars the kit draws", in a strip that sticks to the window's bottom and takes no room, a spacer of its height where nothing scrolls; it starts after the column that stays, shows while the pointer is over the view's frame (`.tbl-frame:hover`, `.board-frame:hover`) or as it scrolls and takes a press only then, its thumb following the scroll on the compositor (the view's `.no-native-scrollbar` names its timeline, the frame hoists it to the rail beside it), placed again as the view is drawn and as it or its content resizes, its thumb dragged, a press on its track moving the thumb there first; pushed on past an end by the wheel or a drag of its thumb, the thumb gives way from that end, the table itself never bouncing, and springs back the moment the pushing stops, as a table frame's does: "Scrollbars giving way"). A cell is selected by a press, a drag, Shift or the arrows: the cell the keys are at framed (`.cursor`), the others of a range lit (`.in-sel`). A row's handle clicked picks its row (its cells lit across, no cell framed as the keys' place: `sel.row`) and opens its menu (the user's 2026-11-24: the cell picked before kept its frame and the row was not lit). A row's handle (in the room at its left under the pointer) dragged moves the row where no sort or group orders them (a ghost of its cells, the line where it lands, which goes by the ghost and never the pointer: past a row once the ghost's leading edge passes its middle, `src/shared/drag-place.mjs`); pressed, its menu. With the pointer on the header row a dot at the top of the nearest column edge, in the room at the left one at the left of the nearest row edge (`.edge-plus`): on it a +, a line along the edge saying where, a press putting a column (its kind chosen, the + and the line staying meanwhile) or a record there. A header pressed edits its name where it stands (the field is the name's own words made editable, `contenteditable="plaintext-only"`, not an `<input>`, which a browser draws by a path of its own that at some screen scales, 125%, 200%, 225%, set its words a device pixel lower; on a line as tall as the name's, so that its selection is lit as tall as its words; the first press in it soon after the click that opened it, which the system counts as that click's second, puts the caret where it lands rather than selecting the word again; one line, a line break pasted a space; left aligned, the field runs on to the header's end, and while a name is typed a press beside its field puts the caret at that end of it: no drag of the column), its menu under it; its kind's icon opens the menu at its kinds; its arrow sorts (ascending, descending, none); dragged, its column moves (a ghost, a line where it goes, by the ghost as a row's); its right edge sized by a drag. Past the last column the header's + (under the pointer in that room) lists the view's hidden columns first (shown again with a click), then a new column of each kind. A cell's options (a select's, a multi-select's) run on past where its padding begins to just inside its line and fade out over that room (`.cell-tags`, `ui/values.js`), clear of the cursor's ring: a column narrower than them shows them going on, not cut short; where the cells' words wrap, they wrap within the padding. Copy, cut and paste go as words between tabs and lines (a spreadsheet's, Excel's, another table's: a number as it shows where that reads as the same number, else every digit of it, `12.3456%` where `12%` shows, `exactText`), Ctrl/⌘+D fills down (the words a cell opens with, every digit of a number: `editText`), Delete clears; the first press in a cell's editor soon after the click that opened it, which the system counts as that click's second or third, puts a caret where it lands rather than selecting a word or the line (`ui/dom.js` `pressFirstAsClick`, the point read in a copy of the field's words laid over it; the user's 2026-11-24); a cell's Esc puts back the value it held as editing began, as it was (a date's too: the month closes, nothing typed is written), and words typed and taken back leave no step of undo; a url's value is a link (in a cell, a card, a record's details, the cell's open button) only where it is no address of code (`safeHref`, the format's `safeAddress`: `javascript:` and the like are its words alone); 导出 CSV writes the columns the view shows, each formula worked out of every column (a hidden one too), and a field a spreadsheet would read as a formula (`=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return first; not a number as it shows, `-5`) begins with `'`; grouped, a heading row per group (folded by a click) with its totals; the calculation row at the bottom. A view of more than 60 rows draws those in the window's sight and a screen's worth around them (`.tr-space` above and below standing for the others, each row's height as it was measured once drawn), so that a long view is a few dozen rows in the page: switching to it, typing in it and the editor's restyling of the page around it stay quick; the selection, the keys and an edit bring their row into sight first. A record's change redraws its row alone, and of it the cells whose drawing changed alone: a cell drawn as it stands already stays (`sameNodes`, by `isEqualNode`; so does the calculation row, of the same columns), as a cell put in anew has the browser lay the whole table out again at every character typed beside it; no rule of its stylesheet hangs on a `:has()` of the table (the header's + shows by a class its rows' pointer sets). Its parts are written as one string each and not read back when they come back as written; a change of some records' values alone (`store.js` `write`'s `rows`) writes their rows alone into the table's draft (`data/file.js` `writeRows`, `rowMarkup` of `src/shared/database.mjs`), the markup that makes looked at against the whole table written anew, which goes in instead where it differs, so that a long table is not read anew for a character. While a drag or a resize lasts, the whole page shows its cursor by an attribute of the body (`drag-cursor.js`, `data-doc-database-drag`), not by a `body:has()` of the drawing's classes, which had the browser restyle the whole page at every row put into or taken out of any table on it.

**The chart view** (`views/chart.js`, its numbers `data/chart.js`) draws the view's `chart` (`{ kind: column | bar | line | donut, x, agg: count | sum | avg, y, color? }`, the file's, `src/shared/database.mjs`) over the records the view shows, its filters applied (`rowsOf`; a chart has no search and no sort of its own): the records in categories by `x` (a select's options in their order, each though no record has it; a multi's, a record in each of its; the others by their words, the records with none last), each counted or the sum or the average of `y`'s numbers (a number or formula column). It is plain SVG, no d3: columns and bars rounded at their far end, a line with its points, a donut and its legend (a value, a share); its head says what it shows, its total and its records (跟随当前筛选), with 编辑图表 opening the view's settings at its chart alone (no layouts to choose there: the view settings' 布局 has them; and no title over its first section's, a card never opening with two names; the user's 2026-11-24). Under the pointer a category is lit (the others faded) and its numbers float by the pointer (`.chart-tip`); a donut says the one under the pointer in its middle. With no column of categories (or no number to measure) it says what to do, with a button to set it up. Its colors are the view's `color` (the settings' 颜色 ›, one step of undo, written in the file so that an export draws them too): none (自动, written as nothing; `"auto"` read as none) each category its option's color, gray for an option of none, the colors in turn for categories of no option (`ui/colors.js` `SEQUENCE`: blue, green, purple, pink, yellow, red, gray), never brown or orange (`CHOCOLATE`): an option of either drawn in the first of those no other category's option has, purple when all are (the user's 2026-11-24: their inks read as chocolate in a chart, the dark theme's orange most of all); one of the nine every category a shade of it (`data/chart.js` `shadeSteps`, `shadeOf`), the first the deepest, evenly apart (two half the way, three three quarters, four or more the whole way): in light from the color's ink a little deepened to the ink paled toward white, in dark from the ink brightened to the ink dimmed toward the page, the palest still clear of it; no label stands on a shade (a column's value is over it, a bar's at its end, a donut's in its legend). A line is one series: in the color's ink, else the accent.

**The layouts of cards** (`views/`: `board.js`, `list.js`, `gallery.js`, `calendar.js`, what they share in `cards.js`, what they work out before they draw in `model.js`) show a record as a card, a line or an item: its title (faded where its record is done: an option of its counts as done) and the values of the columns the view shows (its `hidden`; a box with its column's name; short ones, an option, options, a date, side by side, the rest a line each), a value as `ui/values.js` draws it; on a board's card its options in their own color family (an option's mark, its words of the option's ink, the kit's radius). A card, a line or an item pressed opens its record (`db.openRow`: at the side, in the middle for a wall of cards and a calendar, as `layouts.js` `defaultOpen` and the view's `open` say); where the page takes edits a right click gives its menu (`db.openRowMenu`), a card's ··· too (a flat button: no fill at rest, the gray under the pointer, no shadow, there while the card is under the pointer), and a round + (shown as the pointer comes near) past a wall's or a list's last makes a record there, opened. A drag marks the drawing `is-dragging` (its grabbing hand, no words selected); it writes as it ends, one change of the store, so nothing is held meanwhile; a press that does not move is a click. Where nothing takes edits (an export, a presentation) none of their controls is drawn and nothing moves; a card still opens its record and a calendar still goes from month to month. **A wall of cards** (`gallery`) is a grid of cards as wide as there is room for, its columns at least 190, 250 or 330px by the view's `cardSize` (s, m, l). **A list** is a line per record, its title at the left, its values at the right.

**A board** (`board.js`) has a column per option of the view's select (`boardBy`; none: a word saying so and a button opening the view's settings at its layout), in the options' order (`model.js` `boardColumns`), the records of no value in a column of their own while there are any. A column wears its option's color (`--k` its ink, `--tint` its fill: its ground the fill at 70%, a card on it a touch of its color, in the dark its fill the column's tint at 42% over the floating fill, its title, its date and its bars of it), its width the view's card size (224, 264, 324px), its head its option's pill and its count, and where the page takes edits its ··· and + (shown under the pointer), and past the last column a round + of a new column (a new option 新分组, its name edited at once). A card dragged (5px; `views/card-drag.js`, the user's 2026-11-24: 看板卡片里面的小卡片的拖拽建议也是改成这种有拖拽移动动效过渡的那种) lifts, its copy following the pointer in the colors of the column it is over (`.drag-ghost.board-lift`, in the layer), and the cards make room as a row's things do: the column it goes into is the one its middle is over (the nearest where it is over none), and there it takes the place nearest to where it stands (`src/shared/drag-place.mjs` `placeAmong`, among that column's cards as they stand without it: a card gives way as its leading edge passes that card's middle), the cards there sliding apart to open the room (`.card-slot.card-gap`, a soft fill of the column's color where its card is unseen) and those of the column it left closing up behind it, each sliding from where it is drawn (one under way included); where a sort orders the view the room opens where the sort will put it in another column (`model.js` `sortedPlace`, the records' own order among equals) and stays where it was in its own (替用户定的: the room tells where it lands, which the reader cannot choose then); the board and the page scroll as the pointer comes near their edges (`ui/edge-scroll.js`). Let go, it changes its cell and, with no sort, its place in the records in one change (`model.js` `dropCard`, one step of undo: before the card after its room, else after its column's last record; where a sort orders the view its cell alone), the board is drawn again and the copy flies from where it was let go into its card's place (or fades, a card that the view then no longer shows), every other card sliding from where it was drawn; let go where it was, nothing is written and it flies back. Esc, the window losing the focus or the page hidden put the room back where it was and the copy into it, nothing written, and the release after it opens no record; the board is not drawn while a card is held, a change that came meanwhile drawn as it ends; for a reader who asks for less motion nothing slides or flies; a column's head dragged sideways moves its option in the options' order (`ui/reorder.js`, as a view's tab: one step); its pill pressed edits its name where it stands (a character a write, one step of undo; empty or another's name it goes back as the typing ends); its ··· gives 重命名, 视为已完成, 删除这一列 (its cells emptied, said) and its color; its + a record at its top, opened. The board scrolls sideways under its own thin scrollbar (`ui/rail.js`, as the table's), sideways alone (never down: a wheel turned up or down over it scrolls the page, whatever a fraction of a pixel of its cards past its height at a screen's scale), within the database's own edges (nothing of it shows past them, where the page's selection of the block ends), an edge columns lie past fading over 10px to nothing at the very edge (the user's 2026-11-24: 32px, then 16px, was too much, and a quarter left at the edge read as a dark line there): a mask whose stops are registered lengths, driven by the scroll's own progress where the browser runs animations so (`animation-timeline: scroll(self inline)`: the start's grows over the first 10px scrolled, the end's goes over the last 10px), in the frame the board moves in, and elsewhere by `.more-start`, `.more-end`, said again as it scrolls (a fast swipe left those a step behind).

**A calendar** (`calendar.js`) lays the records out by the view's date (`dateBy`; none: a word saying so and a button opening the view's settings at its layout) in a month of whole weeks from Monday (`model.js` `monthDays`, `recordsByDay`), weekends and the days of the months around it on a faint ground, today's number on the accent; its head the month (`monthTitle`), 未排期 n, back, 今天 and forward. The month shown is the reader's (today's until another is chosen). An item is a small card of the record's title and the values the view shows besides its date, a done record's struck through. 未排期 lists, in a card under its button that takes no room beside the month, the records whose date is none or words that are no date. Where the page takes edits an item dragged to another day writes that day into its date (one step of undo), one dragged out of 未排期 too, its card fading away while the item is held (for a day it covered) and showing again as it is let go; a day's + (under the pointer) or a double click on the day makes a record of that day, opened.

## State bindings

`runtime/spacing.js` (the rule is `src/shared/inline-spacing.mjs`'s, the page editor's too: the word beside a piece of code is looked for out of any bold, italic or link it stands in, up to the edge of its line) puts an ordinary space between inline code or a formula (`code`, `.doc-math-inline`, a formula KaTeX rendered from bare `\(…\)`) and a letter, digit or Chinese character right beside it, where the document's text has none: as the page renders, and for what is added later (formulas math.js renders, a document's script). None beside a space, punctuation or the line's edge, one between two of them; never inside code blocks or text the page editor edits (`[contenteditable]`, which the editor spaces the same way when it prepares it). A closing punctuation mark right after an inline formula (，。、；：！？）」』》 and the like) never starts a line: KaTeX draws a formula as boxes a line may break after, its last one too, so `tieClosing` holds the formula (or the bold, link or span it ends) and the marks right after it in a `span.doc-math-tie` the line does not break in between its children (`styles/math.css`: `white-space: nowrap` on the span, the run it ends still wrapping between its words, so a long bold run ending in a formula never makes a line wider than the page), a runtime mark an export of the view or a card's copy takes off (`untie`, the `math-tie` component's `dehydrate` in `math.js`: the span's children back in its place, the marks' text joined to the words after them). The marks are Chinese and Latin closing ones and the end of a range the formula begins (`\(\rho\approx3\)–5`: the dash and the number). Chrome breaks after an inline-block (KaTeX's last box) by the white-space of the box's parent, so a held formula's `.katex-html` is `nowrap` too and wraps only at `span.doc-math-break`s (`white-space: normal`, a zero-width space each) that `breakBetweenBoxes` puts between each two of KaTeX's boxes, where KaTeX lets a line break, never after the last (the breaks go with the span). A number range (3–13, 1–3B, 22.8%–23.7%, 100M–400M, 3～5) is one word: `holdRanges` holds it in a `span.doc-range` (`white-space: nowrap`, `styles/base.css`), as the page renders and for what is added later, never in code, a formula's drawing, text holding a formula not drawn yet (its delimiters) or text the page editor edits; an export of the view lets it go (`untie`, the runtime's `range` component). A long run (`src/shared/long-runs.mjs`: more than 20 characters with no place to break inside it, a path, a URL, a long identifier) is held the same way in a `span.doc-long-run` that breaks anywhere (`holdRuns`, `word-break: break-all`, `styles/base.css`), so it breaks where its line ends while an ordinary word stays whole: as the page renders and for what is added later, in the document's text alone (never code, kbd, a formula, a drawing, a range, an instance's own region or text the page editor edits, which marks a block holding one itself); a piece of inline code holding one is held whole, the span around it (none goes into code), but in a table's cell, whose code keeps its runs; a run is looked for within one text node (one parted by a bold letter is two). An export of the view lets it go (`untie`, the runtime's `long-run` component). The file stays as written; Agents write the space themselves as a rule, so this mends the odd one left out.
