`runtime/spacing.js` also gives a list item with no text of its own over the blocks it holds (`<li><p>…</p></li>`, an item over a task board, a picture or a list) a line of its own, its marker on it and the blocks under it, as the page editor shows it (`keepLine` in its `core.mjs`: an item keeps its title line however empty): a `br.doc-item-line` ahead of the first block, as the page renders and in what is added later, so that the preview, an export, 演示视图 and print show such an item as the editor does, its marker never beside the first line of what it holds; the file stays `<li>` and its blocks. Which items is one rule, the editor's too (`keepsLine` of `src/shared/components/list.mjs`, which both call through `giveLine`): an item of a list whose markers the kit draws, a bulleted or numbered list with no class of its own or a to-do list standing straight in a flow (`FLOW_BLOCKS` of the descriptions, which the build gives the runtime), in a flowchart's step or held by an item of such a list; holding blocks (`HELD_TAGS`) and nothing but blocks and white space after the first, with nothing before them but white space and elements with no text and nothing that shows (an empty bold or link; a line break there is the item's line already). An item of a list with a class of its own, or of one in a whole block of the document's, a card or a table's cell, gets none: its look is the document's (a widget's timeline laid out by a grid of its children keeps its first child in its first cell), and the browser's marker, where it shows one, stands beside the first line as on any page. None goes into text the page editor edits (it gives the line itself), a flowchart's step (a node holding blocks, no item) or an instance; an export of the view takes it off (the list's description's `runtime.generated`), the copy's kit putting it back, and so does an export of the view made from the editing page, whose line breaks are of that class too. `tests/unit/runtime.mjs` holds it.

`runtime/bindings.js` lets a document declare reading state instead of scripting it ([components/state.md](components/state.md)). An element bound to a key (`data-doc-bind="key"`) writes it and shows it: an input, textarea or select its value, a button with a `value` that value when clicked (`aria-pressed` showing the one chosen), a details whether it is open, a table its sort (`tables/index.js`, `bindSort`). An element with `data-doc-show="key:value"` is hidden while the key holds another value. Equality only: nothing in an attribute is evaluated (an export's CSP runs no code from attributes). The keys live in one `DocState` ("bound") created the first time one is read, its defaults what the markup holds then (a control's value, the button marked `aria-pressed="true"`, a details' `open`), the keys of elements with `data-doc-transient` transient; a key first met later is kept for the visit only. The binds and shows are components of the runtime ("bind", "show"), so a bound block added later (an undo) starts and one removed lets go of its subscription. An export of the view takes `hidden` off what `data-doc-show` hid; the copy's state shows it again.

## State and browser export

`DocState` writes best-effort to `localStorage` under `gallery:<data-doc-id>:<id>` (plus the export scope; one kept before 2026-10-26 under `html-report-studio:<data-doc-id>:<id>` is carried to it the first time the state is made when the new one is empty, and again when the old one changed since, which only a page of the old version does, the old left as it is and what it held kept as a digest beside the new, `…:renamed`), checked against the state version. The id names the state within its report: `view` and `<slug>:view` are the same state, and a copy of a document under another slug (the workspace's duplicate) keeps its own; `transientKeys` are excluded from storage and export snapshots. `DocState.create` refuses an id and version made already, which finds one used twice by mistake; `DocState.getOrCreate` gives the state of that id back when its version and the shape of its defaults (the keys of each object and the kind of value at each, an array one kind however long) are the same, makes one when there is none, and throws otherwise, an old object handed back being what would hide that what reads it changed. The normal export and the browser's “导出当前视图副本” both embed assets. Browser export takes what the reader typed into the copy's fields first, while they are the page's one for one, then takes off what the kit left on the page (`DocKit.dehydrate`, see "Runtime") and stale snapshots, closes open modal overlays, clears `data-doc-transient-view` nodes, and assigns each new file an independent state scope. A scoped browser snapshot takes precedence on subsequent opens; the embedded snapshot seeds another browser. User input containing `<` is encoded safely. The export keeps `connect-src 'none'`; the save destination is chosen by the browser, and a stable picker id helps it remember the last directory. `file://` storage behavior depends on the browser, so it is not a cross-browser persistence format; use the modified-HTML export or `serialize()`/`hydrate()` to carry state.

## The catalog of custom blocks

A custom block (自定义块; a component package before 2026-11-07) is a block of the workspace's own, used again in any document and on a custom page. Its files keep the word component (`components/`, `component.json`, `data-doc-component`), which are the file format, and the code's names keep package (`packages`, `packageTags`, `data-doc-package`, the scenarios' `package…`), which these documents leave as they are named. `src/shared/component-catalog.mjs` is the one reader of `<home>/components/<scope>/<name>/`, for the server, the commands and the conversion worker alike. It stays plain data: no code of a custom block runs on the server. A folder two down in `components/` holding `component.json` is a custom block (`packageFolders`, `src/shared/workspace.mjs`), whatever it says; any other folder there is passed over, one being written or what a document called `components` keeps.

| Field of `component.json` | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | `<scope>/<name>`, its folder's path, in lowercase letters, digits and dashes, neither a name Windows keeps for a device |
| `apiVersion` | the whole number of the interface it is written for; this Gallery runs `API_VERSION` |
| `version` | which version of it this is, shown and never compared |
| `name`, `description` | `{ zh, en }`, what the menu shows |
| `words` | optional: what the menu finds it by |
| `parts` | by name, `{ at, edit, optional?, min?, max? }` in the grammar of `src/shared/components/`, `edit` being `text`, `data`, `flow` or `none`, `min` no more than `max`; one edited as `flow` is a slot, `{ "edit": "flow", "optional"? }` and no `at`, `min` or `max`, its name `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*`, marked `data-doc-slot="<name>"` in the markup, one to an instance, the catalog giving it `at: div[data-doc-slot="<name>"], section[data-doc-slot="<name>"]` (`definition` stays the manifest's text's); one edited as `text` names no element that holds no text to type in (`NO_TEXT`: a script, a field, a list, a table), and the scan takes none of those as one, whatever a selector of no tag finds |
| `runtime` | optional: `{ generated, attrs }`, what it leaves on a page, which a copy of the view takes off; the catalog gives each selector the custom block's instances as its root, as `DocKit.dehydrate` looks through the whole page, none of `doc-*`, `data-doc-*`, `data-ge-*` or `data-block-id` is its to take off, in any case, and no selector of `generated` is the instance or all it holds (`:scope`, `*`, `:scope > *`) |
| `assets` | optional: what it needs besides its own script and style, each the id of a file of the kit's (`KIT_ASSETS`, held to `src/render/assets.mjs` by `tests/unit/catalog.mjs`) or the path of a file in its folder, plain names with an extension |
| `markdown` | optional: `parts` (the default) or `text`, how it exports to Markdown |
| `schema` | optional: the whole number of the newest shape of content its component reads, 1 by default: an instance saying a higher one (`data-doc-schema`) is read-only |
| `places` | optional: `"document"`, `"page"` or both, each once, `["document"]` when left out (`DOCUMENT_ONLY`, what every custom block before the field was): where the block may stand, a document's flow or a custom page's board. One whose places hold `page` has no part edited as `flow` (`pageParts`: a slot holds a document's blocks, which no custom page renders or edits) |
| `listed` | optional: `true` or `false`, `true` when left out (the catalog's `listed`): `false` keeps the block out of every menu and library until the user turns it on in 设置 › 组件列表 ("Settings", below); its instances show and edit as ever. Gallery's `gallery/card` and `gallery/chart-card` say it (2026-11-20) |
| `card` | optional: its size on a board, in cells of a row of 12 (`GRID`: 12 wide, 24 high at most). `sizes`, a list of `[w, h]` each once, the first the default, and then no `w` or `h` (a `min` or a `max` beside them frees the card, `any`: the sizes are the common ones its 大小 menu lists, any whole size between `min` and `max` is its too, resizing by the corner and a `place` included, and each of the sizes stands inside them: `gallery/static` is one); or `w` and `h`, with `min` and `max` as `{ w, h }` (either left out: 1, and 12 or 24), the default between them; `resize` and `bare` true or false, `fit` `"fixed"` or `"content"`, `consent` `{ zh, en }`, the line the kit's consent banner on its card says (允许联网之后显示天气, "A block online"), told to the kit in the block's description (`descriptionOf`) and in no other place. The catalog gives every block one, the defaults filled in (`NO_CARD`: 4 wide, `h` null for as high as its content, `min` 1×1, `max` 12×24, no resize, `fixed`, framed), `sizes` null where it has none, `any` true only where a `min` or a `max` is written beside them, and `w` and `h` then its first |
| `settings` | optional: a card's form, `[{ key, label: { zh, en }, type, options?, default?, required? }]`, `key` `[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9]*` once each, `type` one of `text`, `number`, `select` (its `options` a list of words, each once; no other type has options), `date` (`YYYY-MM-DD`), `switch`, `document`, `default` of its type, `required` true where the card shows nothing until the setting holds a value (weather's city, countdown's date: `gallery page check` notes a card whose setting is left empty, `emptyRequired` in `src/cli/page-check.mjs`); a block with any has the part `settings` (`SETTINGS_PART`), edited as `data`, its `at` ending on a `script` (a JSON part) |
| `needs` | optional: what it uses, each once: `workspace`, `tables` (it is a view of a table of the document: `context.table()`; made from "/" it reads the nearest table above it, and its 设置 choose the table), `net:<host>`, the host lowercase with no port and no path, `*.` first for its subdomains (`HOST`); what the kit is told of a block holds its needs where it has any (`descriptionOf`) |

A manifest holds nothing else: `category`, a unit, a box, a host or a rank are the engines' and no field of it, and a field it does not know is a problem, as a part's is. Its folder also holds, by these names, `kit.js`, `style.css`, `template.html`, `example.html`, the writing notes (`<folder>/writing.md`) and the menus' two pictures of it (`preview-light.webp`, `preview-dark.webp`: `PICTURES`, which `gallery block check` writes, "Making one"; Gallery's own also ship an English pair, `preview-light.en.webp` and `preview-dark.en.webp`, `LANGUAGE_PICTURES`; a `preview.svg`, the drawing they showed until 2026-11-17, is read by nothing now), each optional here: a document's instances run without any of them, and a custom block with no template has nothing for a menu to put in. So has one whose template no new block can be (`templateProblem`, which says why, for `gallery block check`): it is one instance of the custom block closed by its own end tag, holding nothing a clipboard could not bring (`refusedInCopy`: a script, which would run from the next load on alone, a handler, a style, the editor's marks) and the parts its manifest asks for, or the block a reader makes keeps the document from passing `gallery check`. A new instance is written from what that template parses to (`templateOf`, `instanceMarkup`), not from its bytes, what the parser put right being right in the file. Every page holds a manifest's words and a template (below), so each is 256 KB at most, and neither keeps a byte-order mark.

`readCatalog()` answers `{ packages, problems, settings, stamp }`. `settings` is what gallery.json says of the components ("Settings", below). `packages`, by id, are those that load, each a description as a kit component's is (`id`, `match`, `parts`, `runtime`) with what else the manifest says (`apiVersion`, `version`, `name`, `description`, `words`, `assets` of the kit, `markdown`, and `places`, `card`, `settings` and `needs`, each with its default filled in) and `files` (what of its folder a page may ask for), `template`, `folder`, `definition` and `stamp`. `problems`, by id in lowercase, as an instance names it, are those that do not load: `{ id, problem, disabled, version, stamp }`, `problem` being `said()`'s (`problemIn(language, problem)` says it in `zh` or `en`), so that a folder `Local/kanban`, which on Windows is where `local/kanban` is, says why. A problem stays with its custom block: reading one that is broken touches no other custom block and no document, and its instances show as those of a custom block that is not there. A manifest for an `apiVersion` this Gallery does not run is one of them, `disabled`, read no further than its `version`, whatever else it holds. The reason goes to whoever reads the custom block: `gallery check` and `gallery export` print it in their note, a preview's note in the instance says it in the page's language (`problem` of what the kit is told, below), and the server's log says it as the server starts and as one comes, there being no instance of it yet to say so.

What a process uses is `currentCatalog()`: read once, as it is first asked for, and kept. A command's life is short and a job of the conversion worker reads it anew (`forgetCatalog()`, `src/transfer/worker.mjs`: the worker outlives a job and hears no watcher). The server's is read as it starts and taken anew by `createCatalogWatch` (`src/server/catalog-watch.mjs`), which `routes.mjs` calls for what `changeOf` marks `packages`, anything under `components/` but a dot name (a write's temp file), and for the watcher's `reset`. It looks at the disk, never at the events: a stamp that has not changed ends a look whatever was heard, so a file touched tells no page, and an event only brings the next look nearer, never puts it off, as the watcher of a removed folder can fire without end on Windows (`watch.mjs`). A change is taken once the disk has not changed for 1.5s (`QUIET` of `editor/settle.mjs`, as for a document), so a folder written file by file is one change when the files come close together, and each file's own when they do not. A custom block that loaded and now reads as broken, a manifest half written or taken away with its folder standing (a delete and a write, a git operation), is held: the catalog in use keeps it as it was until the disk has not changed for 30s or it has been so for two minutes (`HOLD`, `LIMIT`, the same times), when it is its author's, so an Agent between two edits flashes no instance off, and finished it is taken as it now says, or, put back, nothing happened. A custom block whose folder is gone goes once the disk is quiet, a folder taken away and written again within that moment being the same custom block. On Linux the watcher holds one watch a folder, and all of `components/` is among them (`foldersOf`).

The catalog taken, every page is told `components <stamp> <ids>` on its event stream, the ids being the custom blocks that changed with it, with commas, and each hears `components <stamp>` as it connects, for a page served a moment before a change, or by a server stopped since, where any custom block may have changed. A page goes by what changed for it, as being shown again costs its reader the undo steps, the selection and whatever is open: it waits to be read, which holds its instances, and a page of that stamp or holding no instance of those custom blocks goes on as it is. All it takes of the new catalog is what its menus list, from its own address, by `doc-catalog-changed` ([editor.md](editor.md) "Instances of custom blocks"): what it runs and what it told the kit stay as they were served until its next load or the first instance that brings a custom block, which nothing reads while it holds no instance of those custom blocks. The editor there keeps which custom blocks it is behind on (`components/installed/served.mjs`). The first instance of one that comes onto the page, by a menu, an undo, a paste or anything else, brings it with the tags of that list, where the page runs nothing of it or runs it as it now is; where it runs an older version, or the list has no such custom block, it asks for what a page holding one has had: without it, an instance back from an undo would run a custom block now off, or an old version of one, against the file. The 写作偏好 dialog's page (`?solo`), which no swap serves, hears none of it. Otherwise a page the editor runs on is told by `doc-components-changed` and asks to be shown again as soon as nothing waits and the reader pauses (`autosave.reloadSoon()`), which in a workbench is the swap of a page loaded out of sight ([workbench.md](workbench.md), "Shown again without a reload"), and a page that does not edit reloads as for any other change. The next page is made anew, the catalog's stamp being in the page's key.

What gallery.json says goes the same way, read with the rest of the catalog ("Settings", below).

There are two stamps, kept apart. `stamp` is over what reaches a page of the custom block (`component.json`, `kit.js`, `style.css`, its two pictures, `template.html` and the files `assets` names; `example.html` and the writing notes reach none); `definition` is over the manifest alone, what a page's commit is to be held to. The catalog's `stamp` is over its custom blocks' and over those that do not load, each of which holds why too, as what a page shows of one can move with a file no stamp reads, and over what is hidden and shown and why gallery.json does not read, none of which changes a custom block. A file is read for a stamp once, its hash kept by its size and time unless it was written this very moment, so that a look takes a look at each file and goes through none, however large.

Every preview tells the kit of the whole catalog, an instance of it on the page or not, since a paste or the "/" menu can bring one: that is the one way a part's description reaches a page. Those it has no instance of are told right after its runtime, by a script of the preview's alone (`withDescriptions`, `report-html.mjs`: `DocKit.describe` for each of `descriptionsOf(catalog)`, marked `data-doc-dev`); those it has one of go with their files ("A custom block on a page"). A custom block that does not load is told as `{ id, problem: { zh, en } }`, with no `match`, which the kit's fallback reads as no custom block and its note gives as why. No export carries that.

Two things more reach a page of the catalog, each from its own place. The page the editor runs on holds what its menus show in its map, `components` (`menuOf`, [editor.md](editor.md) "Instances of custom blocks"): no route and no dynamic import, and no part of a custom block, whose parts reach a page the one way above. And `/__gallery/components/<scope>/<name>/<file>` (`src/server/component-route.mjs`) hands out what a custom block's `files` list: `kit.js` in its function (`packageScript`, "A custom block on a page"), and `style.css`, its two pictures and the files its `assets` name as they stand in the folder, of a custom block that loads. A name is found in that list as it is written, so that no spelling of a path reaches anything else (dots, an encoded slash or backslash, another case, a name with a colon, or with a dot or a space at its end), and a link or a junction out of the folder is no file of it (`packagePath`). All of `/__gallery/components/` is this route's: what it does not find is 404 and goes to no one else. A script and a module (`.js`, `.mjs`), a style and WebAssembly go by the types a browser asks of them, and the rest by `mediaTypeOf`. `packageAddress` names a file by its custom block's `stamp`, `?v=<stamp>`, and it is answered `max-age=31536000, immutable` as a kit file is, when that is the stamp of the catalog in use and of what the disk holds as it is read (`packageStampNow`: the catalog in use comes a moment after a write, and holds a custom block half written); by its path alone or under another stamp it is `no-store`, the disk not looked at for it.

## Settings

`components` in a workspace's gallery.json, `{ hidden: [...], disabled: [...], shown: [...], unlisted: [...], listed: [...] }`, says what its owner switched (设置 › 组件列表, [workbench.md](workbench.md) "Components"), and goes with the workspace to any machine. `ours()` knows the `gallery` field alone, so the file's version stays, and a Gallery that knows nothing of the field passes it by. Each list holds the ids of custom blocks, and `hidden` the names of built-in components too; `shown`, `unlisted` and `listed` are written only where they hold an id or were there already, so that a file of 0.1 stays as it was. `unlisted` (2026-11-09) is the master switch at the right of a custom block's line in 设置 › 组件列表, on by default (a block no entry names is listed): a block it names is offered by no menu and no library, which is every way a reader makes one (a document's "/", 在下方新建 and the handle's grid, a custom page's 添加自定义块), while what is written of it shows and runs as ever, and the Agent's commands (`gallery block`, `gallery page add`) are none of its business; only a custom block can be unlisted. A manifest's `listed: false` (2026-11-20; Gallery's `gallery/card` and `gallery/chart-card`, which the kit's highlight panel and a chart of the document's or a 多维表格's chart view do better: the user's word, "默认块包里就不需要再出现什么'卡片'了") turns the default of that switch: the block is off until the user turns it on, which writes its id in `listed`, and an entry of `unlisted` says nothing of it then; turning it off takes it out of `listed`. One rule says it everywhere, `unlistedBy(id, listed, settings)` (`src/shared/components/installed.mjs`): the catalog's rows (`packageStates`' `unlisted`, beside `listedByDefault`, the manifest's), the menus, the library and the page's own list (`menuOf`'s `listed`, `followSettings`). The dialog and `gallery block menu on` send `unlisted` as for any block, and the server's write (`src/server/component-settings.mjs`) makes it `listed` for such a block, taking it out of `unlisted` too as it turns on, so that a switch of before (a 0.3.0 user who turned the card off) stays as the user left it and turning it on undoes both. The block's files are installed and followed as ever, and a document that holds one shows, edits and exports it as ever: only what offers it changed. `net`, `{ "<id>": ["<host>", …] }`, is the reader's consent to the net (a card's 允许访问 or a row's 允许联网, "A block online" below): of each custom block the hosts of its needs it may reach, read by `consentsIn` (an id, then the hosts that can be a needs' host, once each; an id holding none says nothing), written whole for its id by `writeComponentSettings`' `net` (a list: all its needs' hosts, or none, which takes its id away, and `net` with its last id), in the catalog's stamp, said by `packageStates` as each row's `allowed` (the hosts of its needs now among them: one its needs no longer name is none of it) and told to the pages as the settings line's fourth list, `"<id>@<host>"` each (`settingsLine`), which the kit starts instances again by; the line's fifth list is `unlisted`, its sixth the packs off and its seventh `listed`.

Whether the document's insert entries ("/", 在下方新建, the handle's grid) offer a block is one rule, `inDocumentMenus(places, settings, id, listed)` (`src/shared/components/installed.mjs`), which the server and the page both run: one whose `places` hold `document` is offered unless `hidden` names it, one whose do not (a custom page's alone) only where `shown` names it, and one disabled or unlisted (`unlistedBy`, the manifest's `listed` with it) never, nor one of a pack switched off (`packsOff`: "Block packs"; the master switch off takes it out of every menu, which all read the same state). `settingState(id, settings, places, listed)` makes of it the state a menu reads, `normal` or `hidden`. A custom page's add lists blocks by their places, `disabled` and `unlisted` (the library the page service answers, `library()` in `src/editor/page-service.mjs`, Gallery's own the workspace has not yet included), whatever 在文档菜单里显示 says.

| State | Menus, and what an Agent is offered | Instances on a page |
| --- | --- | --- |
| normal | listed | run |
| hidden (out of the document's menus: `inDocumentMenus` false, a block `unlisted` too) | left out of a document's menus; still listed for an Agent, by its places (`gallery block list`) | run and export as ever |
| disabled | left out | no script and no style, in a preview or any export: the kit's fallback, which needs neither |
| missing | left out | the same fallback, and `gallery check` gives a note |

A custom block for an `apiVersion` this Gallery does not run is disabled, and says so. One that does not load of itself is none of the four: its state is `broken`, its instances as a missing custom block's, and its reason goes where the catalog's go. A built-in component can only be hidden, which is [editor.md](editor.md), "Hiding a block".

`componentSettings(root)` is the one reader, for a file a hand may have edited: of each list what can be an id, once each; an id that names nothing stays, for a custom block may come back, and does nothing; a name in `disabled` that is no custom block's id is passed over; a byte-order mark is no part of the file (`markerOf`, `src/shared/workspace.mjs`); and a file that is not there, does not read, or is another program's of that name says nothing. What goes on using a workspace goes by the catalog, for which the last two are a slip and no setting: `readCatalog(root, held)` keeps the settings in use for as long as the file stays so, `problem` saying why (`settingsProblem`, in both languages), so that a stray comma turns no custom block back on, and 设置 › 组件列表 says it and locks its switches. A process with no settings to keep (a command, a job of the conversion worker) has nothing to go by, and the `gallery` command stops there with what is wrong: `does not read as JSON` for its own file with a slip in it, another program's for another's. A file taken away is none of that: it holds nothing, and once the disk is quiet nothing is off. `writeComponentSettings` is the one writer, the server's: atomic (`writeAtomic`), against the text it read, all else the file holds kept, one that does not read, is another program's, changed under the write or cannot be written (read-only, held by another program) refused in the reader's words and left as it is.

Both ways of turning something off are in `readCatalog`, so that nothing else has a case for them. A custom block hidden stays in `packages` and all its stamps stay what they were: no page holding an instance is shown again for it, and only the catalog's `stamp` moves, so that a page served from now on is another. What offers a custom block reads `state`, which `menuOf` gives each entry of a page's map (`normal` or `hidden`, with its `places` and `needs`) and `packageStates` each custom block of a list (`disabled` and `broken` too, with `places`, `needs`, `hidden`, `shown`, `disabled`, `unlisted` and `menu`: whether its switch 在文档菜单里显示 is on, `inDocumentMenus` with nothing disabled or unlisted, as disabling and the master switch are switches of their own), and no setting. A custom block disabled goes into `problems` as one that does not load, what its manifest says kept for who lists it (`off`), with a stamp of its own: it gets no tag and no address, the kit is told why and its note says so, `gallery check` and `gallery export` print that note as they do any other, and a page that holds an instance goes by the rule of "The catalog of custom blocks", shown again by the workbench with nothing to see. `packagesOf` then finds none, so all three exports hold nothing of it.

A change is a change of the catalog. A hand's is heard as a custom block's is (`changeOf` marks gallery.json) and taken once the disk has stood still; the server's is taken at once, `createCatalogWatch`'s `taken()`, what it wrote being no file half written, so that `POST components` answers with what is in use. A custom block disabled is never held, whatever its files say. `createCatalogWatch` takes the clock (`now`) and the timers it sets (`timers`: `set(run, ms)` and `clear(timer)`, the global `setTimeout` and `clearTimeout` by default) as arguments, which a test fires by hand, so that no load of the machine changes when a look comes. Each page is told `settings <hidden> <disabled> <shown> <consents> <unlisted> <packs off>` on its event stream (`settingsLine`), by commas, beside `components`, and as it connects: it costs the page nothing, for the editor only takes it for what its menus offer (`doc-settings-changed`, `page/offered.mjs`), a custom block's state in its map among that. A custom block turned on, as one added, reaches a page holding no instance of it as any change of the catalog does: its menus offer it from the list the page reads, with no load, and the first instance brings it. 设置 › 组件列表 hears the same stream while it is open, and asks again. A word typed in an instance and not saved as its custom block goes off is refused, as the part is no text block now (`package`, [editor.md](editor.md) "Conflicts"): it stays held on the page until the reader takes the file's, nothing lost and the file as it was.

## Block packs

A block pack (自定义块包, since 2026-11-09; `src/shared/block-packs.mjs`) is a scope of the workspace's custom blocks, `components/<id>/`, with its `pack.json` (`src/shared/pack-manifest.mjs` `readPackManifest`: `id` the scope, `name` and `description` words or `{ zh, en }`, `version` x.y.z, `author`, `homepage` an http(s) address, `icon` an emoji; a field it does not know keeps it from reading, `local` and a device's name are no id) and a folder of each block, its id `<id>/<name>` as ever, so that nothing written of a block changes with the packs. `local/` (`OWN_SCOPE`, where `gallery block new <name>` puts a block when it names no scope) is the default pack (默认块包, `DEFAULT_PACK`: its name and words, `{ zh, en }`, Gallery's, since no `pack.json` may name `local`), where an Agent's block goes when it names no pack, so that a single block needs no pack of its own (the user's word of 2026-11-09); any other scope with no `pack.json` is a pack of its own, named by its scope. A block counts in its scope's pack, but for Gallery's own blocks for documents alone, `gallery/card`, `chart-card` (both off in the menus until the user turns them on, their manifests' `listed: false`: "Settings"), and `metric` (the scope `gallery`, `places` holding no `page`), which count in the default pack (`packOf(id, places)`, `src/shared/components/installed.mjs`, the one rule the server, the commands and the page run; 0.3.1, the user's word of 2026-11-19: Gallery 风格 and 柔光风格 are looks of a custom page's cards, and a block a document is written with is no look's): their ids and their folders, `components/gallery/<name>/`, stay what they were, so that every document written with them shows and edits as ever and nothing is migrated, and they are installed and follow Gallery with Gallery 风格's install as before; only where they are listed and which switch takes them out of the menus changed. The catalog reads each pack's `pack.json` for its name alone (`packNames`: `catalog.packs`, by scope, `local` among them where a block counts in it, in its stamp), which `menuOf` gives each entry (`pack: { id, name }`) and `packageStates` each row (`pack`, packOf's).

**Installing.** `gallery block pack install <folder|zip>` and 设置 › 组件列表's 导入 read the pack alike (`src/transfer/pack-source.mjs`: a folder's files, links passed over; a zip's, its sizes read from its directory before any file is inflated, at most 2000 files and 64 MB, and no file inflated past the size the directory declares: `src/transfer/zip.mjs` stops a zip bomb's stream there, in memory and to a file, refused `size`) and hand its files to `installPack`, which reads them as a pack (`readPack`: `pack.json` at the top, or in the one folder at a zip's top; what a system wrote, `.DS_Store`, `__MACOSX/`, passed over) and refuses, with why and a `code`, a name that points out of it (`path`: absolute, a drive, `..`, `.`), one too large (`size`), a `pack.json` missing or that does not read (`manifest`), a block folder whose `component.json` does not name it `<id>/<name>` (`block`), the id of one of Gallery's own packs (`official`, checked before its blocks), and an id a folder of `components/` holds that no install put there (`taken`: another pack, or blocks someone wrote). It copies each block as `gallery block add` copies one of Gallery's own (its manifest last) and records in `.gallery/blocks.json` each block's version and fingerprint (`fingerprintOfFiles` of what it writes, which is `fingerprintOf` of the folder after) and under `packs` the pack's version, the fingerprint of its own files (those at its top: `pack.json` and beside it) and its blocks. Installed again, the same rule as Gallery's own blocks: a block as it was installed is replaced (the files the new one has not taken out), one changed since is kept (someone's work, said), one the new version has not is taken out where it is as installed, and `pack.json` the same way (`ownKept`). `removePack` takes out a pack an install put there, its folder and records, never one of Gallery's (its switch turns it off) nor a scope no install put there (`own`). `workspacePacks` lists the workspace's packs for 设置 › 组件列表 and `gallery block pack list`: the default pack first, always (kind `default`, its switch as any pack's, with no block yet too: `gallery block pack list` then says no other pack is installed), then Gallery's own (what Gallery's `pack.json` says of one whose folder has none, its blocks added one by one before the packs), then those installed, then the workspace's own, each with its kind, its switch, how many blocks, what changed since it was installed and, of Gallery's own, Gallery's newer version that is not put over a change.

**Gallery's own packs** are `src/packs/<id>/` (the release's `packs/`, `packsDirectory`): `gallery` (Gallery 风格, the blocks that were `src/blocks/`: its page cards, and in its folder Gallery's own blocks for documents, which its install puts in and which count in the default pack) and `gallery-soft` (柔光风格, the same page cards in another look, off at first: `OFF_AT_FIRST`). A workspace's server installs them as it starts (`installOfficialPacks`, in `adoptWorkspace` and `startProductionServer` before the home page is made, and `gallery init`), through `installPack` with `official`: one not installed yet is installed (柔光风格 switched off first in gallery.json where it says nothing of it; a gallery.json that cannot be written leaves that pack for the next start), one installed is brought up to Gallery's by the same rule, a block Gallery's pack has come to hold added and one taken away by hand left away (its record says it was there). `GALLERY_NO_PACKS` (set, not "", "0" or "false") installs none: the tests' workspaces (`tests/unit/workspace-env.mjs`, the browser harness), whose scenario of the packs starts the studio again without it. `shippedBlocks` reads every pack's blocks (`{ pack }` each), `shippedBlock` takes a bare name as the pack `gallery`'s and `<pack>/<name>` for another, `isShippedId` says a scope is one of Gallery's packs (the page service's add, `gallery block new`'s refusal, `gallery page add`, the catalog's hook that follows Gallery, `beforeCatalogRead(run, scopes)`).

**The switch.** `components.packs` of gallery.json, `{ "<id>": { "enabled": false } }` (a pack none names is on, bar one of Gallery's off at first that no install has put in yet, `offUntilInstalled`: it is as its first install will leave it, so a start that installs none, `GALLERY_NO_PACKS` or a workspace made by hand, has the library offer Gallery 风格's blocks alone, in plain names; on again writes `true`, the entry kept), read as `settings.packs` (by id) and `settings.packsOff`: a pack off is the master switch off for each block that counts in it (`packOf`: Gallery 风格 off leaves its folder's blocks for documents in the menus, the default pack off takes them out), the same rule `inDocumentMenus` runs on the server and the page (none of its blocks in "/", 在下方新建, the handle's grid, the page service's library), what is written of them shown and run as ever, the Agent's commands none of its business. It is the sixth list of the settings line, and in the catalog's stamp. Where two blocks offered have one name (a block of both of Gallery's packs), the "/" row (`menuVariants`, `components/installed/page.mjs`) writes the pack's name after it in grey (`of`: `page/menu.mjs`'s `.ge-pop-of`), 我的待办 · 柔光风格, or its id where both count in one pack (Gallery's 卡片 and a `local/card` of the workspace's, both the default pack's). The library (`library()`) needs none: it gives each block its `pack` ({ id, name }), the packs in 组件列表's order (the default pack, Gallery's own in their order, then the rest by id), each's blocks by name, and 添加自定义块 draws a section of each pack, its name over its blocks (the user's word of 2026-11-09, in place of the grey name: `page/board/index.mjs`); what is typed keeps the sections it finds blocks in.

**The switches from the command line** (`src/cli/component-switches.mjs`): `gallery block pack on|off <pack>`, `gallery block menu on|off <block>` and `gallery block net on|off <block>` are the dialog's switches for an Agent the user asks, each the workbench service's `components` (`POST /__gallery/workspace/components`, `createComponentSettings(...).write`): through the workspace's running server when there is one (`throughServer` of `tree.mjs`; the open pages follow at once), else `write` here with `taken` forgetting this process's kept catalog so that the answer is what is in use now; its refusals (gallery.json that does not read, is another program's, changed meanwhile, cannot be written) are said in English and exit 1, and a switch that is as asked already says so and exits 0. `pack on|off` installs Gallery's own packs first (`installOfficialPacks`, as `gallery init` and a start do; none under `GALLERY_NO_PACKS`) when the pack named is one of theirs the workspace has not got, then writes `components.packs`. `menu` writes what the row of 设置 › 组件列表 writes: `hidden` for a block whose places hold `document`, `shown` for one of pages alone, and `hidden` for a built-in block of `LISTED_BLOCKS`, named by its id or its name in either language (a bare name is a built-in block's unless the workspace has `local/<name>`; one with `hideable: false` is refused). `net on` prints the websites the block's `needs` name (`netHostsOf`) before anything is written, then the consent for all of them (`components.net`; a block whose needs name none is refused), `net off` takes it back; the usage says to turn it on only when the user asked. `gallery block list --json` and `pack list --json` print one object each (`{ workspace, blocks, available, builtin }` and `{ workspace, packs }`, the fields in the usage, only ever added), the second the very `workspacePacks` the dialog's cards are.

## A custom block on a page

`src/render/package-files.mjs` is what a page and an export hold of the custom blocks a document has instances of (`packagesOf(html, catalog, tree)`, read from its elements as the checks read them, so that a code sample or prose about a custom block loads none, and what an instance holds is that instance's; a page whose text spells no instance is not parsed for it, nor is the catalog read for it: a command's first read of the catalog hashes every block's files, some 300 ms, so `packagesOf`, `withPackageCopies`, `withShownTags`, the table views' check and `exportReports` read it only when a page has an instance): only those, in the order it names them, and a custom block that does not load, or whose `apiVersion` this Gallery does not run, gets nothing, its instances showing as those of no custom block. `packageTags(found)` (`src/shared/component-catalog.mjs`) is the one maker of the tags, by the addresses of `packageAddress`, each marked `data-doc-package="<id>"`: a stylesheet link to `style.css`, a script holding the line that tells the kit of it, and a script of `kit.js`. The line says `script` when a script comes with it, and a custom block with no script gets the line all the same, which is what makes its instances a custom block's and no fallback. `renderReportHtml` puts them in on every page it makes, a preview and a build's, the editor's page in a workbench's frame and a page presented alike, after the kit's (`withPackageTags`, `afterKitTags`: the stylesheet after the last of the kit's and before a document's own, so that a document's rules win; the scripts after the last of the kit's and before a document's own script, so that the kit's names are there as they run). A file of the kit's a manifest names in `assets` goes as any the page needs: `usedAssets` takes a custom block as a component, and a tag an older source writes for it is not pruned. A file of the custom block's own that `assets` names is for its style to name, `url(tick.svg)`, relative to the route; that is how one reaches an export, and a script has no address for it there. What an instance draws is in the page and breaks its lines as the page does (its words whole, inherited); the kit holds no long run in an instance's own region (`runtime/spacing.js`), so a run there breaks only where it is wider than its line, and a component that wants it parted at a line's end says `word-break` itself.

A custom block's script is one classic script, read when the page is: `DocKit` and the kit's other globals are there. It runs in a function of its own (`packageScript`, `src/shared/export-wrap.mjs`, the route's copy and an export's alike, on the file's lines, `this` the global object), so that what it declares at its top is its own: two custom blocks may both have a `const WIDTH` or a `function label`, and a name that is to be global is written `window.name`. No module, no `import`: it runs inlined, in an export, as it does here.

**The kit's interface.** A component registers with `DocKit.component({ id, on, owns, mount(host, context), dehydrate(host) })` (`skills/gallery/sdk/doc-kit.d.ts`), its `id` the custom block's, the id of a built-in having no slash: that is all the kernel tells them by. `on` is a list, and anything else is refused there, in that custom block's script. It owns what it draws unless it says `owns: false`, so that the kit watches none of it but what `source` is, and `content` is heard only with `owns: false`. An instance is kept whatever its mount returns; a mount that is a promise has its handle when it resolves, and what it rejects with is its failure. `frame(draw)` runs the last `draw` asked before the frame, and what asks for the next frame from inside each of thirty frames in a row is an animation, which `data-doc-drawn` does not wait for. Its `context` is a built-in's (`host`, `signal`, `frame`, `fail`) and:

| Of `context` | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `part(name)` | the first element of a part of the manifest in this instance's own region, none an instance in it holds and none a slot holds, or null; a part edited as `flow` gives its slot, the element, to read and never to write in or move (a slot taken off the instance on a page being edited is put back); a name the manifest does not have throws |
| `state({ version, defaults, transientKeys })` | this instance's reading state, by `DocState.getOrCreate`, its id the component's and its name (`stateName`): on a custom page its card's `data-card-id` (the closest `.gallery-card`), in a document its `data-block-id`, and in a file none was given yet a hash of its markup (`markupHash`: a copy dehydrated and its `runtime` taken off, the parts edited as text, data or flow emptied, the tags and attributes of the rest but `data-block-id`, `data-ge-*` and the editor's and the kit's marks) and its place among the custom block's instances of that hash, so that an edit of its words keeps it and another instance of other markup moved past it changes nothing; given a block id, it takes the state that hash's name held (`formerId` of `DocState`, read when nothing is kept under the new name and carried to it at once). Two instances share nothing and a second mount gets the same object |
| `canEdit()` | whether `edit` can write to the file: false until whoever edits the page says so (`doc-editable`), and for good where nothing does, an export, 演示视图 and a page no editor runs on, none of which has an editor to say it; false too for an instance that failed or left the page, and for one past its manifest (`data-doc-schema` higher than its `schema`, 1 where it says none, or no number). An editing page printed is still an editing page: it is true there |
| `edit(part, change)` | a function from the first moment, which does nothing and returns false while `canEdit()` is false, so that a script need not ask whether it is there. Otherwise a question put at once, on the first element of the part, as `part` finds it: a cancelable `doc-component-edit` on the instance, `{ parts: [{ name, part }], run }`, which whoever edits the page cancels to say it took the edit, handing `run` a draft of each part by its name; the kit writes no file and knows of no editor. `part` may be a list of names, the parts one write changes together, `change` then handed the drafts by name: one write, one undo step. `run` runs `change`, and one that throws, or returns a promise, is told on the console, as a failure of the edit alone: nothing has happened and the instance goes on. So it answers synchronously, and with no taker false; with a part the instance does not hold, false too. A name the manifest does not have throws, as for `part`, and so does a part not edited as data, and two that are one element or one in the other |
| `readJSON(part)` | what the part holds parsed as JSON (a script of data's), JSON's `null` as any other value, or `undefined` where there is nothing to read, which no JSON is: an optional part the instance does not hold, and one that holds no JSON, which fails the instance as `fail` does, its data being what it draws from (so that a block that stops on `undefined` still fails, by its own check, on data that is `null`) |
| `editJSON(part, change)` | `edit` of a part of JSON: `change` is handed what it holds, parsed, and changes it or returns what is to stand there instead, and the draft is written as it was laid out, by the one copy of that layout (`jsonIn` of `src/shared/json-layout.mjs`, which `gallery block check` reads by too: on one line where it was on one, else a line an entry but what holds values alone, from the indentation it starts at, `</` and `<!--` escaped); a value as it was writes nothing, a promise or what holds no JSON is a failure of the edit, and it answers as `edit` does |
| `rows(part)` | the table a part is or holds read as rows (`runtime/rows.js`, `rowSet`), or null for none or one with a cell spanning others: `columns`, the header's words (its head's first row, else the body's first, as a chart reads a table), and `rows`, each `{ el, values, links }`, its element, its values by the header's words as a chart drawn from a table reads them (`cellValue` and `cellText` of `src/shared/table-chart.mjs`: a number, its words or null), and the links its cells hold to documents (`pageAddress`: `{ slug, title, block }`, a link within the document by its own slug). `add(values, { after })`, `move(row, { before \| after, values })` and `remove(row)` are each one `edit` of the part, made on its draft at the row's place in the table (`table.rows`), the white space before a row going with it and a row added given the blank of the one it follows, so that a table written a row a line stays so; `move` sets the cells `values` names in the same write; a name the header does not have throws, and a row of a set read before a write is none (false) |
| `table()` | the table of the document this instance is a view of, for a block whose manifest's `needs` hold `tables` (the kit is told them, `descriptionOf`): the one whose frame carries the key the instance's `data-doc-from` gives (`VIEW_FROM`, as a chart's host names its table, "Charts from a table"), read when it is called by the kit's one reading of a table (`runtime/table-reading.js`, in the file's order: a reader's sort leaves it), or null with no key, no such table, a cell spanning others, on a custom page (which holds no table) and for a block whose needs hold none (said on the console once). `columns` and `rows` as `rows(part)` gives them, a row the same object until it or the header changes (the reading kept by key, each cell and row read again only once a change touches it), so that a view keeps what it drew of a row while it is the same; `set(row, column, text)`, `add(values, { after })`, `move(row, { before | after, values })` and `remove(row)` write nothing here: each is a question put at once, a cancelable `doc-table-intent` on the instance, `{ table, op, row, before, after, cells: [[column, text]] }` (the rows' elements, a column by its place in the header, of two alike the later), which the page editor cancels to say it made the change ([editor.md](editor.md), "Table views": one undo step, a cell's words merged word by word with someone else's, a row's move, add or removal the table's operation, held back as its conflict where the table changed meanwhile), false with no taker, in an export and 演示视图, before the page edits, for a row no longer in the table and for a table the editor's tools do not manage; a name the header does not have throws. `canEdit()` asks the same with no change (`op` "check"), true where the editor would take an intent of this table now, so that a view shows one it cannot change read-only (a `<tfoot>`, rows of other lengths) rather than finding out by a gesture that does nothing. `subscribe(listener)` calls `listener` (as the instance's own: a throw fails it) once a batch of the page's changes leaves the rows other than they were, until the function it returns or the instance's end takes it off; a component whose `on` holds `data` hears that as `data` with the detail `"table"`, whether or not it has a table yet (a key put on later, a table pasted back). Its own sort and filter a view keeps in `state`, never in the table |
| `interval(run, ms)`, `timeout(run, ms)`, `listen(target, type, listener, options)` | the browser's, cleared and removed as the instance ends (its signal), `listen` also as the signal of its own options aborts. What a timer runs is the instance's as a frame's drawing is: nothing once it ended or failed, a throw failing it, a write in a slot put back |
| `place` | where the instance is (`placeOf`): `"page"` in a custom page's markup (`main.gallery-page`, which the page's host serves), `"export"` in an exported file (its generator or its export id, as the preview's notes go by), else `"document"` |
| `workspace` | there where the custom block's manifest's `needs` hold `"workspace"` (the catalog tells the kit of them: `needs` in the line that describes a package, `descriptionOf`), no consent asked, as it reads the user's own workspace on this computer; not there otherwise (`runtime/workspace.js`). `query(kind, options)` asks the server the page came from, `GET /__gallery/workspace/query?kind=<kind>&options=<JSON>` ([workbench.md](workbench.md) "The workspace's facts"), at the address the page says (`globalThis.__galleryWorkspace`, `WORKSPACE_GLOBAL`: a script of the server's, `data-doc-dev`, that `src/server/routes.mjs` puts in a document's page, presented too, and a custom page's, and no export or copy of the view keeps, so that the kit's own files name no address of the server's and a page with none is offline), and resolves to `{ data, at }`: `todos` (`{ done, slugs, limit }`: `[{ ref, text, done, slug, title, icon, path }]`), `recent` (`{ limit }`: `[{ slug, title, icon, edited }]`), `stats` (`{ documents, todos: { done, total }, words, editedThisWeek }`) `activity` (`{ days }`: `[{ date, edits, words, reader, agent }]`, a day of none 0, from the day the record started at the earliest) and `table` (`{ slug, key, where, limit }`: `{ columns, rows: [{ values, links }], total }`, a keyed table of another document read-only, the row shape of `context.table()`'s; asked from a document with `&doc=<slug>`, which its export then embeds) and `document` (`{ slug }`, which the kit fills with the instance's own document's, `.doc-page`'s `data-doc-id`, where the block names none and the page is no custom page: `{ slug, title, icon, created, edited, editedBy, words }`, one document's own facts, `editedBy` `reader`, `agent` or null), the kinds, their bounds and the route one list of `src/shared/workspace-query.mjs`. What it cannot answer rejects with an Error whose `code` says why, one of `QUERY_CODES` and never another: `kind`, `options`, `missing` (`table`: no such document, no table of that key, or one with merged cells; `document`: no such document), `unsupported` (which no server of this version says), the server's own refusals of the query passed on as it says them; `offline` (an exported file whose snapshot holds none of that kind, or a server that does not answer); `failed` (the server answered and could not: a read that failed, its 500 `read`; a request it refused, its 403 `host`; an answer that is no JSON; any other reason it gives), which Gallery's own blocks say as 读不到工作区的… and never as an export's line. Each kind an instance asked for is one it hears `data` of from then on (`kinds`, a set of the instance's). `open(target)` (`{ slug, block? }` a document, at a block of it, or `{ page }` a custom page; anything else throws) asks whoever shows the page by a cancelable `doc-open-request` on the window, which the page's session answers (`src/editor/page/custom-page.mjs`: the workbench's `open`; the page editor's `goTo`, as a link to it goes), else the page goes there itself (`/generated/<slug>.html#<block>`, `/pages/<page>`); in an export it does nothing. In an export (`place === "export"`) `query` asks nothing: it reads the snapshot the export embedded (`script[data-doc-snapshot]`, `render/net-snapshot.mjs`: each kind's whole answer to its most, and the tables the document's instances asked for, each row with its cells' words), its options applied as the server applies them, `at` the snapshot's `data-at` (the export's time, which a block says as 截至 …); a copy of the view the browser makes holds none (offline: the block's placeholder). `setTodo(ref, done)` checks or clears a to-do of a document by the ref `query("todos")` gave: a cancelable `doc-todo-set` on the window (`{ ask: { slug, ref, done }, answer(promise) }`), which whoever holds the page's token answers (a custom page's board, a document's editor: `src/editor/page/todo-writes.mjs`, `POST /__gallery/workspace/todo`), resolving to whether it changed the document; refusals reject with a `code` of `TODO_CODES` (`ref` before anything is asked; `changed`, `ambiguous`, `locked` (the item is where the document's own page cannot check it), `missing`, `busy`, `failed` as the server says; `offline` where nothing answers: the 演示视图, a page on its own with no token); not there in an export, so that a block draws its boxes read-only |
| `fetch(url, { as })` | only for a block whose needs name a host of the net (its description carries its `needs`): the web, through the server ("A block online" below): `{ data, at, cached }`, or a rejection whose `code` says why; in an exported file the answer the export embedded, or `offline` |
| `size` | on a custom page, read when asked, its card's `{ w, h, width, height }`: the cells the board draws it at, never the raw attributes (`src/shared/card-size.mjs` `drawnCells`, the rule the board's script lays a card out by: the card the closest `.gallery-card` of any tag, its `data-w` and `data-h` parsed as whole numbers and held to `1 ≤ w ≤ 12` and `1 ≤ h ≤ 24`, one that is no number the default 4 × 2, `w` no more than its board's `data-columns`, fewer than 12 on a narrow board, 12 where it says none) and the pixels its card holds inside its padding; null anywhere else. The canvas sends `doc-layout-change` when it changes, which a component hears as `layout` |

Whoever said the page takes edits is told of no instance: `editable` goes to each that asked (`on`) as `doc-editable` comes, and to one whose mount was a promise as that resolves, so that no mount succeeds and then never hears it can write. One mounted after it finds `canEdit()` true. `example(host, context)` of a component's registration is what `gallery block check` calls (`DocKit.example(host)`), and nothing else. `skills/gallery/sdk/doc-kit.d.ts` declares the types a custom block's script names as global ones too (`DocComponentContext`, `DocComponentHandle`, `DocComponentSignal`, `DocComponent`), as `doc-charts.d.ts` declares `DocTableRows`: a script in a custom block's folder reaches no file of the SDK by a path, so `/** @param {DocComponentContext} context */` is how it names its context, with no import, which the check's type check reads against the SDK.

**A slot is the document's.** What a custom block's component runs as the kit calls it, its mount (not what `clear` runs before it, its `dehydrate`, which may take off what an earlier write left in a slot and so put the slot as the file has it), an `update`, a `context.frame` drawing and what `interval` and `timeout` run, is watched in its instance when it has slots (`guarded`: a MutationObserver of its own on the instance for that call, with what each change took): a change in a slot, a slot's own attributes among them, or a slot taken off or moved, puts back every change of that call, the last first (`putBack`), and the instance fails as for a throw there, the console saying which slot and as it did what ("it wrote in its slot "body" as it heard source"). The editor never takes the change for the reader's: what it hears of it is undone in the same task. What a component writes later, from a listener or a promise, no call of the kit's holds: the page editor puts back a slot taken off (`keepSlots`, [editor.md](editor.md)), and `gallery block check` looks for the rest as the component mounts, mounts again and makes its example.

**Three gestures are the kit's** (`runtime/gestures.js`), each written once rather than by every block: `DocKit.input(field, { commit, signal })` hands `commit(value)` what a reader typed in an `input` or a `textarea`, or chose in a `select`, when they are done with it, Enter (Ctrl or ⌘ with it in a textarea), the field left or a select changed, never while an input method composes (`compositionstart` to `compositionend`, `isComposing`, and keyCode 229, Safari's Enter right after a composition ends: a Chinese IME's Enter only takes its letters) and never a value committed or set last; a field left as it composes commits as the composition ends, Escape puts the value back and leaves it, and the handle's `set(value)` shows what the file now holds unless the reader is typing another there. `DocKit.text(node, { commit, enabled, placeholder, signal })` makes words a block draws from the file (a cell of its table, a setting: a board's card title, a chart's title) typed in where they stand, as a figure's or a layout's words are in the page editor (the user, 2026-11-20: "像图形块或整体块一样支持出现蓝色的背景填充然后直接修改"): while `enabled()` says so (asked as they are set, as the pointer comes onto them and as they are pressed; `context.canEdit()`, and false where what they stand for takes no change) the element is `contenteditable="plaintext-only"` and marked `data-doc-text`, which the kit's `installed.css` gives the page editor's look of a framed text block (`editor.mjs` `[data-ge-framed]`): 3px corners, the accent's light fill inside it (`color-mix(in srgb, var(--doc-accent) 9%, transparent)`, an inset shadow) under the pointer and while typed in, the text cursor, no ring, selectable in what is not (a card that drags), and `data-doc-text-empty` gray words while it is empty (`:empty::before`, never written). One line: Enter and Escape end the typing as leaving it does, both keeping what was typed (the editor's Escape never takes words back; undo does), `insertParagraph` and `insertLineBreak` refused, a paste's line breaks spaces; nothing is written while it is typed; done, never while an input method composes (as `DocKit.input`: a field left as it composes commits as the composition ends), `commit(text)` hears its words, white space one space, trimmed, once, unless they are what it holds; it answers whether the change was taken, `false` putting back the words it holds. Its element is no text block of the editor's (no `data-ge-*`, and `plaintext-only` is no `contenteditable="true"` the editor's rules go by): what happens in it is the component's (`keepsKeys`), its Ctrl+Z its own while typed in (`FIELDS` holds `[contenteditable]`). Elsewhere (an export, 演示视图, before the page edits) it is words as drawn. The handle's `set(text)` shows what the file holds unless the reader is typing others there, `edit()` puts the caret in it (a card just added), `refresh()` asks `enabled()` again. Left, it commits once what took the focus away has run (a microtask), and not at all where it has left the page (a component drawing anew: half of what was typed would be written) or has the focus again (put back where it was by a component that drew it anew). Ctrl+Z there is the browser's, within it, only while what was typed since it took the caret is still to take back (`TEXT_TYPED` of `shared/components/installed.mjs` on the element, which the editor's `pageUndo` reads); otherwise the page's undo, as on any of the component's drawing, and the browser's `historyUndo` and `historyRedo` there are refused: its one stack for the page would take back another field's words (the review of 2026-11-20). `DocKit.drag(area, { items, handle, threshold, enabled, marker, over, drop, signal })` is the board's drag by pointer events: a press of the main button on what `items` finds, while `enabled()`, is a drag once it moves `threshold` pixels (4), `over(x, y, item)` says where a drop there goes, `{ into, before, … }`, the `marker` standing there, `drop(item, spot)` is called on a release over one, and Escape (on the window, in its capture, ahead of the page's keys), a release elsewhere, `pointercancel` or a capture lost drop nothing; the item carries the class `doc-dragging` while it moves, and the click a drag's release makes is swallowed. None writes anything: each calls what the component gives it, which writes by `context.edit` (or asks its table, `context.table()`), once a gesture.

**A choice is the kit's menu, never the browser's popup** (`runtime/choice.js`, `styles/choice.css`; the user, 2026-11-09: a native `<select>`'s dropdown is a square white box that follows no theme, and Gallery never uses it). Every `<select>` of a page opens it, whoever drew it: the document's own (bound by `data-doc-bind` or not), a custom block's, Gallery's own views' ⚙ settings, the kit's. `startChoices` listens once on the window, in the capture phase and so ahead of the page's own listeners and the editor's, and adds nothing to the page: the `<select>` stays where it is as the trigger and keeps its value, its `input` and `change` (so `data-doc-bind`, a form, `gallery block try --select` and a block's script reading `select.value` work as they did, and the file, the editor's commit and an export of the view see no new node or attribute). A press of the main button on it (`mousedown`, where the browser opens its popup: its default is prevented, the focus taken by hand) or Space, Enter, an arrow or F4 on it opens the card; a list box (`multiple`, `size` over 1), a disabled select (or in a disabled fieldset) and any other key are the browser's. The card (`.doc-choice`) is the display menu's look (controls.css), the product's menu card since 2026-11-17: 14px round with 5px inside it (`--doc-radius-card`, `--doc-card-pad`), the floating surface and the card's shadow (`--doc-shadow-card`), rows 28px high, 9px round and touching, that take the row fill (`--doc-hover-row`, 7%) under the pointer or the keys at once (going over 100ms) and `--doc-press` pressed, the chosen option checked at its right (no row holds a place for a check; nothing is outlined), a disabled option dimmed and never chosen, an `<optgroup>` a small heading, a hidden option no row; as wide as its rows, at least as wide as the trigger and at most 320px (past that a row's words are cut by an ellipsis), under the trigger with their left edges together, or above it where that side has more room, kept 8px from the window's edges, its rows scrolling where neither side holds them. It grows out of the corner by its trigger (`transform-origin`: the top left under it, the bottom left above it, the right ones in a right-to-left page) from 96% with its opacity over 140ms (`--doc-ease-out`), and leaves by its opacity alone over 90ms (`is-leaving`, then taken away; one opened meanwhile takes it away at once); opened from the keys it is there at once (`is-keyed`), and where motion is reduced it only fades. The display menu does the same from its button's corner (the top right), hidden by its `hidden` with its `display` kept to the end of the fade, at once opened from the keys (`doc-display-menu--keys`). It is a popover (`popover="manual"`, the top layer: no z-index of the page or of the editor's overlays covers it; `z-index: 1100` stands for a browser without popovers) at the end of `<body>`, or, where the trigger is in an open detail card (`dialog[open]`), inside it, since the page behind a modal card is inert. One is open at a time. Its keys are its own while it is open (a capture listener on the window, `stopImmediatePropagation`: the editor's Esc, which selects the block, never sees them): ↑ ↓ (not past a disabled row), Home, End, PageUp, PageDown move the highlight; Enter or Space chooses it (Space is a letter while one was typed under 700 ms ago); a letter lights the first row that starts with it, letters together the first that starts with them, one letter again the next with it; Alt+↑ chooses; Esc closes (the detail card stays); Tab leaves; an input method's keys (`isComposing`, keyCode 229) are none of its. The focus stays on the trigger throughout, so it is there when the card closes. A press outside, a scroll of the page, a resize, the window losing the focus, the trigger leaving the page or its options changing (a `MutationObserver` on the document, only while the card is open) closes it without a choice; a press on the trigger again closes it (a toggle). A choice sets `selectedIndex` (duplicate values are told apart) and sends `input` then `change`, bubbling, once, and none when it is the option already chosen. The card is a generated node (the `state` description's `runtime`: `.doc-choice`, its parts `doc-choice-*`): an export of the view leaves it out, and it never prints; on paper a select is its chosen option's words (`appearance: none`, no arrow). A select nobody styled has the kit's quiet look (`:where(select:not([multiple], [size]))`, weightless: a class or a block's own rule wins): 28px, no line, `--doc-hover` fill, `--doc-press` under the pointer, the browser's own arrow, which stays whatever background a page sets. `DocKit.choose(anchor, { options, value, label, signal })` opens the same card under any element, a block's own button, and resolves with the value chosen or `null` when it is dismissed (`options`: `[value, words]`, `{ value, label?, disabled? }`, a bare string, `{ group }`); called again on the anchor of an open card it closes it. Tested in Chromium (Chrome, Edge) only: a `mousedown` whose default is prevented is the long-used way to stop the popup in Firefox and Safari too, untested here, as is a touch screen's picker (the compatibility `mousedown` a tap makes is taken where the browser sends one; iOS Safari's wheel may still show). Not done: `select.showPicker()`, which a script may still call for the browser's popup (`skills/gallery/references/block-script.md` says not to).

**A tooltip is the kit's** (`runtime/tip.js`, `styles/controls.css` `.doc-tip`, tokens `--doc-tip-bg`, `--doc-tip-text`, `--doc-tip-sub`): an element of a document or a card with `data-doc-tip` shows its words in Gallery's dark bubble, never the browser's square `title` tooltip, in the workbench and in an exported file; `data-doc-tip-sub` is a second, dimmer line, and `data-doc-tip-cut` (no value) shows the element's own text, only while it is cut short (`scrollWidth` past `clientWidth` by more than a pixel, or `scrollHeight` past `clientHeight`: a line clamp). The look is the page editor's `.ge-tip` to the value (12px on 1.4, 5px 8px, radius 6, 300px at most, #2c2c2b with #f0efed and #ada9a3 in both themes, a 50ms fade in, gone at once), and so is the behaviour: shown 150ms after the pointer (a mouse or a pen) rests on it, 6px under it, over it where the window has no room below, kept 12px inside the window's sides; a focus from the keys shows nothing, nor does anything while a button is held (a drag across the page), nor what comes under the pointer after a press until it moves (a bar drawn anew as its menu opens under a resting pointer), as the browser's own tooltips; the pointer leaving or pressing, any scroll (captured: a scroller of the page's own too), the wheel, the window losing the focus or the page being hidden take it away, and so does its element being taken out of the page, hidden (`display: none`, `visibility: hidden`) or moved or resized by more than 2px, seen at every frame while the bubble shows (the browser says no `pointerout` for those; the page editor's bubbles keep the same rule, [editor.md](editor.md) "A bubble stands only while its control is where the pointer found it"). One listener on the document finds the element (`closest`), so an attribute set later, or changed, is read when the pointer comes. The bubble is a popover (the top layer: over a modal detail card, which it is put in, and any z-index), `role="tooltip"`, `data-doc-dev` (an export of the view leaves it out), never printed. The kit's own first user is a consent bar's 允许访问, whose hosts it shows; a block of an Agent's writes the attribute instead of `title` (skills/gallery/references/block-script.md). On a page being edited the editor's own tooltips stand beside it, the same look.

A mount is the same on any node. `mount` starts by taking off what the instance holds from before, `dehydrate(host)` and then what the description's `runtime` names, `generated` and `attrs`, whose selectors the catalog gave the instance's root, and the kit's marks of one shown as it stands. So a node an undo put back with what the last mount drew in it, a copy of one, and a node a view copy dehydrated all mount as the file's own does: mount, dehydrate, mount gives the same DOM. `dehydrate` may be given a host with nothing on it; if it throws there, that is told on the console, `runtime` still comes off and the mount goes on. `DocKit.dehydrate(root)` takes a copy of the page or of one block, the root being a host like any other: that is how a block is copied (`copyOf`, [editor.md](editor.md)).

A component that throws fails its own instance. In `mount`, in `update`, in a frame or by `context.fail`, even as its mount runs, it is told on the console and, to who writes the page, next to the instance (`.doc-kit-error`, in a preview that is no presentation, and never on paper); its signal is aborted, what it drew comes off, what its mount returned is ended, and the instance shows as one of no custom block (`data-doc-fallback`, its controls inert), staying among the kit's instances as failed, so that no scan starts it again and no frame runs for it. Its text parts take no word while it shows so, as one of no custom block's ([editor.md](editor.md), "With no custom block, or one that does not load"), and the other instances and the page go on. A `dehydrate` that throws in a view copy is told, costs the copy nothing and leaves `runtime`'s marks to come off.

A script that runs and starts nothing (no `DocKit.component`, an error before it, a wrong id, a thrown `on`) fails all its instances: `gallery check` finds a script that does not compile, and for the rest the kit looks once the page has loaded, by what the line that came with the script says (`script`), and shows them as one of no custom block, with a note saying so, in the preview, and a line on the console, there and in an export. A late `DocKit.component`, as the page gets ready, takes the marks off again. A custom block with no script stays as it is.

**Rights.** A component runs with the rights a document's own script has: on the page, with its origin, on the same footing as the document's scripts and no other. No part of Gallery runs one on the server: `component.json` is read as data and `kit.js` only compiled. So a custom block is as trusted as the document that uses it, and only one the workspace's owner or their Agent wrote belongs in `components/`.

**Exports.** All three hold a used custom block whole and work under the export's policy; a custom block that is not used is in none. `DocKit.describe`'s line goes as it stands, so that an exported instance is a custom block's, and a file of the kit's named in `assets` is a kit file. Of a custom block's own:

| | `gallery export` (`exportReports`) and the workbench's 导出 (`offlineReports`) | 导出当前视图副本 (`ui.js`, in the browser) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `style.css` | `<style data-doc-package>`, what its addresses name of the custom block's files as `data:` addresses (`withPackageCopies`, by `cssReferences`) | the same, read back from the route and the files it names, by the loop that inlines the kit's and the same reader; what cannot be read stays as written |
| `kit.js` | `<script data-doc-package>` running `scriptText(packageScript(code))` | the same functions, the route's text being `packageScript`'s |
| what comes off | the source has none of it | `[data-doc-dev]`, the editor's marks and `DocKit.dehydrate`, the whole catalog's other descriptions going with `data-doc-dev` |
| state | as stored in the reader's browser | `DocState.serializeAll()` in the file, each instance's under its id |

The text functions are `src/shared/export-wrap.mjs`'s, as the policy is, and the one reader of a stylesheet's addresses is `src/shared/css-references.mjs`, as CSS's tokenizer reads them (a function's name in any case, `image-set()`, nothing a comment or a string spells), so that neither path can allow what the other does not. A custom block's text is anybody's and may hold anything. A `</style>` in a style is written `<\/style>` (`styleText`), which CSS reads as the same words. A script's `</script>`, and a `<!--` and `<script`, after which the HTML parser takes the next end tag for part of the script, cannot be rewritten in place with one meaning in a string, a regular expression, a comment and code alike; so `scriptText` keeps the text in a string, where `<` can be escaped, and the inline script makes a script element of it, which runs at once, where it stands, as the policy's `'unsafe-inline'` allows, and takes itself off again. An import of the file takes out all that is marked `data-doc-package` (`undoExport`): a document holds its instances and nothing of a custom block. The server's exports also say which custom blocks they hold, each by its stamp (`packagesMeta`, a `<meta name="doc-export-packages">` right after the one that says the text it was made from): an export is as old as those, and `gallery qa` takes one whose custom blocks have changed since for one not of the file as it is, as it does a text that has changed (`exportOf`): exported anew, or refused with `--no-export`.

**The checks.** `componentFailures()` is the one check of whether a component is valid, called by `gallery check` (`quickFailures`), `exportReports` (through it) and `offlineReports` (`checkedSource`, which runs no other authoring check), so that all three fail on one broken instance or script in the same words, the workbench's 导出 of a document that fails one of the kit's components too; its dialog says in a reader's words which custom block it is, when that is what failed first. A Markdown export (`render.check`) holds nothing of a component but its words, and is held to neither this check nor what `exportFailures` says of custom blocks. `exportFailures()` adds what only an offline file asks: `packageExportFailures`, of a used custom block, says of `style.css` an `@import`, an address elsewhere, and a file `assets` does not name, and of `kit.js` what `usesIn` finds of `NETWORK`, `eval` or `new Function`, as it reads a document's code, each with its line. It runs in `quickFailures` as all of `exportFailures` does, so a custom block gone wrong is found at `gallery check` and in a preview's panel, not first at an export. What does not load is a note, never a failure: an export shows what its instances hold.

**Making one.** `gallery block` (`src/cli/component.mjs`, its usage in `src/cli/usage.mjs`, for Agents `skills/gallery/references/building-blocks.md`, `block-script.md` and `pages-and-data.md`; `gallery component` is its other name, `ALIASES`, saying the same) is how a custom block comes to be: `new` writes one that passes the check (`component-scaffold.mjs`), never of the scopes of Gallery's own packs, `gallery` and `gallery-soft` ("Block packs"), nor of a pack installed: Gallery's own blocks (`src/packs/gallery/<name>/`, the release's `packs/gallery/`: `gallery/card`, a title and a slot, and `gallery/chart-card`, a title, a slot holding a plain table and the bars its `kit.js` draws from that table above the slot, again at each `source`, in percents and the kit's series colors, named by its `runtime`: no chart card of the kit's, which no menu can make, see below; the documents' three views of a table, "Gallery's own views of a table" below; and the custom pages' six, "A custom page", weather among them, "A block online"); `add <name>` copies one of those in unchanged (`addShipped`, `src/shared/shipped-blocks.mjs`), its version and a fingerprint of its files in `.gallery/blocks.json`, so that adding it again with a newer Gallery updates a copy as it was added and never one whose files changed since (exit 1, saying to use `--recipe`), and a copy as it was added follows Gallery without the command (`followShipped`;
